# Open follow-ups

Every item below is one of: (a) blocked on an external credential / account, (b) an operator deploy step on code that's already merged, or (c) a sized-but-unstarted feature / a deferred-with-reason finding awaiting a product or architecture call.

> **Completed work was pruned from this file on 2026-06-14.** The full pre-prune snapshot — every shipped `[x]` item and closed section with its implementation context — is preserved in [`followups_archive.md`](followups_archive.md). Pair this file with [`docs/testing/testing.md`](../testing/testing.md) "What's *not* covered" for the test view. Operator cutover recipes for the Go-service migrations live in [`apps/job_worker/deployment.md`](../../apps/job_worker/deployment.md) and [`apps/web/deployment.md`](../../apps/web/deployment.md).

## Deploys (code merged, operator steps remain)

- [ ] **share-run / share-route Lambda og:image PNG — verify the arm64 @resvg binary ships in the zip.** Both Lambdas serve their og:image PNG at request time and `build.mjs` copies the `@resvg/resvg-js` loader + `@resvg/resvg-js-linux-arm64-gnu` native package into the zip. The Lambdas run on arm64, so that native package must resolve on the build host. If `build.mjs` fails fast with the missing-binary error, run `npm install --workspace=apps/web --cpu=arm64 --os=linux @resvg/resvg-js-linux-arm64-gnu` before the zip step. Operator: confirm the first prod/preview deploy renders `/og/run/<id>.png` + `/og/route/<id>.png` (200, valid PNG) and that a private/deleted id returns the generic branded card at 200, not a 404. The new `/og/run/*` + `/og/route/*` + `/share/route/*` CloudFront behaviours are a `terraform apply` on `infra/envs/{prod,preview}`. See `apps/web/lambda/share-run/README.md` + `apps/web/lambda/share-route/README.md`.
- [ ] **Live spectator hub → Fly.io — cutover only.** The worker is already deployed (2026-07-21, Fly app `threkir-worker`, region `ord`) and the `live.threkir.com` CNAME has landed. What remains: the `flyctl certs add` TLS check, then set `PUBLIC_LIVE_HUB_URL` (web sops blob) + `LIVE_HUB_URL` (mobile release builds). Client wiring already switches transport on the env flip; no code change. Recipe: `apps/job_worker/deployment.md § Live spectator hub`.
- [ ] **Map matching deploy** — OSRM alongside Supabase, OSM-extract refresh pipeline, auth endpoint, matched-geom return + raw-vs-matched toggle, offline fallback. Engine choice + trigger wiring shipped; the deploy is what remains.
- [ ] **Route builder needs a reachable OSRM — design decision first, not just a deploy.** The shipped internal-track build (`mobile_android@1.0.5`, 2026-07-14) has `OSRM_URL` unset, so the route Build button throws on a phone. **Do NOT "just" expose `osrm.threkir.com`** — the previous wording of this item said that, and it contradicts a documented decision: `osrm-routed` is the upstream `osrm/osrm-backend` image with **no auth of any kind**, which is exactly why `apps/job_worker/osrm/fly.toml` attaches no public IP and `apps/job_worker/deployment.md` § DR states "OSRM has no auth; that's why it can never have a public route" (it declines to expose even `/health` directly). A naked public OSRM is an open, abusable `performance-2x`/8 GB compute service.

  The real blocker is **cross-cloud reachability + no auth**, and it has three parts:
  1. **OSRM is on Fly 6PN; its consumer is an AWS Lambda.** The `osrm-proxy` Lambda (`apps/web/lambda/osrm-proxy/`, issue #198 / decisions §242) is built, Terraformed (`infra/envs/{prod,preview}`) and bundled by `release-web.yml` — but it cannot reach `osrm.internal`. `var.osrm_url` defaults to `""`, and an empty value leaves the proxy at 501 with the **web** route builder degrading to straight-line segments. Confirm whether prod actually has it set before assuming web routing works.
  2. **Auth has to be added in front of OSRM, not to it.** The house precedent is `graph_cycle`: `[http_service]` + `force_https` + a required `GRAPH_CYCLE_API_KEY` header — but that works because `graph_cycle` is *our* Go code (`apps/graph_cycle/main.go`). OSRM is a third-party binary, so the equivalent needs a small auth reverse-proxy in front of it on Fly (or moving OSRM into AWS beside the Lambda, removing the cross-cloud hop entirely).
  3. **Mobile must go through the Lambda proxy, not straight at OSRM.** `routing.dart` currently builds `$OSRM_URL/nearest/v1/...` and `$OSRM_URL/route/v1/...` and calls them directly from the device — the precise exposure web *removed* in §242, where a user's pin coordinates (routinely their home) left the client with no server boundary. Pointing `MOBILE_OSRM_URL` at a public OSRM would re-open it on mobile. The proxy deliberately mirrors OSRM's own GET path shape, so mobile's URL building needs **no change** — set `MOBILE_OSRM_URL=https://threkir.com/api/routes/osrm` and send the Supabase JWT in the `x-supabase-authorization` header on the two fetch helpers — the standard `Authorization` header is owned by CloudFront's OAC sigv4 signing and never reaches the Lambda (`apps/web/lambda/osrm-proxy/src/index.ts`); the proxy 401s without a signed-in user.

  Until (1) and (2) are decided and provisioned, mobile route-building cannot ship past Internal, and the mobile code change in (3) is not worth landing (it would only move the failure). Operator-gated: needs `flyctl` + Fly/AWS credentials.
- [ ] **Graph-cycle loop generator (v3) — prod deploy only.** The Go graph sidecar (`apps/graph_cycle/`) + its integration as the first link in the generator chain (`apps/web/src/lib/routes/generate/graph_cycle.ts`) are shipped and gated on `GRAPH_CYCLE_URL`/`GRAPH_CYCLE_API_KEY` (falls back to `round_trip` then polygon). Operator: `flyctl deploy` the service per its `fly.toml`/`deployment.md`, then set `GRAPH_CYCLE_URL` + `GRAPH_CYCLE_API_KEY` in the web env. No further code required. Design in [graph_cycle_loop_generation.md](../features/graph_cycle_loop_generation.md).
- [ ] **Protomaps self-hosted tiles** — all 4 items from `roadmap.md` "Future — Protomaps self-hosted tiles".
- [ ] **Live-hub Redis — provisioning, and the Bridge that provisioning would switch off.** Surfaced 2026-08-26 by the partial-box reconciliation ([decisions § 756](../architecture/decisions.md)); it was the one `- [~]` in the tree tracked in no other place. `apps/job_worker/internal/livehub/redis_hub.go` implements the multi-replica backend and `main.go:556` selects it on `REDIS_URL`, so the deployed hub runs `backend=in-process (single-replica)` today. Two things are owed, and only the first is an operator step. **(a) Provisioning:** stand up Upstash Redis, set `REDIS_URL`, then lift `--ha=false` (`apps/job_worker/deployment.md`) — the in-process hub shares no state, so a second machine silently breaks fan-out. Live pings are GPS coordinates, so hosted Upstash is a cross-border processor transfer whose DPA is a pre-cutover item in [`sub-processors.md`](../compliance/sub-processors.md). **(b) Unwritten code:** `main.go:671` wires the Realtime→hub Bridge inside `if inProc, ok := hub.(*livehub.Hub); ok`, so setting `REDIS_URL` **silently drops the Bridge** — with no log line — and a hub spectator stops seeing runners still on the legacy transport, which is precisely the population that exists during the cutover above. `bridge.go:37-40` names the cause: the cursor and the hub-native guard are in-memory and moving them into Redis is "not built". Do (b) before (a), or the two cutovers interfere.
- [ ] **Engagement email send gate — provision worker SMTP.** The weekly digest and the lifecycle drip are code-complete including their `pg_cron` schedulers, and both drain to `done` without `SMTP_HOST` (see the `[x]` entry under **Email / lifecycle** below for what shipped). Filed here so the open half is reachable from the operator's own list rather than only from the roadmap: the full recipe is `roadmap.md` § **Email — production ops** — provision SMTP (Resend/SES) + `APP_BASE_URL` on the worker, confirm the pg_cron schedules in prod Supabase, set SPF/DKIM/DMARC. Sending also needs the CISO/counsel sign-off recorded in [`email.md`](../features/email.md), which gates the deploy and not the code.
- [ ] **Auth email — localized templates (Send Email Hook).** The signup/reset redirect fix + custom-SMTP sender are done (project custom SMTP → Resend fixes the `noreply@threkir.com` From). The `auth-email` function's six-locale templates are **not** live: prod uses GoTrue's built-in **English-only** templates until the send-email hook is enabled. Operator: deploy `auth-email` to prod, `supabase secrets set` its `SEND_EMAIL_HOOK_SECRET` + Resend `SMTP_*`, then enable the hook (Dashboard → Auth → Hooks → Send Email) — in that order (an enabled hook pointed at an absent function 404s all auth mail). Also set the hosted **Site URL** = `https://threkir.com` + add `/auth/callback`, `/auth/reset`, `com.threkir.app://login-callback` to the Redirect-URLs allow-list. Recipe: [email.md § Production ops 2d](../features/email.md).

## Blocked on external credentials / accounts

- [ ] **Push notifications (FCM + APNs + web Push)** — operator: create a Firebase project, drop `google-services.json` (Android) / `GoogleService-Info.plist` (iOS), enable an APNs auth key + upload to Firebase, generate `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` for web. The client legs are built, not owed: `firebase_messaging` registration ships on both mobile twins and writes to `device_tokens` (never `user_devices.push_token`, a column that has never existed), and the server-side **web-push** leg ships too. What is left after the credentials is the workout-reminder + kudos-receive handlers and `apps/web/src/lib/util/push.ts`. The delivery rail itself is **Native push delivery** below.
- [ ] **Garmin Connect** — blocked on the multi-day Garmin Developer Program application + OAuth client provisioning. Then follow the Strava pattern: a `garmin.dart` helper + a `garmin-import` Edge Function + a Settings tile wired to OAuth. (Distinct from the on-watch Connect IQ data field at `apps/watch_garmin/`, which needs no Garmin approval — see [decisions.md § 107](../architecture/decisions.md#107-vector-1-starts-as-a-connect-iq-data-field-grade-adjusted-pace-not-a-full-watch-app).)
- [ ] **RunSignUp race-results** — needs a runsignup.com API key (free for non-commercial use). Then follow the parkrun pattern: a `runsignup-import` Edge Function + a Settings tile that ingests into `runs.metadata.event` / `position`.
- [ ] **iOS verification** — Mac-only. The byte-identical Dart codebase already supports every Android feature ([decisions §39](../architecture/decisions.md#39-mobile_android-and-mobile_ios-share-a-byte-for-byte-dart-codebase)); `parity.md` cells stay ✗/Partial *by design* until simulator/device-verified. Gates: `Runner.entitlements` (HealthKit + Sign-in-with-Apple), `pod install`, the Apple Developer Sign-in-with-Apple Services ID + APNs setup. Info.plist is already complete.
- [ ] **Apple Watch** — Xcode / watchOS device required: route-nav visuals, **in-run mini-map on a wrist**, ultra-length stress test, live race participant, complication target (Widget Extension), activity types, lap markers, hold-to-stop, TTS cues, pedometer, GPS self-heal, indoor mode, route picker, BLE pairing UI — each wired in Xcode + verified on a simulator or paired device. The mini-map is the one item already **built and host-tested** (2026-08-21, [decisions § 702](../architecture/decisions.md)): `xcodebuild test` is green on the watchOS 26.4 simulator, so what remains for it is strictly *device* verification — line weights and the position dot read on a real panel in sunlight, the paged swipe between stats and map is usable with a wet glove, and the trail's shape survives the thinning passes a multi-hour run puts it through.

## Mobile

- [x] **Sign-up takes the password in a single field, so a typo locks the account out permanently.** Closed 2026-07-16 alongside the web fix: `auth_gates.dart` (the `checkPasswordPair` Dart twin, 17 mirror tests) + a confirm field on `sign_up_screen.dart` checked before `signUp`, ARB keys across all seven catalogues, four widget tests, mirrored to the iOS twin. Sign-up is the only password-minting surface on mobile, so the class is closed there. See [web_app_auth.md § Password confirmation](../features/web_app_auth.md#password-confirmation).
- [x] **Change password now has a current-password step-up (the mobile half of issue #381).** Landed 2026-07-20 in the same PR as the web fix: `settings_account_screen.dart`'s `_changePassword` dialog carries a current-password field and drives the pure `password_change.dart` twin of `lib/core/password_change.ts` (now a parity pair — `checkPasswordPair`, then `ApiClient.verifyCurrentPassword` over `signInWithPassword`, fail-closed, before `updatePassword`), plus an "email me a reset link" fallback for OAuth-only accounts (lands on web `/auth/reset`). ARB keys across all catalogues, unit + widget tests, and the byte-identical iOS mirror shipped alongside. See [decisions §278](../architecture/decisions.md).
- [x] **Undo has no mobile equivalent** — closed in round 13 (decisions § 523). `apps/mobile_android/lib/undo_queue.dart` is the Dart twin of web `core/undo_queue.ts` (a new parity pair, 14 mirror tests each); `widgets/undo_bar.dart` hosts the offer in a **root `Overlay` entry** rather than the `SnackBar` this entry originally proposed — a snack bar under a modal barrier is dropped from the semantics tree entirely, and `showSnackBar` was already banned in `lib/screens`/`lib/widgets` by an architecture guard. All seven of web's adopting deletes adopted, `undo_window_s` is read AND editable on the phone, and the deferred-commit contract is guarded per call site.
- [x] **Undo offered on every eligible web surface** — round 12 closed the four the U8 sweep enumerated ([decisions § 516](../architecture/decisions.md)): the **notification** dismiss (single row and a collapsed group — one intent, one undo slot, one batched delete), the **route review** delete (issue #664), the **route marker** delete, and the **gear wear-log** delete, which was the one blocked on design. That block is resolved rather than routed around: `Modal.svelte`'s Tab ring now admits a designated `[data-modal-trap-include]` host, so the bar is keyboard-reachable inside a dialog, guarded by `src/lib/undo_modal_trap_guard.test.ts` + a keyboard-only leg in `tests-e2e/settings/gear.spec.ts`. Everything still destructive keeps its confirm on purpose — see [conventions.md § Destructive actions](../architecture/conventions.md).
- [x] **Mirror "train for this race" onto the phone.** Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 671](../architecture/decisions.md)): `race_plan_preset.dart` is a registered parity pair (17 mirror tests each), `RacesScreen`'s card shows a "Train for this race" action under the same refusal gate the web card uses, and `PlanNewScreen` takes `raceDateIso` / `raceName` / `raceDistanceM` — the listing's facts, re-derived against today exactly as the web URL is — seeding goal, name, start date and week count, and rendering the anchored note or the refusal reason. ARB keys across all seven catalogues; iOS twin mirrored. Original entry: Web shipped the race-calendar → plan-generator bridge on 2026-08-14 ([decisions § 606](../architecture/decisions.md)): `apps/web/src/lib/training/race_plan_preset.ts` sizes a plan so its final `race` week contains race day, and `RaceCalendarCard` → `/plans/new` carries the race's facts. Mobile has both ends already — `screens/races_screen.dart` and `screens/plan_new_screen.dart` (which holds `_startDate` / `_weekOverride` / `_goalEvent` and already drives `generatePlan`) — but nothing between them. The mirror is a Dart twin of `race_plan_preset.ts` (a new parity pair: register it in `CLAUDE.md` **and** `.claude/agents/shared-library-syncer.md`, or `check_parity_pair_registry.mjs` fails the PR), a preset argument on `PlanNewScreen`, a "train for this race" row action on the races screen, ARB keys across all seven catalogues, and the byte-identical iOS mirror. Deliberately not written on web's schedule — a Dart twin with no caller is dead code the parity guard would then police forever.
- [x] **The comeback runner gets no load warning in the case that most warrants one.** Closed 2026-08-15 by `apps/web/src/lib/training/comeback.ts` + the dashboard `ComebackCard` ([decisions § 612](../architecture/decisions.md)): the week is graded against the runner's own pre-break weekly average rather than a near-empty chronic window, firing only where `selfLoad` is silent so the two cards are mutually exclusive by construction. Original entry: `self_load` (and `plan_ramp` before it) refuse to grade below `MIN_ACTIVE_WEEKS` of the chronic window, so a runner returning from a layoff who logs one big week after three empty ones sees `activeWeeks = 1` → `insufficient` → no card. The gate is right and should NOT be lowered: dividing a comeback week by a near-zero base manufactures a terrifying ratio out of thin air, and a safety signal that cries wolf gets ignored (decisions § 609). The gap is that ACWR is the wrong *instrument* for this runner, not that its threshold is wrong — the honest signal is a different claim ("you have not run in N weeks; this is a big first week back"), derived from the layoff itself rather than from a ratio. `training_load.ts` already knows what a layoff is (`kLayoffResetDays`, 28), so the input exists. Surfaced by the round-3 audit; sized as its own change because it needs new copy and a new rule, not a tweak to this one. Relevant persona: `runner-comeback`.
- [x] **Mirror the runner's own load-ramp band onto the phone.** Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 670](../architecture/decisions.md)) together with the comeback mirror below, because the two share the reduction and the gate that keeps them mutually exclusive: `self_load.dart` + `comeback.dart` are registered parity pairs (15 and 21 mirror tests each) importing `recentRunVolume` / `volumeSample` from `plan_ramp.dart` rather than copying them, and `widgets/load_ramp_card.dart` + `widgets/comeback_card.dart` render them on the dashboard off one conversion of the runner's runs. The DNF divergence from the coach roster (decisions § 592) is carried over and pinned by a test. Original entry: Web shipped it 2026-08-14 ([decisions § 609](../architecture/decisions.md)): `apps/web/src/lib/training/self_load.ts` composes the existing ACWR policy over the runner's own runs and `LoadRampCard` renders it on `/dashboard`. Mobile already carries the `coach_load.dart` twin (the coaching screen uses it for the roster) but has no self-facing surface, so the mirror is a Dart twin of the `selfLoad` composition plus the acute half of the volume reducer, a dashboard card, ARB keys across all seven catalogues, and the byte-identical iOS mirror. Deliberately not written on web's schedule — a Dart twin with no caller is dead code the parity guard would then police forever. Note the DNF divergence from the coach roster (decisions § 592) is intentional and must be carried over, not "fixed" to match the SQL.
- [x] **Nobody can change an SMS consent after confirming, on either platform.** Closed 2026-08-25 ([decisions § 726](../architecture/decisions.md)). `fetchSafetyContactOf` reads the contact-of half of `safety_contacts` scoped on `contact_user_id` — the predicate the linked-contact SELECT policy already permitted, so no migration — and both platforms render a "you are a safety contact for" section: an SMS toggle over `set_safety_sms_opt_in` and a withdraw over `decline_safety_contact`. The withdraw is the correctness point: `removeSafetyContact` became owner-scoped in § 720, so wiring the button to it would match nothing here and toast success while the relationship stood; a Playwright leg pins that the RPC fires and no table DELETE does. One correction to the entry below: the number is reported as a FACT (`has_phone`), not shown, mirroring `my_pending_safety_requests` — a contact cannot correct a wrong number from there anyway (no owner UPDATE policy), so a relationship with none renders a note rather than a toggle that would consent to nothing. Web + Android + the iOS twin, all six web locales + all seven ARBs, two Playwright legs, five Flutter widget tests, source guards on both clients, five pgtap assertions. Original entry: `set_safety_sms_opt_in` (migration `20270410_001`) lets a confirmed contact turn SMS alerts on or off later, and works — but no surface on web or mobile lists the relationships a user is the *contact* of, only the ones they own. So the consent can be given exactly once, at confirm time, and never withdrawn in-app; a contact who changes their mind has to withdraw from the relationship entirely. The Dart binding was removed in round 11 rather than left as an unused method ([decisions § 719](../architecture/decisions.md)). The fix is a small "you are a safety contact for…" section on both `/settings/safety` and Settings → Safety contacts — the linked-contact RLS read policy on `safety_contacts` (`20261218_001`) already permits the query, so it needs no backend change. **Read the item below first.** That query's accidental union used to be where these rows surfaced; as of decisions § 720 it is scoped, so this section has to fetch the contact-of direction deliberately (`.eq('contact_user_id', uid)`) — which is what it always should have done, and which the linked-contact read policy already permits. Relevant persona: `runner-woman`.
- [x] **`fetchMySafetyContacts` claims to be owner-scoped on both platforms and is not.** Closed 2026-08-24 ([decisions § 720](../architecture/decisions.md)). Verified as described, with one correction worth recording: the table carries **four** permissive policies, not two — `safety_contacts linked contact read` **and** `safety_contacts linked contact delete` (`20261218_001`, both still present after `20270416_001`'s initplan wrap) sit alongside the owner pair. So the Remove button was not the benign "withdrawal" this entry read as: `removeSafetyContact` deleted by `id` alone, the linked-contact DELETE policy permitted it, and pressing it therefore **deleted the other person's emergency-contact row** while toasting the presser that their own list had been tidied. RLS neither refused it nor no-op'd it. Fixed at the root on both clients — `.eq('owner_id', uid)` on the read *and* the delete, both refusing outright when signed out rather than issuing an unscoped query, and the false doc comments replaced with what the policies actually do. Deliberately **not** a policy change: the contact-side policies are correct, and the follow-up above needs exactly those rows. Pinned by source guards on both clients (`data.test.ts`, `packages/api_client/test/safety_contacts_scope_test.dart`) plus four pgtap assertions in `safety_contacts_test.sql` that demonstrate the union, the scoped read, the destructive unscoped delete and the inert scoped one. Original entry: Web's `fetchMySafetyContacts` (`core/data.ts`) and Dart's (`api_client.dart`) both select from `safety_contacts` with no `owner_id` filter, and both carry a doc comment saying "RLS scopes to `owner_id = me`". That was true when the table shipped, but `20261218_001` added a second SELECT policy — `safety_contacts linked contact read`, `using (contact_user_id = auth.uid())` — so the query now returns the union: the contacts you added, **plus** every relationship where someone else added you and you confirmed. Both surfaces render that union as "your safety contacts" with a Remove button (which does work — the sibling delete policy permits withdrawal), so a runner sees people they never added listed as their own emergency contacts, and the SMS state shown beside such a row describes a number they do not own. Not a data leak (RLS is doing what it was told; the columns are ones the contact may read) and not a new regression — it predates the SMS work, which is why round 11 did not fix it inside a mobile-mirror slice. The fix is one `.eq('owner_id', uid)` on each side plus a corrected comment, and it should land **with** the follow-up above, since the rows it removes from the owner list are exactly the ones the contact-side section needs. Web + mobile, same change.
- [ ] **Pro native RevenueCat purchase sheet + native donate** — scaffolding shipped (`revenuecat.dart`); the live sheet needs a RevenueCat project + a `pro_monthly` package + `REVENUECAT_API_KEY_ANDROID` / `_IOS` provisioned. Until then the Subscribe tile falls through to the web URL. (The store-localised Pro price is already wired via `getOfferings()`; it just needs the RC project provisioned for the localised amount to appear.)
- [x] **Health Connect import brings tracks (garmin/android, mobile + device)** — Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 783](../architecture/decisions.md)). **Three of this entry's four claims were false when it was read.** `health_connect_importer.dart` has not hard-coded `track: []` since #664: it reads `WORKOUT_ROUTE`, joins on the `ExerciseSessionRecord` id, screens each fix for finite in-range coordinates, orders by time, and passes a real track into `Run` — with `READ_EXERCISE_ROUTES` in both the manifest and `res/xml/health_permissions.xml`, a `HealthRoutePermissionBridge` method channel to ask for it, a `withheldSessionIds` set driving an after-the-fact backfill, a `readFailure` field so a throw is not reported as an absence, and 60-odd host tests. "Lapless" is true but was filed as though it were the same problem: `ExerciseSessionRecord.laps` is surfaced by the `health` package **only on the write path**, so it is not reachable at all (see the entry below). What was left was the **cadence** half, and its cause was neither the route nor a sample series: `fetchWorkouts` read `value.totalDistance` off the `WorkoutHealthValue` and dropped `value.totalSteps` — a field already in the object in hand — so no imported run ever carried `metadata.steps` and the run-detail Steps and Cadence tiles could not render for one. `READ_STEPS` was also declared nowhere, and the plugin builds `totalSteps` by summing a `StepsRecord` read over the session window, so the field would have stayed null even had the Dart side asked. Both fixed, with `stepsForWorkout` screening `cycle` and physiologically impossible counts, and no `cadence_spm` written — steps is the fact, the shared `steps / moving_time_minutes` derivation is the cadence. 7 host tests, iOS twin mirrored.
- [ ] **Health Connect: verify both grants on hardware, and reach the laps that are not reachable today** (needs an Android device with Health Connect + a routed session written by another app). Two separate things, filed together because one device session settles both. **(a) The device gate.** Everything about the route and step paths is host-tested against fixtures; nothing has run against a real Health Connect. What must be checked on hardware: that the `READ_EXERCISE_ROUTES` sheet raised by `HealthRoutePermissionBridge` actually appears and returns `true`; that a granted route read yields locations rather than the zero-location `ConsentRequired` shape (and that a *refused* one yields exactly that shape, which the withheld-set logic depends on); that `READ_STEPS` makes `WorkoutHealthValue.totalSteps` non-null for a foot-powered session; and — the one with teeth — **what Health Connect does with the plugin's ungranted sub-reads**. `handleWorkoutData` reads `DistanceRecord`, `TotalCaloriesBurnedRecord` and `StepsRecord` inside the session loop with no permission check (the `authorizedTypeMap` filter guards only the top-level type), and `getData` swallows anything thrown into `result.success(emptyList())`. We hold `READ_DISTANCE` and now `READ_STEPS` but **not** `READ_TOTAL_CALORIES_BURNED`. If `readRecords` throws `SecurityException` for an ungranted type rather than returning empty, the entire workout batch comes back empty and *no* activity imports — a state the app would report as "no workouts found". That permission was deliberately not added speculatively: we consume no calorie value, and an undeclared-but-unused health permission is a Play Data Safety liability (the bridge's own comment states the principle). Settle it on a device; if it throws, the durable fix is our own `ExerciseSessionRecord` read alongside the route bridge rather than one more permission to satisfy a plugin's unguarded aggregation. **(b) Laps.** `ExerciseSessionRecord` carries `laps` and `segments`, but `health` 13.3.1 binds them only in `HealthDataWriter.kt` (preserving them across an update); there is no read binding and no `HealthDataType`, so a multi-lap imported session flattens to one interval and `metadata.laps` is never written. Closing it needs a lap read of our own beside `HealthRoutePermissionBridge`, or an upstream change — either way it is a build, not a wiring fix, and it shares the device session above.
- [ ] **Background sync doesn't execute on an iOS device (BGAppRefreshTask vs `fetch`)** — `background_sync.dart`'s `registerPeriodicTask` maps to a `BGAppRefreshTaskRequest` on iOS, which requires the `fetch` `UIBackgroundMode`; Info.plist only declares `processing`. `AppDelegate.swift` registers the identifier so launch no longer crashes, but the app-refresh submit is rejected at runtime so background sync never runs in the background. Fix: add `fetch` to `UIBackgroundModes` and keep app-refresh, OR switch the Dart side to a `BGProcessingTask`. Needs on-device testing. iOS-only — Android uses `WorkManager` and is unaffected. See `apps/mobile_ios/CLAUDE.md § Catch-up status`.
- [x] **Retire the vestigial `onStartRun` route-carrying callback plumbing** — DONE, and this box was stale rather than open: the cleanup landed with #791 and was re-verified 2026-08-26. No `void Function(cm.Route)` remains anywhere under `apps/mobile_android/lib/`, and `routes_screen` / `explore_routes` / `fitness_hub` — three of the four screens this entry named — no longer mention `onStartRun` at all. What survives is a *different, live* member that the entry's wording invites a future reader to delete by mistake: a `VoidCallback?` on `dashboard_screen`, carrying no route and reached from one `home_screen` call site, which is the dashboard's "start a run" affordance. Issue #789 already recorded this as closed; the drift was doc-side only.
- [ ] **Course-marker drag-to-move on mobile** (needs device) — **snap-on-tap-placement SHIPPED 2026-07-02**: `route_markers_panel.dart` gained a default-on "Snap to route line" toggle that projects the tapped point onto the route polyline via `snapToPolyline` before placing (render-only; `position_m` still server-derived), mirroring web's `RouteMarkerEditor`, twin-mirrored to iOS, pinned by two widget tests. **Remaining:** the draggable-symbol affordance on `LiveRunMap` (maplibre-flutter `SymbolManager` drag) — needs on-device verification, deferred. See [route_markers.md § Surfaces](../features/route_markers.md#surfaces).
- [x] **`activity_type` has no shared localized vocabulary, and the coach surface renders raw English.** Closed. The mobile + web halves were already done by `d129a55bb` (`activity_type_labels.dart#activityTypeLabel`; web's `runs/activity_type.svelte.ts` behind the broader `enum_vocabulary` registry guard) — **verified, not assumed**, including that `feed_screen._activityLabel` correctly keeps `lift` and its "All" default, which are feed-filter scopes over the `activities` view and NOT members of the CHECK domain. The bug was still live on a **fourth** platform: Wear OS carried its own resolver covering four of five values with a `replaceFirstChar { it.uppercase() }` fallback, and `stroller` was reachable there without ever being cycled to, because `default_activity_type` primes the pre-run chip straight off the phone's settings bag with no allowlist — so the one value the wrist could not name was the one the phone could hand it. Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 654](../architecture/decisions.md)) with the label in all six Wear catalogues, the capitalising fallback replaced by the raw token (matching web's documented rationale), and a third guard deriving its value set from the `runs_activity_type_check` migration exactly as the mobile and web ones do.
- [x] **Nutrition targets are not a peer of the mobile Nutrition surface** — closed 2026-08-19 ([decisions § 695](../architecture/decisions.md)). `screens/nutrition_targets_screen.dart` mirrors web's `/nutrition/targets`: the derivation (resting metabolism → activity factor → goal delta → base → today's workout add-on) then the macro split, composed only from what `nutrition_targets.dart` already exported — no new pure logic and **no parity-pair change**, exactly as the entry asked. The two non-sensitive levers edit inline through the same `SettingsSyncService.updateUniversal` path the consent-gated editor writes them on (the label switches were promoted to `activityLevelLabel` / `weightGoalLabel` rather than copied, so the two surfaces cannot name one stored value differently); height / weight / DOB / sex are **read-only** here and keep their single Art 9 entry point in `SettingsBodyMetricsScreen`, pinned by a source guard mirroring web's `targets_peer_guard.test.ts`. The rings card carries the entry **ungated** — it renders whether or not targets exist, which the widget tests pin in both states, because the runner with no targets is the one who needs the derivation. 24 keys across all seven ARBs; 12 tests; iOS twin byte-identical. Original entry: **Nutrition targets are not a peer of the mobile Nutrition surface** — web closed issue #666 M1's structural half on 2026-08-05 with `/nutrition/targets` (derivation + the two non-sensitive levers at its own URL, ungated from the `/nutrition` header; height/weight/DOB/sex stay in the Art 9 consent-gated Settings editor). On mobile, `nutrition_screen.dart` reaches `SettingsBodyMetricsScreen` from its **empty state only** ([decisions § 490](../architecture/decisions.md)) — once targets exist there is no route to the number the rings are measured against, and no derivation view exists on either twin. The mobile shape is a peer entry beside the rings card (or a row on the § 488 peer strip if Nutrition gains one), pushing a new `NutritionTargetsScreen` that composes the **existing** `nutrition_targets.dart` exports — the Dart twin already has every constant and `mifflinStJeorBmr`, so this needs no new pure logic and no parity-pair change. Web is canonical per § 24, so this is a follow-up, not drift to close first.
- [x] **Nutrition diary date navigation is web-only** — closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 656](../architecture/decisions.md)). `nutrition_screen.dart` holds the viewed day as a local `YYYY-MM-DD` over the new registered `diary_day` parity pair (26 mirror tests each). A stepper above the rings walks the day, and every read (food window, exercise add-on, trend buckets, water key, meal-detail deep link) and **every write path the screen owns** (composer via the log sheet's new `diaryDate`, logged meal template, logged recipe) follows it, stamping `entryTimestampFor(viewedDay, now)` at SAVE time — a surface that lets a runner review yesterday but silently logs to today would be worse than no stepper. Steps go through `DateTime(y, m, d + n)` so a 23/25-hour DST day cannot repeat or skip, and the code emits no `toIso8601String()` at all. 10 keys across all seven ARBs; `parity.md` flipped.
- [x] **`fetchGymWorkouts` has no date window, so a deep diary day under-reads its lift calories** — `fetchRuns` takes `startedAtFrom` / `startedAtBefore` and the nutrition day view uses them to fetch exactly the viewed day's runs, but its gym sibling only took a `limit`, so the page pulled the newest 50 sessions and filtered. A diary day older than the caller's 50 most recent lifts therefore contributed no gym calories to that day's `exerciseKcal` — an under-read, identical in effect to "the lift wasn't logged yet", not a wrong kind of number, but avoidable. **Done 2026-08-13** ([decisions § 597](../architecture/decisions.md)): `fetchGymWorkoutsWithError` / `fetchGymWorkouts` now take a `FetchGymWorkoutsOptions` bag carrying `limit` plus the same two bounds `fetchRuns` has, applied as PostgREST `.gte()` / `.lt()` on `started_at`; `/nutrition` passes the viewed day's window and its `GYM_FETCH_LIMIT` + client-side filter are gone. The three other call sites (`/gym`, `/dashboard`, `/nutrition/targets`) moved to `{ limit: n }` with no behaviour change. Pinned by a source guard in `lib/core/data.test.ts`.
- [x] **A long-lived `/nutrition` tab does not roll over at local midnight** — `todayIso` / `yesterdayIso` (`routes/nutrition/+page.svelte`) were recomputed only inside the `$effect`, which fires on a `$page.url` or `authReady` change and never on a timer, so a tab left open past local midnight kept labelling the previous day "Today" and kept the Next-day button wrongly disabled. A stale-label bug, not a data bug: every write path resolves `entryTimestampFor(viewDate, new Date())` at save time with a fresh clock ([decisions § 591](../architecture/decisions.md)), so nothing was ever filed onto the wrong day. **Done 2026-08-13** ([decisions § 597](../architecture/decisions.md)): a `syncDay` re-check on **two** triggers, each costing one wakeup a day — a single `setTimeout` armed for `msUntilNextLocalMidnight(new Date())` (the new calendar-stepped helper in `diary_day.ts`, DST-tested), which is the only trigger that reaches a tab that stays *visible* across midnight, plus the proposed `visibilitychange` check for the backgrounded tab whose timer the browser throttled or whose machine slept. Still no polling interval. The day is re-resolved from the URL, so a bare `/nutrition` follows the clock while an explicit past `?date=` only changes its label. Pinned by `nutrition/diary_rollover.test.ts` + the `msUntilNextLocalMidnight` cases in `diary_day.test.ts` / `diary_day.dst.test.ts`.

- [x] **Mobile mirror: the plan wizard's opening-week ramp check** — closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 655](../architecture/decisions.md)). `plan_ramp.dart` is a faithful twin (34 mirror tests each — the docs' claim of 32 was stale and is corrected), banding through the existing `coach_load.dart` twin's `injuryRiskBand` so the wizard and the coach roster cannot drift; `plan_new_screen.dart` renders the note in the preview card off a guarded `TrainingService.fetchRecentRunVolume` whose failure leaves the wizard untouched. Silent below three active weeks, and `under` still withheld from a beginner walk-run plan. Registered in both registries. It did **not** duplicate the shared reduction: `recentRunVolume` / `volumeSample` / the two constants live in `plan_ramp.ts` on web (both `self_load.ts` and `comeback.ts` import them from it), so the faithful port lands them at the identical module boundary in `plan_ramp.dart`.
- [x] **Mobile mirror: famous-segment catalogue UI** — closed 2026-08-19 ([decisions § 693](../architecture/decisions.md)). `screens/global_segments_screen.dart` carries both halves: a browse list with accent-folded search, region + surface filters and the four sorts, and a `GlobalSegmentDetailScreen` with the curated geometry as a `TrackPreview` plus the block-guarded gender / age-band leaderboard and its crown label. The run-detail famous-segment chips, which linked nowhere on the phone, now open the detail screen, and the Fitness hub gained the catalogue entry. `catalogue_browse.dart` is a registered parity pair (28 mirror tests web / 27 Dart — the web extra covers an NFD case Dart reaches through an explicit table instead), registered in **both** registries as § 604 requires. ARB keys across all seven catalogues; iOS twin byte-identical. Original entry: **Mobile mirror: famous-segment catalogue UI** — web shipped `/segments`, the browse half of the §233 catalogue ([decisions § 593](../architecture/decisions.md)), on top of the new web-only `segments/catalogue_browse.ts` (search with accent folding, region / surface filters, four sorts). Mobile carries the `computeGlobalSegmentEffort` twin in `segments.dart` but **no catalogue UI at all** — neither a browse list nor a detail screen — so the run-detail effort chips there link nowhere. The mirror is a `GlobalSegmentsScreen` + a catalogue detail screen; the Dart twin of the four shaping helpers lands with them, not before (a twin with no consumer is dead code). Web is canonical per §24.
- [x] **Mobile mirror: add a club event to the phone's calendar** — closed 2026-08-19 ([decisions § 692](../architecture/decisions.md)). `event_detail_screen.dart` gained an "Add to calendar" action for the occurrence on screen and, for a recurring event, "Add whole series" beside it; both are withheld for a past or called-off occurrence. The mirror is the native one as this entry asked: `lib/calendar_intent.dart` hands the event over the `run_app/calendar` channel to `ACTION_INSERT` on `CalendarContract.Events` (`CalendarBridge.kt`) and to `EKEventEditViewController` (`CalendarBridge.swift`), so the runner confirms the save in their own calendar app — no permission on Android, write-only access on iOS. **No Dart port of the `.ics` builder and no parity pair**, per § 24. The series crosses the channel as an RFC 5545 RRULE (the one grammar both platforms already speak) built by `buildRrule`, which mirrors web's refusals — a monthly series anchored past the 28th is withheld, an `until` + `count` pair resolves to whichever binds first, BYDAY is read off the expansion in UTC. Neither hand-off carries `EXDATE`, so cancelled occurrences ahead are **named under the button** (over `event_occurrence.dart`'s `isOccurrenceCancelled`, reused not re-derived) rather than silently kept. 5 ARB keys across all seven catalogues; 38 tests (30 unit + 8 widget); `parity.md` + `clubs.md` corrected.

## Watch (Wear OS) — sized features awaiting hardware + a product green-light

Most need the Wear build toolchain plus on-watch verification (Galaxy Watch / Pixel Watch) — the recording stack isn't runtime-testable without a device.

- [x] **Honour the mi distance preference on the watch** — `preferredUnit` added to `UniversalSettings` (parsed from `prefs.preferred_unit`, validated against `{km, mi}`) and threaded onto `RunViewModel.UiState` as a `DistanceUnit` (same path as `bodyWeightKg`). `recording/UnitFormat.kt` gained `DistanceUnit` + `formatDistance` + `paceSecPerUnit`; `distance_mi` / `distance_mi_to_go` / `pace_per_mi` added to `values/strings.xml` + all five `values-*`. Applied at the running screen (distance + pace + ambient), the route "to go" badge, the route picker, the PostRun summary, and the active-run tile (unit carried on `RecordingRepository.Metrics`, set at `startRecording` via `EXTRA_PREFERRED_UNIT`). Pure parse/format pinned in `UnitFormatTest` + `ActiveRunTileFormattersTest` + `UniversalSettingsTest`. **TTS closed 2026-08-03** (issue #664, [decisions.md § 467](../architecture/decisions.md)): `UnitFormat.kt` gained `splitIntervalMetres` / `completedSplits` so the cue *fires* once per mile for a mi-mode runner rather than every kilometre, and every spoken resource split into a `_km` / `_mi` pair across all six locales with the pace scaled through `paceSecPerUnit`. Pinned in `UnitFormatTest` / `TtsPhrasesTest` plus a `TtsSplitUnitWiringTest` source guard (the announcer's resource lookup needs Robolectric, which this module doesn't carry). **Remaining:** the on-face visual flip and the audible read-out both need a device check.
- [ ] **BLE chest-strap HR paired directly to the watch** (~1-1.5 wk + device) — today watch HR is optical (Health Services `MeasureClient`) only. A strap paired to the *watch* means an on-watch Wear OS BLE GATT client: scan/pair UI, HR Service (`0x180D`)/HR Measurement (`0x2A37`) subscription, `BLUETOOTH_SCAN`+`BLUETOOTH_CONNECT` perms, source-preference override into `metadata.avg_bpm`. Reconnect/dropout on the wrist is the fiddly part. Minority feature; needs hardware to validate.
- [ ] **Real body weight → calorie estimate** (~2-4 d + device) — `recording/RunCalories.kt` defaults to 70 kg when `body_weight_kg` is unset. Pipe the actual weight in. Source options: (a) Samsung Health BIA (most accurate, heaviest — Samsung SDK/partner approval); (b) Health Services/Health Connect `WeightRecord` (vendor-neutral, lighter); (c) the existing universal-setting sync from web/mobile (cheapest, manual). Watch still won't apply the female calibration (reads `user_settings.prefs` only). Needs a device to confirm the read path returns a value.
- [ ] **Watch-face complication** (~1-1.5 wk + device) — only an active-run *tile* ships today; a true watch-face complication is unbuilt. Needs a `ComplicationDataSourceService` (`wear-watchface-complications-data-source`), `SHORT_TEXT`/`RANGED_VALUE`/`MONOCHROMATIC_IMAGE` types, a tap `PendingIntent` into `MainActivity`, the same `requestUpdate`-on-stage-transition wiring the tile uses, + preview/picker label. Pure formatters unit-testable; binding + render need a watch face that hosts the slot.

## Internationalisation

RTL *layout* is complete web-wide; web + mobile string extraction is complete (5,049 web keys ×7 locales — the ~2,682 figure predated several rounds of copy; the mobile ARB pass is done at 3,772 keys ×7). Remaining:

- [x] **The rate-limit refusal was English on every platform, for every bucket.** **CLOSED 2026-08-26** ([decisions § 744](../architecture/decisions.md)). `rateLimitErrorMessage` was a TS↔Dart pair that returned a finished English sentence, so the prose sat inside the one module the lockstep forbids either platform from diverging on and no locale could get anything else. It is parse-only now — both halves return `{bucket, seconds}` — with nine translated sentences plus three wait forms added to all six web catalogues and all seven ARBs (including a real European/Brazilian Portuguese split) and assembled by per-platform render glue outside the lockstep. Filed as a `direct_messages` copy item; it was the whole helper, ten buckets across six surfaces, and **web fell through to the generic `'doing that'` sentence for the two send buckets exactly as Dart did** — a trade-off § 737 made deliberately, and one the split makes free. English is unchanged character for character (three Playwright specs match on it), both parsers carry a source guard that no user-facing phrase may return to them, and the render suites pin every bucket in every locale.
- [x] **`create_challenge` throttles through its own trigger, so a refused challenge create still shows the raw postgres exception.** **CLOSED 2026-08-26** ([decisions § 747](../architecture/decisions.md), migration `20270610_001`). The bucket count was right — exactly ten reach `enforce_create_rate_limit`, and `create_challenge` is the only non-conforming rate-limit raise in the whole migration set (the other `raise exception` lines naming a rate limit are `check_rate_limit` / `check_rate_limit_tiered` argument-validation and caller-guard errors, not throttle refusals). **The user-facing half was wrong in the direction that mattered.** Nobody read a raw postgres exception: mobile has no challenge-create surface at all (`SocialService.createChallenge` has zero callers under `apps/mobile_*/lib`), and web's `ChallengeEditor` discarded the error and toasted the generic "Couldn't create the challenge." So the defect was a *swallowed* refusal naming neither cause nor wait, not a leaked one — worse, because the raw string at least told a reader to come back later. "A migration plus both type regenerations, not a client change" was wrong both ways: the migration alone reaches no reader (the editor drops `e.message`), and there is nothing to regenerate — a trigger function never enters `database.types.ts` and the Dart generator reads only `create table` / `alter table`; both generators re-ran to a zero diff. Fixed at the producer (one `perform enforce_create_rate_limit('create_challenge', new.creator_id, 30, 3600)`, which also moves the forged-`creator_id` case from a mis-classified P0001 to RLS's 42501, skips `service_role`, and drops a fail-open wrapper guarding against nothing) plus the client wiring, `rateLimit.createChallenge` in six catalogues and seven ARBs, a class-level source guard that no trigger body may call `check_rate_limit` directly, and `challenge_create_rate_limit_test.sql`. Original filing below.
  - Surfaced counting the buckets for § 744 (2026-08-26). Ten buckets go through `enforce_create_rate_limit`, which raises the `rate limit exceeded for <bucket>, retry in Ns` shape the client parser reads; `create_challenge` (migration `20270308_001`) instead raises the bare string `challenge_create_rate_limited` with no bucket and no retry figure, and nothing on either platform handles it. The durable fix is to route that trigger through `enforce_create_rate_limit` like every other bucket so it inherits the parser, the localized sentence and the cap accounting for free — a migration plus both type regenerations, not a client change. Adding a second parser branch for the odd shape would be the band-aid.
- [ ] **Native review of machine-extracted translations** — the de/es/fr/ja/pt-BR/**pt-PT** strings (web catalogue + mobile ARB + **the Wear OS `values-*` sets** + the nutrition/consent copy) are best-effort machine output. Agents flagged fitness-domain tooltips (CTL/ATL/TSB/VO₂max) and a few register / article-agreement / fragment-split spots. **European Portuguese joined this list 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 755](../architecture/decisions.md)) and is the least reviewed of the set: web's 5,049 keys and the wrist's 102 resources were derived from Brazilian through a substitution corpus mined from `app_pt.arb`'s own 359 curated strings, not translated by a human. **`app_pt.arb` itself joined the list the same day** ([§ 760](../architecture/decisions.md)), when 822 of its strings were derived through that same corpus, so the phone is now model-authored on the same terms as the browser and the wrist rather than being the curated source they were mined from — all three want the one pass. **The same day it widened again to the server-sent and watchOS catalogues** ([decisions § 761](../architecture/decisions.md)): 293 further model-authored strings across `apps/job_worker/internal/{email_i18n.go,sms_i18n.go}` (181 — every notification, lifecycle, drip, digest, safety and both push channels), `apps/backend/supabase/functions/auth-email/lib.ts` (55 — sign-up, magic link, password reset, e-mail change, reauthentication) and `apps/watch_ios/WatchApp/Localizable.xcstrings` (57). Those are the highest-stakes strings in the set — a password-reset mail and an account-erasure receipt are read once, under pressure, by someone who cannot ask us what a word meant — so they want the native pass **first**, ahead of the UI catalogues. Known-weak spots named at the time: the register choice (third-person elision throughout, where `app_pt.arb` mixes `tu` and third person), `password` → "Palavra-passe", which § 760 propagated to the phone and the browser so all five surfaces now agree (they did not when this list was written), the Wear battery-settings strings that quote real Android menu paths ("Apps" vs pt-PT's "Aplicações"), and `hike` reading "Trilho" on the wrist against "Trail" on phone and web. **Widened again 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 767](../architecture/decisions.md)) with two things measured and left rather than guessed, both wanting a native call specifically. **(a) Register: ~~the phone still addresses its reader two ways~~ — CLOSED 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 782](../architecture/decisions.md)), and *not* as a native-speaker call: web's `pt-PT.ts` had already decided the possessive unanimously (335 `seu`/`sua`, **zero** `teu`/`tua`) and `app_pt_BR.arb` carries zero as well, so aligning the phone was a consistency repair rather than a linguistic judgement. The count was **44 occurrences over 39 keys**, not 36, and the possessive was one axis of four — 7 tu-only finite verbs, 6 `-te` enclitics, one `contigo` and 9 tu imperatives no token can see brought it to **54 strings**, plus **20 spoken cues**. Half the possessive strings carried a tu verb in the same sentence, so the filed scope could not have been executed on its own. Guarded on both axes by `architecture_guards_test.dart § locale reach`, proved to fail against the pre-fix catalogue and on a single planted string. **(b) `longão`**, Brazilian running slang for the long run, in **7** web keys and **5** phone keys. It is community vocabulary rather than dictionary vocabulary — `o longo` and `a corrida longa` are both plausible in Portugal — and getting the register wrong across twelve sites is worse than leaving one word Brazilian. Wants a native-speaker pass before advertising full localisation.
- [x] **`app_pt.arb` is not reachable at runtime, so its European-Portuguese content ships to nobody — decide whether pt-PT is a locale.** **Settled 2026-08-25 by shipping it** ([decisions § 740](../architecture/decisions.md)). Half of the 2026-08-05 measurement was already stale when this was re-measured on `b103c9388`: [§ 547](../architecture/decisions.md) put `Locale('pt')` into `supportedLocales` on 2026-08-06, so a `pt-PT` / `pt-AO` / bare-`pt` device does reach the European catalogue. The other half was live — `_editLanguage` listed its options as `['', 'en', 'de', 'fr', 'es', 'ja', 'pt-BR']`, so a Portuguese speaker on any other device could never select it. The picker now derives from `supportedLocales`. Option (b) was refused on the numbers: all seven catalogues carry 3,758 of 3,758 keys with zero missing and zero orphan, and `pt` differs from `pt_BR` on 349 of them — a complete catalogue that already resolves for real devices, not a mechanical copy (the filed 3,251-of-3,477 figure is superseded). Guarded on both platforms so the class cannot recur: `apps/mobile_android/test/architecture_guards_test.dart § locale reach` and `apps/web/src/lib/i18n/locale_reach.test.ts`.
- [x] **Web ships no European-Portuguese catalogue, so the phone and the browser answer a `pt-PT` reader differently.** **CLOSED 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 755](../architecture/decisions.md)). The filing was accurate — the first in this issue whose premise survived measurement — but the assumption under it was not: `app_pt.arb` is **9.5% Europeanised**, 359 of 3,772 keys, with the whole auth block and much else left as byte-identical Brazilian, so web was behind a catalogue a tenth finished. Lifting from it would have imported those leftovers. `locales/pt-PT.ts` ships instead, 5,049 keys derived from `pt-BR.ts` through a substitution corpus mined from those 359 curated strings: 133 lifted, 1,165 rule-transformed, 5 pinned to the activity vocabulary the wrist cross-checks, differing from Brazilian on **1,288 keys (25.5%)** — which makes the browser the most European surface we ship. `BASE_TO_LOCALE.pt` now points at `pt-PT` (matching the phone; Brazil reports its region on every client, and `pt-AO`/`pt-MZ`/`pt-CV` share Portugal's orthography), giving `BASE_FALLBACK_EXEMPT` the `pt-BR` entry its own comment said it was shaped for. Two things the filing did not anticipate: making pt-PT real would have **dropped Lisbon readers from Brazilian Learn guides to English**, since `getGuide` fell back to English alone — it now falls back within the reader's language first, so the still-unwritten pt-PT prose costs nothing; and six further suites hand-listed the locale set and would have skipped the new catalogue silently (see the new box below). Not natively reviewed — see the native-review item above. Original filing below.
  - Surfaced re-measuring the above (2026-08-25). Mobile carries `app_pt.arb`; web's `BASE_TO_LOCALE.pt` points at `pt-BR`, so the same reader gets European Portuguese on the phone and Brazilian on the web, and the eight localized Learn guides have no `pt` variant either. This is a *missing* catalogue, not an unreachable one — `locale_reach.test.ts` passes today because everything on disk is reachable — but it is drift from [§ 24](../architecture/decisions.md)'s web-is-canonical rule in the one direction that rule does not anticipate. Fixing it is Europeanising a copy of `locales/pt-BR.ts` plus eight guides, which is translation work and wants the native review above, not a mechanical port. Adding it also gives `locale_reach.test.ts`'s `BASE_FALLBACK_EXEMPT` its first entry.
- [x] **Wear OS ships no European-Portuguese string set, and its own locale declarations are unguarded.** **FULLY CLOSED 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 755](../architecture/decisions.md)); the declaration half closed 2026-08-26 ([§ 748](../architecture/decisions.md)). `values-b+pt+PT/strings.xml` ships the remaining **102 named resources** (99 `<string>` + 3 `<plurals>`), which reconciles this entry's "99 strings" with section S's "102 resources" — the same file counted two ways, both right. Both declaration sites moved with it: `locales_config.xml` gained `pt-PT`, `localeCatalogues` gained the `values-b+pt+PT` → (`app_pt.arb`, `pt-PT.ts`) row, and **`unmirroredPhoneCatalogues` is now empty — the wrist mirrors every phone locale for the first time.** Verified the way § 748 verified its own work: with the string set on disk and both sites reverted, `testDebugUnitTest --rerun-tasks` fails exactly two tests, one per site; restored, `assembleDebug testDebugUnitTest` is green at 638 tests, 0 failures. Not natively reviewed. Original filing below.
  - Surfaced re-measuring the pt-PT item (2026-08-25). `apps/watch_wear/android/app/src/main/res/` carries `values/` plus `values-{de,es,fr,ja}` and `values-b+pt+BR`, 99 strings each and all reachable — no `resConfigs` filter strips them, and `res/xml/locales_config.xml` lists exactly those six. A `pt-PT` watch falls back to the Brazilian set through Android's own same-language region matching, which is the right degradation, but it means the wrist disagrees with the phone for the one locale the phone ships and web does not. 99 strings; do it with the web catalogue. **The declaration seam is CLOSED 2026-08-26** ([decisions § 748](../architecture/decisions.md)) — the string set itself is still owed. Counting the sites found **five**, not the two filed: `locales_config.xml`, the manifest's `android:localeConfig` reference (without which that file is inert and the OS picker never lists the app), `L10nResourceParityTest.localeDirs`, `ActivityTypeVocabularyTest.localeCatalogues`, and its `unmirroredPhoneCatalogues` record; a sixth is absent rather than wrong — no Gradle `resConfigs` / `localeFilters` exists, confirming nothing strips a set from the APK. The counts also want one correction: 99 `<string>` plus 3 `<plurals>` is **102 named resources** per directory, not 99. The defect was reproduced before it was fixed: a seventh key-incomplete `values-pt/strings.xml` on unmodified `main` left the suite at 627 tests, 0 failures. `WearLocales` now reads the resource set and every site derives from or is held to it, and `testDebugUnitTest` was found to take `src/main/res` as no input at all — so the guard reported green on a resource-only change until that was declared. **Still open: the 102-resource pt-PT translation itself**, which is translation work and wants the native review above; do it with the web catalogue.

Held to `lib/l10n/` by the locale-reach guard, so it is a declaration site that cannot fall behind rather than a second list to remember.

- [x] **`app_pt.arb` is a tenth of a European catalogue, so the phone is now the LEAST European of the three Portuguese surfaces.** **CLOSED 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 760](../architecture/decisions.md)). The counts were right and one round stale: **359 of 3,793** keys, **9.46%**, with **3,434** byte-identical Brazilian rather than 3,413 — twenty-one keys landed between the filing and the fix. **822 strings changed**; the catalogue now differs from Brazilian on **1,164 of 3,793 keys, 30.69%**, above web's own 27.70%. The 359 curated strings are preserved verbatim. The corpus was re-derived rather than reused: word-diffing BOTH aligned pairs (`pt-BR.ts` ↔ `pt-PT.ts`, 1,289 keys, and this ARB pair's 359) yields 717 distinct substitutions, of which the ones that fire here are kept and four are DROPPED after reading their call sites — `meta`, `endereço`, `download` and `recursos` are correct European Portuguese in every place this catalogue uses them. **Half-converted strings: 17, not the 2 filed.** Beyond the two named, the whole voice-cue block spoke two registers (`ttsPaceAlertFast` third-person against `ttsPaceAlertSpeedUpByKm`'s `tu`, and the same split across `ttsPaceDrift*`, `ttsTurn*`, `ttsSlight*`, `ttsUturn`), and the curated set contradicted ITSELF on the most-used word of the lot — nine keys called a shareable link a `ligação` where four called it a `link`. **A defect the filing did not reach: `ttsLanguageTag` mapped bare `pt` to `pt-BR`**, so a European reader's spoken cues were Brazilian over European text. Not natively reviewed — see the native-review item above. Original filing below.

Measured: it differs from `app_pt_BR.arb` on **359 of 3,772 keys, 9.5%** — against web's new 25.5%. The remaining 3,413 are byte-identical Brazilian, and not at random: the entire auth block (`authPasswordLabel` "Senha", `signInForgotPassword` "Esqueceu a senha?", `authErrorOffline` "Você parece estar offline. Verifique sua conexão"), `historyTimelineEmpty` "Nada registrado", `gymOfflineCached` "treinos salvos", `runDetailSuggestRanRoute` "Parece que você correu", `settingsAccountRestoreTileSubtitle` "backup .zip salvo". Two curated strings are half-converted: `safetyAutoLiveShareSubtitle` still reads "compartilhamento ao vivo … em andamento … visibilidade padrão" inside a sentence that was otherwise rewritten, and `ttsPaceAlertFast`/`Slow` keep "Acelere"/"Diminua" where the neighbouring `ttsPaceAlertSpeedUpByKm`/`SlowDownByKm` were moved to "Acelera"/"Abranda" — so the phone says two different things for the one cue. The durable fix is to run the same substitution corpus over the ARB that built `pt-PT.ts`, in one pass, with the iOS twin mirrored; it is a mobile change and was deliberately out of this slice's scope. Wants the native review above either way.
- [x] **`app_pt.arb`'s locale tag disagrees with every other platform's, and nothing can detect it.** **CLOSED 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 760](../architecture/decisions.md)), though **not by the rename the filing proposed — that one is not available.** `flutter gen-l10n` refuses outright when a country-coded catalogue exists without a bare base of the same language ("Arb file for a fallback, pt, does not exist, even though the following locale(s) exist: [pt_BR, pt_PT]") and generates nothing at all, so `app_pt_PT.arb` cannot be the only Portuguese base. Reproduced before it was worked around. The filename is therefore the tool's and **the tag is ours**: `supportedLocales`, `localeLabels`, `_exact`, `_baseToLocale` and `locales_config.xml` all carry `pt-PT`, the generated delegate resolves `Locale('pt', 'PT')` through its `pt` base, and `arbTagOverride` in the locale-reach guard holds the two spellings together with a companion assertion that each entry is still an override — the shape [§ 740](../architecture/decisions.md) already used for the plist. `plistTagOverride` is **gone**: Apple names a locale by region, which is now what every other declaration does too. The parity pair is registered in both registries, and registering it found a real divergence — **the order of `supportedLocales` is load-bearing on the phone and not on web.** Following the device locale leaves `MaterialApp.locale` null and Flutter resolves against the list itself, first language-only match winning; measured against Flutter's own resolver, reversing `pt-PT` and `pt-BR` sends a bare-`pt` device to Brazilian while `_baseToLocale` still says European. Both are now pinned against each other. The durable half is a **tag-vs-content guard on both platforms**: a catalogue tagged for one Portuguese variant may not contain words only the other uses. It flags **319 strings** in the pre-derivation ARB and 0 in this one; no existing guard could see the difference, because they all ask whether a catalogue is REACHABLE and never whether it says what its tag claims. Original filing below.
  - Same round. The phone spells European Portuguese bare `pt` (`@@locale: pt`, `Locale('pt')`), while iOS's `Info.plist` already overrides it to `pt-PT` through `plistTagOverride`, web now ships `pt-PT.ts` and the wrist `values-b+pt+PT`. Bare `pt` is also CLDR's Brazilian default content, so the phone's tag means the opposite of what the file contains. Renaming `app_pt.arb` → `app_pt_PT.arb` (plus `supportedLocales`, `localeLabels`, `_exact`, `_baseToLocale`, the gen-l10n output and the byte-identical iOS twin) would make one tag mean one thing everywhere. Related and worth doing in the same change: `apps/web/src/lib/i18n/locale.ts` and `apps/mobile_android/lib/l10n/locale_support.dart` **describe each other as twins in their own doc comments but are not a registered TS↔Dart parity pair**, so `shared-library-syncer` never checks them — which is exactly why the two drifted to different Portuguese answers for eighteen days without anything noticing. Register the pair once the tags agree.

Meanwhile `delete-account`, filed with the same weight as the others, carries **zero** catalogue keys — it normalizes a tag and the worker renders it — so the translation work was three files, not four. Two counts were off: watchOS is 56 entries × **five** explicit locales plus an implicit `en` (281 localizations, not 336 — the source string is the key in 55 of 56 entries), and `knownRegions` holds **seven** rows, because Xcode's `Base` marker sits among the locales and the derived guard has to skip it by name. **The unwired parity script was exactly as filed — and running it found nothing hiding**: green on `3204ff84d`, exit 0, so the wiring was owed for the future rather than covering a live defect. Three test files asserted the defect (the filing named one); all three now pin the rule, plus the three unshipped European regions nothing had covered. Each surface gained a guard proved to trip by deleting a row, including a cross-tier one: `delete-account` renders nothing itself, so its test parses the worker's Go catalogue and fails when the receipt can name a locale the worker has no copy for. `check_xcstrings_parity.sh` now runs in a Linux `watch-ios-locale-parity` job. The possessive half of the filing was exact — **335 singular `seu`/`sua` as filed, 458 counting the plurals, zero of the `teu` family** — so this was a consistency repair and not a native-speaker call, as § 782 argued. **The residue was 42 strings, not 15**, and the shortfall was three axes nobody had counted rather than one undercounted: § 782's marker list was built from what `app_pt.arb` happened to contain, so it missed `usas`, `digitas`, `corres`, `participas` and `registares`; it had no **second-person preterite** at all (`Ganhaste`, `Completaste`, `confirmaste`, `selecionaste` — scanned here as an `-aste`/`-este`/`-iste` suffix with the nine non-verbs that end the same way named beside it); it never had to consider the **proclitic** `te` (4 strings, scanned in `pt-PT` only because Brazilian writes it beside `você`); and the **imperative** axis added 7 more (`Importa` ×3, `Ativa`, `descarta`, and `Corre`/`visualiza` in the coach block). **The `precisas` trap is live on web too** (`runDetail.hrDisclaimerSuffix`) **and there is a second the phone did not have**: `aceitas` is `aceitar` in the tu form and a feminine plural participle, live in both catalogues at `settingsAccount.aiConsentUpdatedToast`. Both are excluded at the list. **`pt-BR.ts` was fixed first, as this entry said it must be** — 13 strings, every one under `coachPage.*`, a consent disclosure reading in two registers — and doing so proved § 782's blindness claim rather than assuming it: four of those strings were byte-identical to pt-PT's, so the derived scan saw no difference until the Brazilian side moved, at which point it named three tu imperatives that had been invisible. **Reading the derived scan the other way found a defect nobody had filed**: four pt-PT strings ran the same rule backwards and turned a third-person indicative into a subjunctive — "ele **confirme** e passa a receber", "Isto **adicione** ou sobrescreve" — which is broken Portuguese rather than the wrong register. Two further closed classes fell out of the measurement and were fixed with their own derived guards (see the two boxes below for what was left). Not natively reviewed. Original filing below.

The phone's fix is the model: ~15 strings plus the two-test guard mirrored into `apps/web/src/lib/i18n/locale_reach.test.ts` beside its variant-vocabulary and sense-split siblings. **Note the derived half needs a `você` reference to measure against** — on the phone that is `app_pt_BR.arb`, and web's `pt-BR.ts` does *not* qualify unmodified: it carries **6 `teu`/`tua` in the `coachPage.consent*` block**, half-Europeanised strings that also kept `tiveres`, `digitas` and `corres` beside Brazilian `gênero` and `por padrão`. Fix that block first or the guard's second half is blind.

At least one is not defensible: `coachPage.consentPrivacyLink` is "Política de privacidade" where all six other catalogues are lowercase, and it renders mid-sentence inside "estão na nossa …". Settling this is a per-site pass over 248 strings, which is the native review below rather than a mechanical fix — a capital is right for a button and wrong for a clause, and only reading the call site says which.

- [x] **`padrão` means both *default* and *pattern*, and the European catalogues use it for the first.** **CLOSED 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 767](../architecture/decisions.md)). The judgement was right and every number was low. **47 occurrences, not 42** — web **26** across 25 lines (`onboarding.step5Hint` says it twice), the phone **21** — because the filed figures counted matching LINES in lower case; the two a case-sensitive grep walks past (`live.statTimer` / `liveSpectatorStatTimer`, `Cronômetro`) were missed by this round's manual pass too and named by the guard. The wrist and watchOS hold **zero**, so "do it with the wrist in the same pass" needed no wrist pass. **44 mean the pre-set value; three do not, and "at least two" was short by a whole third sense** — two are *pattern* (`loadRamp.meaning_high` / `loadRampMeaningHigh`) and `dash.prAgeGradeTitle` is "the world **standard**", which reads correctly as `padrão` in Portugal exactly as *pattern* does. Every site was classified against `en.ts` / `app_en.arb`; `predefinição` was chosen over `por omissão` because § 760 had already named it and a second vocabulary is worse than an imperfect one. Four strings carry pronouns bound to the now-feminine noun (`substituí-lo` → `substituí-la`), the gender class § 760 recorded for `tela` → `ecrã`. **The guard is the inverse of a deny-list**: `padrão` fails the tag-vs-content scan's own "a word the other variant does not use at all" test, which is why § 760 declined it — so the word is banned and the other-sense sites are named with their English source, and the allowlist fails loudly when a named key goes stale. **Reading the 47 found 30 more Brazilian forms**: the ô/ê-for-ó/é proparoxytones (`cronómetro`, `oxigénio`, `autónomo` — the class the deny-list already held `quilômetro` and `gênero` from, one word at a time), postposed `demais`, `planilha`, `todo mundo`, `liberar`, one `Pulou`, and a third shape of § 755's elision defect: `planNewRecent5kWarning` read "Até **si** confirmar", a stressed oblique standing as a subject, against a web twin that is correct. The wrist and watchOS catalogues had **no lexical guard at all** and now share the web one cross-tier. Not natively reviewed, and two things left for it deliberately — see the native-review item above. Original filing below.
  - **`padrão` means both *default* and *pattern*, and the European catalogues use it for the first.** Measured 2026-08-27 while deriving `app_pt.arb` ([decisions § 760](../architecture/decisions.md)) and left alone deliberately. European Portuguese prefers `predefinição` / `predefinido` where Brazilian says `padrão`, and the phone says `padrão` **20 times**, web **22** — but `padrão` is a less-preferred synonym rather than a Brazilianism, so it is not something the tag-vs-content guard can or should catch, and **one of the phone's twenty means *pattern*** (`loadRampMeaningHigh`, "o padrão mais associado a lesões"), as does at least one of web's. A blanket rule breaks those; converting them wants reading each of the 42 call sites. Doing it needs the wrist's `values-b+pt+PT` in the same pass, and it wants the native review above rather than a second rule. Low value on its own — a Lisbon reader understands `padrão` — so it is worth folding into that review, not a round of its own.
- [ ] **The eight Learn guides have no pt-PT prose.** Same round. `getGuide` now falls back within the reader's language before English ([decisions § 755](../architecture/decisions.md)), so a Lisbon reader is served the Brazilian guide silently and correctly rather than being dropped to English — the absence costs nothing today. Authoring them is 8 long-form MDsvex articles, prose rather than UI strings, and `locale_reach.test.ts` already accepts a `<slug>.pt-PT.md` the moment one lands.

## Email / lifecycle

The notification-email, welcome, Pro-receipt/dunning, and server-side web-push legs all ship (see archive). Remaining:

- [ ] **Native push delivery (FCM/APNs leg) — operator credentials only.** Native push to a locked phone. **All of the code ships** (2026-06-19, migration `20270212_001`): the FCM HTTP v1 + APNs HTTP/2 sender (`apps/job_worker/internal/nativepush/`), the `native_push` handler dispatched from `worker.go`, the device-token-gated enqueue trigger + `clear_device_token` RPC, and the mobile `firebase_messaging` registration (`push_messaging_bridge.dart`, both twins) writing through `ApiClient.registerDeviceToken`. This entry previously read "no `firebase_messaging` wiring exists" and named a `user_devices.push_token` column that has never existed — corrected 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 756](../architecture/decisions.md)); the table is `device_tokens`. What remains is operator-only: a Firebase project, `google-services.json` / `GoogleService-Info.plist`, an APNs auth key, and `FCM_*` / `APNS_*` on the worker. Unset is fail-closed — `handler_native_push.go` leaves the row pending **without stamping it**, so a later credentialed deploy delivers the backlog. Also unblocks safety-contact finish alerts to a locked phone.
- [x] **Account-deletion receipt** — SHIPPED 2026-06-20 (migration `20270217_001`, `account_deleted` template). Built as enqueue-with-inline-address + a non-cascading send-once record (not a second SMTP transport in the EF): `delete-account` captures `user.email` + locale before the cascade, enqueues a `lifecycle_email` job carrying `{email, locale}` and **no `user_id`** AFTER `admin.deleteUser` succeeds (so the `payload->>user_id` drain leaves it untouched), and the worker dedups on a hash of the address via the non-cascading `account_deletion_receipts` table. GDPR Art 17 confirmation mail — no per-feature compliance gate; goes live with the shared `SMTP_HOST` worker gate (see `docs/features/email.md` § Production ops) and a CISO eyeball as a pre-prod deploy check, no new credential. `docs/features/email.md`, `decisions.md § 121`.
- [ ] **Security emails (password-changed, new-device sign-in)** — blocked on absent infrastructure: no GoTrue auth hooks configured and no sign-in/device tracking (`device_tokens` now has a write path — `ApiClient.registerDeviceToken` — but it records push tokens, not sign-ins, so it is not the device/session history this needs). Needs a password-change auth hook + a device/session table with new-device detection. `decisions.md § 121`.
- [x] **Weekly digest + lifecycle drip (engagement email)** — BUILT (digest foundation `20270108_001` + scheduler `20270220_001`; drip `20270223_001` + first-week template `20270331_001`; decisions §174 + §177). Shipped as dedicated `weekly_digest` / `lifecycle_drip` job kinds (not extra `lifecycle_email` templates — §177 records why): per-stream opt-IN prefs (`email_weekly_digest`, `email_lifecycle_drip`, both default off, toggles on web + mobile Preferences), stream-aware RFC 8058 one-click unsubscribe (`/unsubscribe/{weekly-digest,lifecycle-drip}` off one HMAC secret), the `email_suppressions` hard-block + bounce/complaint webhook (`POST /v1/email/bounce`), four localized drip templates (`drip_onboarding`, `drip_first_week`, `drip_reengagement`, `drip_streak`) with cohort selection in SQL (`enqueue_lifecycle_drip()` daily pg_cron). **Send is the fail-closed gate, not the code**: nothing mails until an operator provisions SMTP + the unsub/bounce secrets AND the CISO/counsel sign-off lands (bulk mail under CAN-SPAM + GDPR/ePrivacy) — pre-deploy checklist in `docs/features/email.md § Production ops` items 4-5.
- [x] **Data-export-ready notification (email or native push)** — SHIPPED 2026-08-25 ([decisions § 729](../architecture/decisions.md), migration `20270607_001`). Built as ONE `notifications` row of a new `data_export_ready` kind, so the three existing transports (`notification_email` / `web_push` / `native_push`) pick it up as siblings with no new rail. **The staleness objection is answered by read-time URL minting, not waived**: the message links to `/settings/account` and carries no URL and no expiry of its own, because § 717 stores the object PATH and signs when the subject asks. Idempotency is one statement — `notify_data_export_ready()` inserts the inbox row and stamps `data_export_jobs.notified_at` under a row lock — which makes a redelivery silent AND lets the handler's already-built early return re-ask, repairing a crash between the finish write and the announcement; a failed announcement never fails the export. The pref is an opt-OUT per-kind mute (`notify_data_export_ready`, default on, web Settings → Preferences) that can only ever subtract from the channel modes. Mobile renders the inbox row and routes the tap to Settings → Account; the mute toggle is web-only per § 24. Goes live on the shared `SMTP_HOST` worker gate, no new credential. Original filing below.
  - The precondition this entry ruled itself out on now holds. It read "NOT planned: the export endpoint is synchronous and returns a 10-minute signed URL inline, so an async 'your export is ready' email would arrive stale. Revisit only if export moves to an async/job model." The export IS an async job model since [decisions § 717](../architecture/decisions.md), and since [§ 724](../architecture/decisions.md) it is the only rail on either client — and the staleness objection is answered by the same design that closed it: the signed URL is minted when the subject asks, not when the worker finishes, so a message can carry a LINK TO THE PAGE rather than a URL with a clock already running on it. Both clients currently make the weaker, true promise instead ("you can close this — the download will be waiting"), which is honest but means a runner with a deep history has to remember to come back. The work is a `data_export` completion hook on `FinishDataExportJob` fanning out an existing job kind (`notification_email` or `native_push`), an opt-in pref, the i18n catalogue entry, and the same suppression/unsubscribe rails every other stream rides. Mobile deliberately does **not** poll in the background for this (§ 724: iOS background refresh is opportunistic, so a client that promised to notice would be promising what the OS does not allow), which is exactly why the notification is the right shape rather than a client-side fix.
