## Feature-scale / product-gated

- [x] **In-app "Send a route to a follower" (targeted DM send)** — **v1 SHIPPED on web 2026-08-25** ([decisions § 734](../architecture/decisions.md), [route_direct_share.md](../features/route_direct_share.md)). A "Send to a follower" button beside Share on `/routes/[id]` opens `SendRouteDialog.svelte` and sends the public `/share/route/[id]` URL as a plain `direct_messages` body through the existing `sendDm` — no migration, no column, no new RLS. **The picker is the DM-*eligible* set, not the follower list** (`social/dm_recipients.ts`): the INSERT policy admits a follow edge in EITHER direction, so a followers-only picker would hide people the send accepts and an open search would offer sends RLS refuses; the block half is deliberately not mirrored client-side because `user_blocks` is owner-read only and a "can't be messaged" marker would leak the block. Privacy widens nothing the copy-link share didn't — the body is a link whose track still resolves through `clip_route_for_viewer`, and a private route routes through the SAME `share-confirm-dialog` public-flip confirm, cancel leaving it private and the picker closed. Pinned by `tests-e2e/routes/send-to-follower.spec.ts` (5 specs) + 13 unit tests. **v2 CLOSED 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 772](../architecture/decisions.md)), and the filing was wrong in both halves. "A raw URL" undersold it: the thread rendered `{m.body}` as escaped text with no linkification anywhere on the surface, so the recipient received an **unclickable** string to copy by hand. And `TrackPreview` is the one route renderer with no clip in it — `security_guards.test.ts` already pins three surfaces OFF it for exactly that reason, because a bare `<TrackPreview points={route.waypoints} />` was the pre-audit shape that leaked non-owner polylines (§ 33); a DM thread is a non-owner surface by construction, so built literally the filing is a privacy regression. Shipped instead: migration `20270619_001` adds a nullable `route_id` FK (the plain FK the spec's own v2 section preferred over a jsonb bag, `on delete set null` so a tidied-up route cannot delete someone's correspondence) with the INSERT policy gated on `private.is_route_visible_to` — sender-side, because a recipient-side check would accept or refuse the same insert depending on who it is addressed to. `DmRouteAttachment.svelte` resolves through the owner-aware `fetchRouteById` (`public_routes` + `clip_route_for_viewer` for a non-owner) and only then draws the trace, pinned by two new guards. The body **keeps** the share URL — it is the disclosure `routeDetail.sendDm.linkNote` makes, and `dm_threads()` and the Art 20 export both read the body and neither resolves an attachment — and is suppressed on screen only, and only when it points at this route's own path. 12 unit tests, 6 pgtap assertions, `tests-e2e/messages/dm-route-attachment.spec.ts`. **Still owed: the mobile leg**, which remains gated — re-verified 2026-08-28 that no mobile `/messages` twin exists (nothing under `apps/mobile_android/lib/` or `packages/` references `direct_messages`, `sendDm` or `DirectMessage`; the only filename matches are `ble_treadmill.dart` and `rate_limit_message.dart`), so mobile stays on the Share link → OS share sheet and `dm_attachment.ts` is owed no Dart twin.
- [x] **Rate-limit the `direct_messages` rail** — CLOSED 2026-08-25 ([decisions § 737](../architecture/decisions.md)). Migration `20270608_001` puts a `before insert` trigger on the table itself, debiting **two** buckets through the existing `enforce_create_rate_limit` helper: `send_direct_message_burst` at 30/60 s and `send_direct_message` at 250/3600 s. Two windows because `check_rate_limit` is fixed-window, so one "N per hour" either admits the whole allowance in a single instant or trips a real back-and-forth; the burst bucket bounds how *fast* messages arrive, the hour bucket how *many*, and together they cap a flood at 250 messages over at least ~8 minutes instead of 250 in two seconds. Table-level, so `/messages`, the § 734 route-send dialog and every future affordance are covered by one mechanism — verified by a sweep confirming `direct_messages` has exactly one write path (a PostgREST INSERT; no RPC, no Edge Function, no Go worker). **Three things the filed entry had wrong or unstated.** The bucket name it proposed, `'direct-messages'`, cannot be matched by the `rate_limit_errors` pair's `(\w+)` regex, so a hyphenated bucket would have surfaced the raw postgres string at the sender — the buckets are snake_case for that reason. `check_rate_limit`'s documented "denied calls still count" property is **false through a trigger**: the raise aborts the statement and rolls the increment back with it (measured — a refused send left the burst bucket on 30, not 31), so on this rail the counter counts *successful* sends. And the sender saw nothing comprehensible before this: `sendDm` re-threw the `PostgrestError` verbatim for anything but a 42501, so the P0001 now goes through `rateLimitErrorMessage` after the follow-graph branch, with a source guard pinning that order. New pgtap `direct_message_rate_limit_test.sql` (9 tests: burst admitted, burst refused in the client-parser format, nothing written, a batched multi-row insert refused per row, per-sender isolation, the hour bucket refusing on its own, a forged sender still 42501 rather than mis-reported as a throttle, and the roll-back-on-refusal accounting) plus a Playwright spec pinning the composer's alert and the surviving draft.
- [ ] **Strava community segment import (~2-3 wk)** — Strava's segment API requires per-segment OAuth scopes we don't request, and segment-leaderboard data has Strava ToS redistribution limits. Needs a legal + API-scope decision before building.
- [ ] **Family / household Pro tier (~1 wk + pricing decision)** — a household plan (shared subscription across linked accounts) is a pricing/product decision (RevenueCat entitlement model + a household link table), not a code-only change.
- [ ] **Segment-KOM "crowns" in the recap (deferred)** — `segment_efforts` has no stored rank, so a per-segment global-min aggregation across all users would be needed — heavier than a recap card warrants. Personal records stand in as the achievement metric for now.
- [ ] **Auto-follow on club join — product decision (decided: NOT a silent fan-out)** — `join_club_by_token` deliberately does not create `user_follows` edges; club invite tokens are generic so there's no specific person to follow, and auto-following every member is presumptuous + a consent concern. The right shape, if wanted, is an opt-in "Follow members" suggestion list on the club page — tracked here so it isn't re-raised as a silent trigger.
- [x] **A run's segment rank and the segment's leaderboard rank are computed from different populations** — CLOSED 2026-08-19 ([decisions § 696](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The server half had already shipped when this entry was written** and the box was never ticked: `20270523_001` (2026-08-13, [§ 594](../architecture/decisions.md)) gave both `segment_effort_ranks` and `global_segment_effort_ranks` the per-athlete population — counted as distinct strictly-faster rival athletes, which IS the boards' `distinct on (se.user_id)` reduction — plus the block filter and a self-exclusion, pinned by `segment_effort_ranks_per_athlete_test.sql` (8 tests, including the A 60+65 / B 70 → B #2 shape asserted against the board itself) and `global_segment_effort_ranks_per_athlete_test.sql`. So no new migration was needed, and two claims in this entry were wrong: the visibility predicates were **not** divergent — the board's `private.is_run_visible_to` is verbatim the `segment_efforts` SELECT policy (`20260812_001`) and route visibility arrives through the segments EXISTS, so SECURITY INVOKER supplies both; and the block filter, the one predicate RLS cannot carry, was added by `20270523_001` too.
  What the re-audit did find is a **third** population on the phone: `ApiClient.fetchEffortsForRunWithSegments` never moved to the RPC and still counted strictly-faster effort ROWS client-side, so the fixed defect kept shipping to mobile users — and contradicted mobile's own `segment_leaderboard_tiered` board. Now on the RPC (which also retires an N+1), pinned by `packages/api_client/test/segment_effort_ranks_source_test.dart`. The two stale client/test contract comments the entry flagged are corrected. Remaining, deliberately: the boards inner-join `user_profiles` where the chip does not (unreachable — the Art 8 consent gate makes a profile row a precondition of owning a run — and dropping the rival would restate every slower athlete's rank, which § 596 refused on the same grounds), and the demographic tiers / `p_limit` / the anon caller stay unmirrored. Noted while there: both clients degrade a missing rank row to **#1**, so an RPC failure paints a false crown — the RPC is written to always return a row precisely because of it, but the degrade itself deserves its own round. **CLOSED 2026-08-26** ([decisions § 746](../architecture/decisions.md)) — and the premise it rested on was wrong twice over. There were **four** call sites, not three: `core/data.ts` carries the degrade in `fetchGlobalEffortsForRun` as well as in `fetchEffortsForRunWithError`. And the RPC does **not** always return a row: `20270523_001` added the block filter as `not is_blocked_either_way(auth.uid(), …)` inside two SECURITY INVOKER bodies whose calling role may be `anon`, whose EXECUTE on that function `20261108_001` had revoked — so since 2026-08-13 an anonymous reader of a public run was admitted into the body and 42501'd inside it, and only once the rival subquery yielded a row. The RPC therefore succeeded exactly when the crown was real and failed exactly when it was not. Rank is now nullable end to end on both platforms with a neutral placeholder; `20270609_001` routes both bodies through `private.viewer_blocks` and grants `anon` EXECUTE on the catalogue twin, which `20270411_001` had never granted at all.
- [x] **The segment catalogue is in no global nav slot and no sitemap** — DONE 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 677](../architecture/decisions.md)). `/segments` is the fifth tab on the shared `RunSurfaceTabs` strip (Runs · Routes · Segments · Plans · Races), the catalogue index is in `sitemap.xml`, and `/segments` joined `anonExtraExact` so the URL the manifest advertises answers with the catalogue rather than a login form (`global_segments` grants select to anon).
- [ ] **Make an individual `/segments/[id]` page indexable** — the catalogue INDEX is in the sitemap; the per-segment pages deliberately are not (§ 677): each is a leaderboard of named runners, the people-directory objection that keeps profiles out, and they are CSR, so a crawler gets the empty shell. Doing it properly is the step-2 path in [seo.md](../features/seo.md) § Adding a new indexable surface — a `share_segment_lookup` returning anon-safe metadata only (name, geometry, region — never a leaderboard row), a `share_segment_meta` builder, a `/share/segment/[id]` route, a `share-entity` dispatcher branch + CloudFront behavior, and the `share_log_groups` alarm registration. Needs the product call on whether a curated segment is public marketing surface at all before the Lambda work.
- [x] **Consent-flow consistency for health-data fields (legal-adjacent)** — CLOSED on the clients 2026-08-24 ([decisions § 718](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The filed premise was backwards.** Onboarding was the only surface applying a coherent rule; the two settings pages were aligned with neither it nor each other, and they wrote to *different stores*. `/settings/preferences` wrote the `user_profiles` column and never the prefs mirror, and **refused the save entirely** when a DOB was present without consent — so a minor who declined the Art 9 checkbox could not record a birth date at all and kept a NULL age record, the exact fail-open the floor exists to close. `/settings/account` wrote the mirror and never the column, and aborted every save on a DOB whose input the same condition disabled — a deadlock for anyone whose stored DOB outlived their consent. Mobile Preferences wrote the mirror **ungated** and never the column, while `nutrition_targets_screen.dart` and § 695 both claimed DOB was editable only behind the body-metrics consent gate, where there is no DOB field. The decision is now stated rather than implied: the column is the **age record** (child protection, no consent term, written by every entry point and re-asserted after the withdrawal RPC), the bag key is the **Art 9 health-use mirror** (written under consent, cleared on withdrawal, everywhere). Shipped web + mobile + iOS twin with guards on all four surfaces.
- [x] **`withdraw_health_data_consent()` still nulls the age record** — CLOSED 2026-08-24 ([§ 718](../architecture/decisions.md) open item 1, now [§ 721](../architecture/decisions.md)). Migration `20270605_001` is the bare-body `create or replace` dropping `date_of_birth` from the RPC's null list; `height_cm`, `gender` and the `body_metrics` series still go in the same transaction. Both generators re-ran with no diff, confirming the function-only shape. `withdraw_health_data_consent_test.sql` splits into "nulled the consent stamp + height + gender" plus "kept the age record the under-18 floor reads" (10 tests → 11), and the compensation § 718 added on both platforms is gone: web's two settings pages no longer order their profile write after the RPC to undo it, and the phone's body-metrics screen no longer loads the DOB just to write it back (a new withdrawal test pins that it doesn't). Lawfulness is stated in § 721 — the surviving column does child-protection work on a different lawful basis, and Art 17 erasure still clears it through `delete-account`'s `auth.users` cascade.
- [x] **Client surfaces derive age from the ungated DOB column** — CLOSED web + mobile + iOS twin 2026-08-24 ([decisions § 722](../architecture/decisions.md), [§ 718](../architecture/decisions.md) open item 3). **The filed count and the mitigation were both wrong.** Seven sites were named; the sweep found nine reads of the age record across the two platforms, of which **four** were already fail-closed for a second reason, **two** were genuine defects, and **three** were never on the column at all. Already fail-closed: web `dashboard/+page.svelte`'s PR age-grade and mobile `dashboard_screen.dart`'s (both need `gender`, which `ageGradeForRun` refuses without and which `withdraw_health_data_consent()` nulls), and web `nutrition/+page.svelte` + mobile `nutrition_screen.dart` (both need `height_cm` and the `body_metrics` weight series, nulled and erased by the same RPC). Genuine: web `PlanEditor.svelte` ↔ mobile `training_service.dart`'s `fetchViewerAge`, where age ALONE reshapes the generated plan through the masters recovery calibration (50+ → wider hard-day spacing + a 3-week build/recover cycle), and the nutrition-targets metrics card on both platforms, which rendered the runner's age outright on a health surface. Never on the column: web `runs/[id]`, mobile `run_detail_screen.dart` and `intensity_card.dart` all read the settings-bag mirror, which already follows consent. **The filed mitigation — "the open-category age-grade on `/dashboard` is the only claim age alone still produces" — is false in both halves**: `ageGradeForRun` has no open category and returns null without a binary sex, and the claim age alone actually produced was a differently-shaped training plan. Fixed with a `health_consent` parity pair (`healthUseDob` / `healthUseDobState`) rather than seven remembered checks, plus a source guard on each platform. Two things the sweep turned up beyond the filed scope: `training_service`'s `fetchViewerAge` AND `fetchViewerGender` both went through a direct column select the lockdown refuses with 42501, so neither calibration has ever fired on the phone — both now share one `get_my_profile()` read; and the metrics card now says "Needs health-data consent" rather than "Not set" about a date the runner did set.
- [x] **The age-banded segment leaderboards read DOB with no consent term** — CLOSED 2026-08-25 ([§ 718](../architecture/decisions.md) open item 2, now [§ 727](../architecture/decisions.md)). Migration `20270606_001` puts both boards' age band behind `health_data_consent_at`, at **two** sites per function: the band-membership filter inside `best_per_athlete` and the caller's own `age` echo in the outer select. An unconsented runner falls out of the age tier entirely rather than into a wrong one — the same outcome the adjacent `date_of_birth is not null` branch already produced for a runner with no date, and the suite pins the two as indistinguishable — while staying untouched on the unfiltered all-comers board. Gender tiers gained no term and needed none: `gender` is only ever populated under consent. **Two things the filed item had wrong.** The catalogue RPC is `global_segment_leaderboard`, not `global_segment_leaderboard_tiered`. And both bodies it named (`20270424000003` / `20270513_001`) had been superseded by `20270524_001`, the §206 shadow-hidden definer backstop — a bare-body `create or replace` built on the text the item pointed at would have silently reverted that fix, so the migration re-emits `20270524_001`'s bodies with the consent term as the only edit (diffed to confirm). New pgtap suite `segment_leaderboard_age_band_consent_test.sql` (10 tests, both boards) plus a `health_data_consent_at` stamp on the DOB-carrying fixtures in `segment_leaderboard_tiered_test.sql`, whose age bands would otherwise have gone dark. Both generators re-ran with a zero diff, confirming the function-only shape.

## Compliance — DSAR export coverage (GDPR Art 20)

- [x] **DSAR export omits four personal-data tables — and the guard that should catch this is blind to them.** (Closed 2026-06-20.) `session_plans` (`author_id`), `event_orders` (`buyer_user_id`/`host_user_id`), `route_photos` (`owner_id`), and `event_pricing` (host data) were missing from **both** export paths — the Go worker `exportPersonalDataSpecs` (`apps/job_worker/internal/supabase.go`) and the `export-data` EF `buildBackupSpecs` (`apps/backend/supabase/functions/export-data/backup_spec.ts`). Erasure was always fine (all four cascade-delete from `auth.users`, so Art 17 held); the gap was the Art 20 **export**. **Durable fix shipped:** the export-completeness guard (`apps/job_worker/internal/personal_data_export_guard_test.go`) no longer keys on a literal `user_id` column — it now flags any table with *any* owner-style FK to `auth.users` (`user_id` / `author_id` / `owner_id` / `buyer_user_id` / `host_user_id` / `contact_user_id`) that isn't in the export specs or the reasoned-exclusion list (the `user_id` form by name; the other owner names only when the column declaration carries an inline `references auth.users`, so a non-user `owner_id` can't false-positive). All four tables are wired into both export paths (`event_orders` × 2 legs, `event_pricing` via embedded `events!inner` host-id join), the Deno export test asserts each, and widening the guard surfaced five more pre-existing plain-`user_id` gaps now wired in too (`achievements`, `challenge_participants`, `challenge_badges`, `public_recaps`, `route_conditions`). Documented in [`docs/compliance/data-subject-rights.md`](../compliance/data-subject-rights.md).

From `roadmap.md § Competitor-parity backlog`; sizes are rough estimates carried from the roadmap table:

- [ ] **Heatmap / popular-route discovery** (#4, ~2 wk) — materialised tile table or a Go tile service; anonymised aggregation. Open decision: opt-in vs opt-out privacy default.
- [ ] **Trail / offline navigation** (#5, ~3-4 wk) — turn-by-turn on a loaded route + offline tile packs + condition reports. Needs a routing-engine choice (Valhalla vs GraphHopper).
- [x] **Audio-coached runs** (#9) — **the TTS half is now genuinely shipped; closed 2026-08-30** ([decisions § 801](../architecture/decisions.md) + [§ 802](../architecture/decisions.md)). The entry as filed had the scope right and the status wrong in both directions. The pre-recorded-voice half was not blocked, it was **descoped** — roadmap row 9 resolved it to "TTS-only v1, no audio CDN" in 2026-06, so no audio CDN strategy or voice-talent budget was ever owed. And the TTS half that row called shipped **was not built**: `cuesDue` had no production caller on either platform, `run_screen.dart` contained zero occurrences of "guided", the only callers of `speakGuidedCue` were the library's preview button and an unrelated gym timer, and `guided_runs_screen.dart`'s own doc comment called the L0–L4 integration a follow-up. Both surfaces sat at browse-and-preview; nobody could run a guided run. **What landed:** the arm-from-idle affordance + the L4 tick dispatch, its own `guided_run` voice-cue id across all four rails, the owner-only `runs.metadata.guided_run_id` stamp, and the first product-driven `SET1` push to the custom watch. **Three defects found beneath the filing**, each recorded where it belongs: `speakGuidedCue` interrupts (`_tts.stop()`), which `run_recording.md` row 18 names as the deliberate exception for the *preview* — wiring the recorder to it would have truncated a split or off-route **safety** cue mid-word on both platforms, so the recording path got its own queueing `announceGuidedCue`; the `guided_runs.ts` ↔ `guided_runs.dart` pair asserted in both doc comments that the shared-library-syncer watched it while being registered in **neither** registry (§ 801); and `runs.metadata.guided_run_id` was first classified public-safe, which `public_runs`' denylist projection would have published to anonymous readers as a legible `first-timer-15` beginner label (§ 802).
- [ ] **Pre-recorded voice talent for guided runs** — reviewed 2026-08-30 and deliberately left open. The remaining half of #9 is a budget and vendor question (voice talent, an audio CDN, per-locale re-recording across seven catalogues), not a keyboard-ready item; v1 is TTS by decision, not by omission.
- [x] **No handoff from the Coach tab's guided-run detail screen to the recorder.** — **CLOSED 2026-08-30** ([decisions § 805](../architecture/decisions.md)). `Use this run` on `GuidedRunDetailScreen` parks the library id in a `pendingArmGuidedRun` notifier beside `pendingStartWorkout` and pops to the shell; the Run tab arms it through the same `_armGuidedRun` the picker uses, so the watch push, cue-cursor reset and metadata stamp are single-sourced. The estimate held (~40 lines, 4 files). One deliberate divergence from the pattern it copied, and the reason is a latent defect in that pattern: `pendingStartWorkout` PARKS its value when the screen is busy, which for a `String` id would make a second tap on the same run a silent no-op, since a `ValueNotifier` does not fire on an unchanged value.
- [x] **`runs.metadata.guided_run_id` has no reader.** — **CLOSED 2026-08-30** ([decisions § 805](../architecture/decisions.md)). Both platforms now name the script a run was recorded under, resolved through the library for the active locale and never shown as the stored id: a chip in `/runs/[id]`'s provenance row on web, `_buildGuidedRun` on `run_detail_screen.dart` on mobile. An id the shipped library no longer answers to keeps the claim and drops the name rather than self-hiding — the run WAS guided — and both suites pin that the slug never reaches a reader. The projection stays owner-only; a public share-page reader would still be a deliberate re-classification.
- [ ] **`WatchSettings` still has no non-dev product push besides the guided-run arm.** Reviewed 2026-08-30 and deliberately left open: a settings-push surface is its own feature (max HR, pacer, gear, ICE), the device is research-tier with no unit in anyone's hands ([§ 71](../architecture/decisions.md)), and nothing in the app is gated on it. Not a residue of the guided-run work.
- [x] **Race calendar + results import** (#10) — **the client half is closed 2026-08-30** ([decisions § 806](../architecture/decisions.md)); the entry's stated blocker was wrong. "Gated on the RunSignUp key" described neither of the two things actually missing. The `race-results-import` function has had four built legs since 2026-06, and **both clients' import UI branched on `runsignup` alone** — so a ChronoTrack or UltraSignup listing fell through to manual paste, and provisioning `CHRONOTRACK_*` would have lit up a Settings card linking to a page that could not reach the leg it advertised. **UltraSignup did not exist on the phone at all**: absent from the provider union, no probe, no tile, zero ARB keys, against a full web surface in seven catalogues — a straight § 24 inversion. Both platforms now render every leg from one provider catalogue, each with its own scope field and its own unavailable explainer, on both the calendar sheet and the run-detail auto-match prompt (which carried the same defect on a second surface), guarded against the Edge Function's own dispatch set. **Two defects found beneath the filing:** the UltraSignup leg had no pre-fetch scope gate where both its siblings did, and fell back to reading the listing's `provider_race_id` — a RACE id by name and by every other provider's use — as an athlete account, which would have pulled a race's finishers and stamped the caller's `user_id` onto one of them; and the `RACE_IMPORT_UNAVAILABLE` fall-through gave ChronoTrack RunSignUp's error string.
- [ ] **`race-listings-sync` is a stub, so no provider race ever enters the calendar** — the real remaining work on #10, and **not** a credential gate. It validates its credentials and returns `{ synced: 0 }`; the upcoming-races fetch + upsert is unwritten (the function's own comment has said so since it shipped). Fully provisioned, the calendar would still hold only parkrun, crowd submissions and hand inserts. Deliberately not written speculatively on 2026-08-30: the response shape cannot be observed without a key, and guessing it is the same fragility the per-site scrapers were deferred for. Needs one provider's credentials in hand, then the parser is small.
- [ ] **`isProviderConfigured` fails open on anything that is not a `FunctionException`** — offline, or Supabase not initialised, both read as configured. Inherited verbatim from the `isRunSignUpConfigured` it replaced and preserved deliberately rather than changed under a refactor, but it means the screen-level degrade-to-unavailable catch almost never fires. Worth a decision: fail open (a probe failure should not hide a working provider) or fail closed (the house default). Same question on web.
- [ ] **Advanced analytics polish** (#11, ~2 wk) — no new tables; richer dashboard breakdowns + race-time predictor over what VDOT / training-load already ship. **Race-time predictor shipped** (2026-06-20): the multi-distance `race_predictor` TS↔Dart parity pair (`predictRaceLadder` — recency-weighted Riegel anchor, per-rung confidence reusing `predictionConfidence`) + the `RacePredictorCard` on web `/dashboard` AND the mobile `widgets/race_predictor_card.dart` on the Home dashboard (5K/10K/Half/Marathon ladder, self-hiding on the qualifying-run gate, both platforms). **Consistency card shipped** (2026-07-11): the web-only `training/consistency.ts` pure helper (`computeConsistency` — active weeks out of the last 12, current + best active-week streak with an in-progress-week grace, and a steady/variable weekly-volume CoV) + the self-hiding `ConsistencyCard` on web `/dashboard` (< 2 active weeks → hidden), scoped to `filteredRuns` so the source/activity-type filter carries through. 16 unit tests + a Playwright surface test. Web-only (no Dart twin, deliberately off the parity list). Remaining ideas: easy/hard intensity distribution, week/month trend deltas.
- [x] **Dashboard best-streak sub-label is windowed, not all-time.** Closed 2026-08-03 ([decisions § 471](../architecture/decisions.md)) — the last surviving half of the #332 follow-up (§ 470 closed the PeriodSummary drilldown). The `run_streaks_for_user(p_tz, p_source)` gaps-and-islands aggregate (migration `20270501_001`) serves `(current, best)` in one row on the paint path, bucketed by the runner's **local** day (client passes its IANA zone, matching `computeRunStreaks` rather than the awarder's UTC shortcut) and taking the dashboard's source filter so the sub-label's all-time claim stays true under a filter. Web `fetchRunStreaks` returns null — never zeros — on failure, and `streak_card.ts#streakCardState` then suppresses the numeric best claim instead of presenting the windowed number as all-time. 12 pgtap tests (pre-window island, 23:30-local/UTC-date edge, grace day, source filter, owner scoping) + 8 helper unit tests + source guards. **Mobile closed 2026-08-03 ([decisions § 475](../architecture/decisions.md))** — the § 471 note's open store-population question resolved the other way: the dashboard card reads the SAME RPC (`ApiClient.fetchRunStreaks`, device IANA zone via `flutter_timezone`, explicit `'UTC'` degrade) through the new `streak_card.ts` ↔ `streak_card.dart` parity pair, with a mobile-only pointwise-max fold so an unsynced local run is never walked back; no full-history backfill onto the device.
- [ ] **Premium billing extensions** (#12, ~1-2 wk) — Stripe Checkout + customer portal + paywall enforcement across web + mobile.
- [x] **Treadmill BLE FTMS — live run-screen wiring** (#13, mobile-only) — **the code all shipped**; this entry was stale. The parser, model, pairing tile and `RunRecorder.setTreadmillSample` seam were already in; the run-screen wiring landed 2026-06-19 ([treadmill_live_mode.md](../features/treadmill_live_mode.md)) — `_toggleTreadmillMode` in `run_screen.dart` (paired-belt-gated `SwitchListTile`, own `_treadmillSub` / `_treadmillStatusSub`, L4 try/catch + banner-revert) against the app-owned `BleTreadmill` singleton threaded `main.dart` → `HomeScreen` → `RunScreen` and → `TreadmillTile`. `parity.md`, `roadmap.md` item 13 and `integrations.md` were all flipped at landing; only this line was not. **Closed 2026-08-21** after an audit that found the toggle was not honest about the belt's state and closed the gap ([decisions § 704](../architecture/decisions.md)): the subtitle now switches on `BleTreadmillStatus`, `connected` is the only arm that can format a speed, and the `connecting` arm gained the `setState` it never had. 12 widget tests now drive the real screen through the real toggle + status listener via the `BleTreadmill` emit seams (the five states, the subscribe/unsubscribe lifecycle across start/pause/resume/stop, a mid-run disconnect leaving L0/L1 intact, the connect-failed path, the sample-fault guard) — the three that existed before replicated the screen's wiring in unit tests rather than driving it, so a screen-side regression could not fail them. **Still owed: hardware-in-the-loop verification against a real FTMS belt** — a device gate on the FTMS decode + reconnect behaviour of actual hardware, not unwritten code. Nothing in the app is gated on it; the belt is opt-in and off by default.
- [x] **Route-design preferences (scenic / cul-de-sac / multi-objective ranking)** — **closed 2026-08-30** ([decisions §§ 795-798](../architecture/decisions.md)). Three of the filing's four remaining items shipped; the fourth was blocked on data, not on code, and the filing did not name the defect underneath any of them. **The defect:** `handleGenerate` skipped the graph-cycle sidecar entirely whenever a preference was set (`config.graphCycleUrl && !req.preference`), so ticking "Quiet roads" silently moved the runner OFF the durable v3 graph search onto a custom-model `round_trip` — the preference was in practice a switch trading route quality for road class. It had to be that way, because the sidecar's `decodeBody` uses `DisallowUnknownFields` and a request carrying `preference` got a 400. **What shipped:** the sidecar retains a packed per-edge attribution byte (road-class bucket + a green flag rasterised from `leisure=park` / `natural=water` / `landuse=forest,grass` ways onto the spatial index's own 200 m cells) where `build.go` previously discarded every way tag — unbuilt prerequisite work the filing did not name, since there was nothing to weight; soft preference multipliers on the search (never 0, never an edge removal, so a hard filter cannot disconnect a buildable neighbourhood) with a never-deny unweighted retry; opt-in cul-de-sac mode splicing capped structural dead-end stubs (4 / 250 m one way / 20 % of target, only onto a loop running short) and crediting them at a weight sized to cancel the isoperimetric penalty they incur; and one multi-objective score replacing the two-branch selection, pinned against a verbatim copy of the old selector over 800,000 random candidate sets spanning the whole accepted target range — which caught a real divergence, a 1e-9 tolerance on the leading objective that made the old comparator **non-transitive**, so no scalar score could reproduce it until the objective was snapped to that same grid. **The first draft of that score was itself a live regression in the default generator** and its pin could not see it, because the corpus stopped at a 4.5 km target and the failure began there; the review that caught it is recorded in [§ 795](../architecture/decisions.md). The preference now rides the chain (sidecar first carrying it, `round_trip` under a custom model only as the fallback it already was), and both the 200 body and the sidecar report `preferenceApplied` from the **serving engine's verdict, never echoed from the ask** — every path can serve a loop that ignores the preference, so a client repeating the ask would tell the runner their quiet route was quiet on no evidence. `/routes/new`'s checkbox became a four-option native radio group (the values are mutually exclusive on the wire), which reports when an ask was not honoured; 7 i18n keys × 7 catalogues; and the AI route assistant extracts `preference` as a whitelisted vocabulary beside `shape`/`surface`. **A second unfiled defect, found twice independently:** the assistant listed "Avoid main roads." in its "Filled in from your request" summary while `handleNlRequest` never set `quietRoads` — the summary claimed a constraint the generate request did not carry. **Deferred, for want of data rather than code:** elevation-aware. GraphHopper boots with no `graph.elevation.provider` (`apps/job_worker/graphhopper/config.yml` lines 5, 13-15) and the sidecar's PBF parse keeps only `lat`/`lng` because an OSM extract carries no terrain, so a grade-weighted cost would have nothing behind it; `elevation` is absent from the wire vocabulary rather than accepted and ignored. Unblocking it costs **a DEM as a deploy input** (SRTM/Copernicus tiles on the sidecar's Fly volume beside the PBF, sampled per node at build time), which is a deploy-shaped change, not a code-shaped one. Web-only (route generation is web-canonical). Design in [graph_cycle_loop_generation.md § Extension](../features/graph_cycle_loop_generation.md).
- [ ] **Elevation-aware route-design preference — needs a DEM on the sidecar's deploy** — new 2026-08-30, split out of the entry above so the one blocked item is not buried inside a closed one. Not code: neither engine imports terrain. See § 795 for the sourced reason and the cost.

- [x] **Mobile mirror: session-derived Log-as-workout prefill on event detail** — done 2026-07-11. `event_detail_screen._logAsWorkout` now loads the attached session plan (`_maybeLoadSessionPlan`, best-effort L4) when a `class` event carries a `session_plan_id`, expands it via `expandSessionSteps`, and passes `seedSets` + `seedTitle` to `showGymComposeSheet` through the pure `logWorkoutSeed` helper (mirrors web's `logWorkoutPrefill`: discipline/plan title → flat title fallback, one seeded set per expanded step). Added `session_plan_id` to `_eventSelectCols` and carried `duration_s` through the composer's `seedSets` map so timed poses survive. Twin-mirrored; unit tests in `event_detail_screen_test.dart`.

- [x] **Mobile mirror: honour `show_calories` on run detail** — done 2026-07-11. `run_detail_screen`'s calorie tile now gates on `SettingsSyncService.service.effective<bool>('show_calories') != false` (default on), and Settings → Preferences → Units & Display carries a "Show calorie estimates" `SwitchListTile` writing the universal bag via `_putUniversal`. `SettingsKeys.showCalories` registered; ARB keys `prefsShowCalories`/`prefsShowCaloriesHint` in all six locales; widget tests in `run_detail_screen_test.dart` (tile hidden when false / shown by default) + `settings_show_calories_test.dart` (toggle write path); mirrored to the iOS twin.

## CI reliability

- [x] **`playwright install-deps` hard-failed a test shard when the runner's apt mirrors did not answer.** Five shard failures across two pushes on 2026-08-18 (round 4: shards 1, 7, 13; round 5: shards 6, 7), every one identical: three 120 s attempts, all timing out, then a hard fail, with no test ever run and a clean re-run every time. Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 627 amendment](../architecture/decisions.md)) by making the apt half **conditional**: the step only ever verified — every library it installs is already the newest version on the hosted image — so `./.github/actions/install-playwright` now verifies directly by **launching Chromium** (the same headless-shell binary `devices['Desktop Chrome']` drives, and strictly stronger than reading `ldd`, which cannot see what Chromium dlopens) and reaches for apt only when that launch fails. The verdict afterwards is another launch rather than apt's exit code, because install-deps can succeed with the launch still broken and can fail on a mirror having already installed what was missing. No retries were added. The check cannot pass vacuously: any launch failure for any reason sends the job to apt, and a launch that still fails there fails the step with the browser's own error naming the library, plus the mirror list.
- [x] **Two Renode sim flakes, neither of them the firmware — closed 2026-08-24 ([decisions § 716](../architecture/decisions.md)).** Both surfaced on the same afternoon on PRs touching no firmware (a Terraform lockfile bump, an npm dep bump) and both passed on re-run, so neither is the `pages` wedge below: that one was deterministic per binary and lived in the RTC model, these two live in the harness. **(1) `alerts` sampled a repaint.** It waited for `record: alert <Kind>` — the RECORD task's line, emitted *before* the alert is published to the composer — then dumped the panel and retried four times. The flush lands ~11 ms of firmware time later, so which side of it the dump arrives on is decided by the runner's timing: **3 of 8 identical local runs missed on the first attempt**, and a contended two-core hosted runner missed all four and reported "+340 ink" against the 500 an inverse-video band adds. The screen task now states its own post-flush condition (`ui: banner <Kind>` / `ui: banner none`, the same contract `ui: page` has carried since § 361), every dump waits for it and re-reads it once the frame is on disk, and the quiet baseline's lowest-of-three sampling is gone. Eight consecutive post-fix runs landed the banner on the first attempt. **(2) `bin/watch-sim.sh` failed silently.** Its EXIT trap ended `exit 0` unconditionally — hence "exited early with **status 0**", a failure detectable only by a missing side effect — and ran under `set -e`, so `pkill -P $$` returning 1 (nothing left to sweep) abandoned the rest of the teardown including the `wait` and the latest-run pointer. Both fixed at the trap, with SIGINT the one deliberate exception. The phone-port TOCTOU the old message speculated about is real and was reproduced by racing two sims: the fixed port 7788 is now **claimed** with a per-port `flock` held on a descriptor Renode inherits, the monitor port (previously a bare unchecked draw) likewise, and the 30 s pty deadline — short enough to fail a healthy cold boot — is now a 180 s failure bound whose message names the last `watch.resc stage:` marker reached instead of guessing. **Not reproduced locally:** all four attempts missing in one run; what was reproduced is the per-attempt coin flip it is made of.
- [ ] **14 fixed real-clock delays remain in the mobile widget suite, each with a stated reason; 44 were converted 2026-08-24 ([decisions § 723](../architecture/decisions.md)).** The round-11 estimate of "~50" measured 58 delay sites sitting next to `tester.runAsync`. Converting them was a classification exercise, not a rewrite, and the conversions were graded by temporarily instrumenting `pumpUntil` to print the iterations it actually spent: a predicate that never spins has converted nothing. Two findings are worth carrying forward. **A store's row list is not its write** — `OfflineSyncStore.persist` populates `rowsById` before `writeJsonAtomic` and notifies only once the row file and the index are both down, so a row-count predicate returns mid-write and lets an `addTearDown` temp-dir delete race it; wait on the store's `notifyListeners` (via `addListener`, no production seam) or on a UI state ordered after `persist()` returns. And **`pumpUntil` can never wait for a route transition, animation, or dialog dismissal** (it deliberately never advances the fake clock) and **cannot be called from inside a `tester.runAsync` block** (no nesting).
  **What is left, all deliberate.** *Models elapsed time (1):* the 11 s GPS blackout in `run_screen_recording_flow_test.dart:848`. *No condition that is ever false (7):* absence assertions in `nutrition_log_sheet_test.dart:351` (cancelled scan), `import_screen_paths_test.dart:153` (pick with no readable path), `checkpoint_checkin_screen_test.dart:181` (empty bib), `run_screen_test.dart:318` + `run_screen_recording_flow_test.dart:1247` (the treadmill pairing read, invisible at idle), plus `watch_screens_editor_screen_test.dart:81` and `plan_new_screen_test.dart:265`, which drain a mocked in-memory read the assertions then consult directly. *No observable outcome (6):* teardown drains at `gym_screen_test.dart:602` and `routes_screen_fab_double_tap_test.dart:96`; `watch_live_screen_test.dart:183`, which exists to let a stream-cancel finish; `roadbook_screen_test.dart:64` + `route_detail_watch_course_test.dart:326` (21 call sites over a stored-race-plan read that resolves to `null` in 13 of 14 tests, so it changes nothing to poll for); and `dashboard_streak_card_test.dart:98`, whose test bodies are themselves inside `runAsync`. **Do not add a test seam to production code to make any of these convertible.** The remaining live question is whether the two existing `pumpUntil` call sites at `nutrition_screen_test.dart` that wait on `f.store.rows.length == 2` should move to the `persisted` probe — they are the same before-the-write proxy, in a file whose `finally` deletes the temp dir.
- [x] **The `pages` sim scenario wedged the firmware before its first log line — closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 666](../architecture/decisions.md), [#788](https://github.com/Absence0760/project-running/issues/788)).** The first CI failure carrying § 661's probe named `pc=0x1a000` with 3.9 M instructions retiring across two samples — a guest busy-wait — and the ELF the job stages beside it disassembles there to `ldr`/`cmp`/`bne` against `0x40011504`, RTC1's COUNTER: embassy-nrf's `RtcDriver::init` spin, confirming § 661's finding. **Two claims in the old entry were wrong**, both blaming the environment over the artifact: booting the ELF *CI itself uploaded* reproduces the wedge on arm64 macOS at the same PC, and the outcome is deterministic per **binary**, not per host — never a race, which is why `PerformanceInMips` 1/1000, `SetGlobalQuantum` 1 us and `CyclesPerInstruction` 200 all "still booted". Measured cause: Renode folds a CPU's executed slice into `BaseClockSource` only when the CPU reports, and `Update` charges that accumulated time to every *enabled* entry, so an RTC enabled part-way through absorbs the part that ran before the guest started it — `TASKS_START` at virtual time `0.001000000 s`, the COUNTER read one instruction later at `0.001065330 s`, 65.33 us, two ticks, and the spin then waits out the 512 s wrap. Fixed in the model, not the firmware: `sim/NRF52840_RTC_Overflow.cs` syncs the CPU's unreported time into the clock source before START / STOP / CLEAR / TRIGOVRFLW touch the timers, pinned by `sim/test_rtc_model.py` (a scenario cannot guard it — dropping the sync only fails for builds landing the wrong side of a tick). No budget widened, no retry, no skip. Verified on the exact CI ELF: 3/3 `pages` at `--budget 300`, reverting only that file wedges the same command at the same PC, and all nine scenarios green in one sweep.
- [x] **The local Supabase edge runtime intermittently never boots, failing a job in setup.** Closed 2026-08-19 ([decisions § 697](../architecture/decisions.md)) for both halves the entry asked about. **(2) The error string was the one lying.** The loop counted 45 attempts and called that "90s", which charges the `sleep 2`s and nothing else, so an attempt that spends curl's whole `--max-time` is free: 45 x (10 s + 2 s) = 540 s, i.e. the ~9 minutes actually observed. The budget is now a wall-clock deadline, the last attempt's `--max-time` is clamped to what is left of it, and the failure reports the elapsed seconds and attempt count it really spent. The budget itself was **not** widened, no retry was added, nothing was skipped — a probe whose upstream answers fast still gets the same ~45 tries. **The 90 s deadline was measured, not inherited:** across 277 jobs carrying this step in 14 workflow runs (2026-08-13 to 2026-08-19, every stack-using job type), the 275 green ones took a median of 1.21 s and a max of 12.15 s — 274 under 2 s, exactly one needing a single retry (shard 10/14, run 32150279557) — so the worst healthy boot on record uses 13 % of the deadline, and 90 s still affords eight full-`--max-time` attempts against the one ever observed. The sampling also found a **third occurrence** the entry did not know about: run 31834502152 (2026-08-14, e2e shard 12/14), 530.04 s over 45 attempts, reported as "within 90s", with attempts 2-45 exactly 12.013 s apart — 10 s of curl timeout plus the 2 s sleep, which is the measured proof that `000` here is a hung upstream, not a refused connection (attempt 1 got kong's instant `502`). The same arithmetic corrects this entry's own reading of `000`: 12 s per attempt means kong was accepting the connection and hanging for the full `--max-time`, not refusing it. `(last status 000000)` was a third small lie — `$(curl … || echo 000)` appended a second `000` to the one curl writes itself. **(1) Forensics now exist:** giving up dumps the stack's container list, every container's state + exit code, and the failing sidecar's plus kong's `docker logs` tail, so the next occurrence is diagnosable from the run log alone. The loop moved out of `action.yml` into `.github/actions/start-supabase/wait_for_sidecars.sh` so it could be pinned — `wait_for_sidecars.test.mjs` drives it with stub `curl`/`docker` on `PATH` under `workflow-lint`, and every accounting assertion was verified to fail against the old arithmetic. Original text: Seen twice on 2026-08-14 in one session — `Web e2e (Playwright) shard 8/14` on PR #764 and `Web e2e — Go live-hub (WebSocket)` on PR #766 — both dying in the *Start local Supabase stack* step with `edge runtime (clip-public-track) never became ready within 90s (last status 000000)` after ~45 consecutive curl attempts, ~9 minutes apart from the stated 90 s. No test ever runs; a re-run of the failed job passes clean both times. Two things to look at: (1) why the edge runtime container never serves, which the step's own forensics do not currently capture (it logs the wait loop but not the container's logs or exit state); (2) the message says "within 90s" while the loop actually ran ~9 minutes, so the wait helper's timeout accounting and its error string disagree — whichever is right, one of them is lying to whoever reads the failure. **Do not "fix" this by widening the timeout**: the runtime is not slow, it is not coming up at all (status `000000` is connection-refused, not a slow 200), so a longer wait would only make each failure more expensive. Worth catching because the failure is indistinguishable from a real break at a glance and trains people to re-run reflexively.

- [x] **A per-function edge worker could not boot without reaching `registry.npmjs.org`, which is what "the edge runtime never boots" has been all along.** Diagnosed and fixed 2026-08-19 ([decisions § 699](../architecture/decisions.md)) — the diagnosis came from the forensics the entry above added, on their first real firing (PR #793's own `Edge Function tests (Deno)` run). The runtime itself starts normally; the **worker** dies: `failed to create the graph: Failed loading https://registry.npmjs.org/@types%2fnode for package "@types/node" … Network is unreachable (os error 101)`. Cause: esm.sh's default (denonext) build of supabase-js imports `node:buffer` / `node:process`, and the runtime resolves `node:` builtin **types** from npm before a worker may boot. Every probe then hangs to curl's `--max-time` and reports `000`, which is the measured signature above. Reproduced deterministically outside CI — `edge-runtime start --main-service` with `--add-host registry.npmjs.org:127.0.0.1` reproduces the CI error byte for byte — and that harness is what graded the candidates:

  | candidate | offline worker boot |
  |---|---|
  | esm.sh default (denonext), i.e. before this change | **fails** |
  | `?no-dts` | **fails** — it is the `node:` specifier, not the declarations |
  | `?target=deno` | **boots** (no `node:` specifiers at all) |
  | `?target=deno` on stripe | **fails** — the stripe SDK imports `node:http` itself |
  | edge-runtime v1.73.15 / v1.74.3 (latest), default build | **fails** — a CLI bump does not fix it |
  | import map redirecting `npm:@types/node` to a local stub | not intercepted |

  Fixed where it is load-bearing rather than everywhere: `clip-public-track/index.ts` (the function every stack-using job's setup probes for its 405) and `_shared/rate_limit.ts`'s **type-only** import, which measurably re-arms the failure on its own even though it is erased at emit. Verified by booting the real repo files against a blackholed npm registry and getting the probe's own `Method not allowed`. **Deliberately NOT applied to the other 16 functions**: `?target=deno` swaps the `node:` imports for esm.sh's `deno.land/std@0.177.1` polyfill tree, which grows one function's bundle from **228 KB to 2.4 MB** (measured with `edge-runtime bundle`), so blanket-applying it would pay a 10x cold-boot cost on every function to convert a test-time failure that names itself into a slightly earlier one. Residual exposure, stated rather than hidden: the four stripe functions (`donations-checkout`, `events-cancel`, `events-checkout`, `events-connect-onboard`) and the other supabase-js functions still resolve `@types/node` at boot, so an npm outage can still fail a test that calls one — but it can no longer kill a job in setup before any test runs. Closing that residue needs a vendored/pre-seeded runtime module cache, not an import parameter. Guarded by `_shared/offline_worker_boot_guard.test.ts`, which fails the PR if either probe-path import loses `?target=deno` (mutation-checked).

## Testing gaps

- [ ] **Device-instrumented `integration_test` harness** — none today; would cover tile-cache / foreground-service / background-sync on real Android primitives. New infrastructure.
- [ ] **OSRM smoke test in CI** — blocked on free-runner capacity (OSM PBF extract + osrm-extract memory). Options: a self-hosted runner, or a pre-built OSRM cache in S3 the workflow downloads.
- [x] **`tests-e2e/routes/segment-catalogue-browse.spec.ts`** — written alongside the `/segments` browse page ([decisions § 593](../architecture/decisions.md)) by a round that shared a local Supabase stack it could not reset, so it landed unexecuted; **run on the integration branch 2026-08-13 and it passed.** No longer an open gap.
- [ ] **Positive-path Edge Function tests** — the envelope suite covers auth-rejection only; 200-on-valid-HMAC / replay-dedupe / freshness-window tests need real secret values in the CI config.

- [x] **The `Schema / type drift` job reports "database.types.ts is out of sync with the Supabase schema" for any failure in the job, including a plain unit-test failure.** Closed 2026-08-24 ([decisions § 711](../architecture/decisions.md)) — the trailing `if: failure()` step is gone and each of the five checks now diagnoses itself in the `if ! cmd; then echo "::error::…"; exit 1; fi` form the `twin-parity` and `schema-codegen-drift` jobs already used, with its own remediation; each migration guard now runs its own unit suite FIRST, because a broken guard's verdict on the migration set means nothing. The job was renamed `Schema / type drift + web unit tests` rather than split in two — splitting is the only thing that makes the job NAME say which check broke, and it costs a second `npm ci` plus a hosted-runner slot on every PR against a budget the concurrency block is already rationing, while a per-step `::error::` reaches the checks summary for free. `scripts/check_ci_diagnostics.mjs` (in `workflow-lint`) fails the build on any `failure()`-conditioned step that prints a diagnosis without naming a step outcome, and on a check step added to that job without one; verified against the pre-fix file, which it reports five ways. A failed web source guard in that job printed the type-drift error and the "run `npm run gen:types`" instruction, which is a confident statement about the wrong thing — the first look went to the migration and the generated types, neither of which was involved. Same class as the CI wait loop's "within 90s" ([§ 697](../architecture/decisions.md)): the message should name what actually failed, or the step should be conditioned on the drift check specifically.

## Web bundle weight

- [x] **Externalise the RevenueCat web SDK behind hosted checkout** (done 2026-06-20) — `@revenuecat/purchases-js` was a top-level static import in `apps/web/src/lib/billing/revenuecat.ts`, shipping the full SDK (~178 KB gzipped) into the `/settings/upgrade` bundle. The web Pro flow now drives both checkout and management through RevenueCat's **hosted-checkout redirect**: `proCheckoutUrl()` redirects to the Web Paywall Link `https://pay.rev.cat/<token>/<userId>?redirect_url=…`, and `managementUrl()` opens the no-code customer portal — no embedded SDK, no API key in the bundle. The dependency was dropped from `apps/web/package.json` + the root lockfile, removing the ~178 KB from the summed bundle metric (no `purchases-js` import remains in `apps/web/src`). Env config renamed `PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_WEB_API_KEY` → `PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_WEB_CHECKOUT_URL` (+ optional `PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_WEB_PORTAL_URL`), with the production-env guard, CI workflows, and security_guards test updated in lockstep; the fail-closed configured-vs-unconfigured fallback is preserved. Pure URL builder unit-tested (`revenuecat_links.test.ts`, 6 cases); the unconfigured-fallback e2e covers the redirect-fail-closed path. **Mobile parity:** N/A by design — `apps/mobile_android/lib/revenuecat.dart` keeps `purchases_flutter` because iOS Guideline 3.1.1 / Play policy require native IAP for digital goods; the web hosted-redirect is a web-only optimisation, not a parity gap.

- [x] **The bundle ceiling now climbs once per language, because the metric counts catalogues nobody but that language's readers download.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 771](../architecture/decisions.md)). The premise held — a single total over every emitted chunk is right for a dep and wrong for a translation — but three of the filed numbers did not, and one of them decided the design. **Measured on a production build:** the total is exactly **2462 KB** as filed; the largest chunk is **245 KB**, not 248; and the catalogues are **six chunks totalling 522 KB** (de 88, es 85, fr 88, ja 91, pt-BR 85, pt-PT 85), a **fifth** of the bundle rather than a quarter, not seven totalling ~609 KB. **The seventh is not a chunk and "every reader downloads exactly one" is false**: `i18n/store.svelte.ts` imports English statically as the synchronous fallback dict, so rollup emits it inside the shared store chunk (80 KB gzipped) that every reader downloads before a locale is negotiated — every non-English reader downloads **two** catalogues, and the English 80 KB is an unconditional cost that belongs in the code budget rather than the catalogue one. The measurement is now `scripts/check_web_bundle_budget.mjs` (+ its `node --test` sibling, both run from `web-bundle-budget.yml`), classifying chunks from vite's client manifest because client chunks are content-hashed and carry no name a pattern could match. Three ceilings replace the one: **code 2120 KB** (measured 1934, ~9.6% headroom), **largest single code chunk 350 KB** unchanged (measured 245, catalogues now excluded), and **100 KB per catalogue, never summed** (measured max 91, ja), so a new language moves nothing. Proved on the real build in both directions: a 200 KB rogue dep totals 2656 and **passes** the retired 2700 ceiling while failing the code one; three more languages total 2720 and **fail** the retired ceiling while leaving the code figure untouched. Slack against a rogue dep goes 244 KB → 186 KB, and — the point — stays 186 KB however many languages ship.

- [x] **The bundle budget measures no HTML, so a per-locale prerendered surface would be invisible to all three ceilings.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 775](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The premise about `/learn` is false and the file the same walk was hiding is 54x the whole population asked about.** The build emits **16** HTML files, not the 8 guides x 6 languages the filing assumed: 8 guides, 6 category pages, `/learn` and `/`, in **one** language — prose localization has not shipped, so the second per-language cost does not exist yet. All 16 total **71 KB gzipped**, largest 5 KB. Widening the walk to see them found what else `*.js`/`*.css` was hiding: **33 files and 4058 KB gzipped**, of which one file is **3866 KB** — `material-symbols-outlined.woff2`, the full unsubsetted variable font, `@font-face`d from the ROOT layout's stylesheet with `font-display: block`, i.e. **1.8x the entire 2120 KB code ceiling** and blocking for every reader who renders an icon. Decided: the population is **every emitted non-JS/CSS file**, not HTML's alone, budgeted at `MAX_ASSET_KB = 100` **per file and never summed** — the catalogues' rule, for the catalogues' reason, since a reader loads one prerendered page and one favicon. So prerendering `/learn` once per language multiplies the file count and moves no ceiling, which is the filed question answered. The font is exempted **by name** with its own 3900 KB ceiling (pattern skipping vite's content hash, so a version bump keeps it and growth does not); an exemption matching no emitted file fails, and so does one whose asset has shrunk back under 100 KB. Six new tests; subsetting the font is `apps/web` source work and is filed separately below. Original filing below. **The bundle budget measures no HTML, so a per-locale prerendered surface would be invisible to all three ceilings.** Inherited from the retired rule and unchanged by [decisions § 771](../architecture/decisions.md): the walk matches `*.js` and `*.css` only. Today that costs nothing measurable — the 8 Learn guides × 6 languages prerender to static HTML and emit no JS chunk, so the second per-language cost in the tree is outside the metric entirely — but it means the guard cannot see prerendered weight at all, and the `/learn` tree is exactly the surface that would grow it. Decide whether prerendered HTML gets a population and a ceiling of its own, or is deliberately out of scope and said to be.

- [x] **A red web bundle budget does not block a merge.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 775](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The premise held exactly; the costing of the fix did not.** The filing priced moving the job into `ci.yml` as "a second `npm ci` + a `vite build` on the gate's critical path" — but `build-web` in `ci.yml` **already runs that exact install and that exact build**, with the same four `PUBLIC_*` placeholders, and is already in the gate's `needs:`. The second install was the one `web-bundle-budget.yml` was paying: **47 s** repeating what a **69 s** job had just done, and only on `pull_request`, so a direct push to `main` measured nothing at all. The measurement now runs against `build-web`'s own output at a measured **0.21 s**, plus 9 s of stdlib-only guard tests, and the duplicate job is deleted. **The gate's critical path is unchanged either way**: on run 33175862509 it is `test-packages` at **17 min** against an 18 min 01 s wall clock, so any candidate here is two orders of magnitude below it and the decision was about runner-minutes, not wall clock. Bundling rather than splitting follows `parity-matrix`'s own written rationale; `build-web` now derives as a bundled job and each of its named steps diagnoses itself per § 764. Original filing below. **A red web bundle budget does not block a merge.** `web-bundle-budget.yml` is its own workflow and its jobs are not in the `CI gate` aggregator's `needs:` list, which is the single required status check — so the budget is advisory in practice however tight the ceilings are ([decisions § 771](../architecture/decisions.md), § 764 for the aggregator rule). Unchanged by the split and not fixed there because it is a `ci.yml` decision, not a budget one: either the budget job moves into `ci.yml` (paying a second `npm ci` + a `vite build` on the gate's critical path, against a runner budget the concurrency block already rations) or the aggregator learns to wait on another workflow. Worth deciding rather than leaving implicit.

- [x] **A single 3866 KB icon font is 1.8x the whole web code budget, and it blocks first paint.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 780](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The filing is right in every particular, and the fix is a subset built from the tree rather than a smaller exemption.** Re-measured: the upstream file is 3,960,036 bytes, 3866 KB gzipped, `font-display: block` on the root layout, and the non-JS/CSS population was 33 files / 4058 KB. The glyph set is DERIVED in two rules — element text at every render site, plus every quoted `[a-z0-9_]+` literal under `apps/web/src` that the upstream font can render, which is what finds the ~190 names that only ever reach a span through `{item.icon}` or a `*_ICON` map. That needs a vocabulary and the package does not ship a usable one: `index.d.ts` lists 3899 names against the font's 4271 and omits every alias whose ligature resolves to a differently-named glyph (`terrain` → `landscape`, `emoji_events` → `trophy`), twelve of which this app renders — so the vocabulary is read out of the font and committed at `scripts/material_symbols_ligatures.txt`. 347 icons over 332 glyphs; `GRAD`/`opsz` pinned at the values the source already asks for, `FILL`/`wght` kept variable because components vary both. **3866 KB → 74 KB (−98.1%)**, assets 4058 → 266 KB, and the exemption is deleted rather than resized — `ASSET_EXEMPTIONS` is now empty. `font-display: block` STAYS: for a ligature icon font every other value paints the icon's own NAME in the fallback face, and blocking was only ever a problem because the face was 3.9 MB. Guarded by `scripts/web_icon_font.test.mjs` in `build-web`, which re-runs the selection and compares with the manifest in both directions, so an icon added to the tree and an icon that left it each fail with the name in the message. Original filing below. **A single 3866 KB icon font is 1.8x the whole web code budget, and it blocks first paint.** Found measuring the asset population for [decisions § 775](../architecture/decisions.md). `apps/web/src/app.css` imports `material-symbols/outlined.css`, so the **full unsubsetted** Material Symbols Outlined variable font lands in the root layout's stylesheet as an `@font-face` with `font-display: block` — every reader who renders any icon (which is every page: the nav alone uses several) waits on a **3,960,036-byte** download before that text paints, and 3866 KB gzipped is **1.8x the entire 2120 KB `MAX_CODE_KB` ceiling** and **42x** the largest catalogue (ja, 91 KB). It was invisible to the bundle budget until the walk was widened, and it is now held at a named 3900 KB exemption in `scripts/check_web_bundle_budget.mjs` — ceilinged so it cannot grow, not fixed. The durable fix is to subset to the glyphs the app actually uses: the icon names are literal ligature strings in `.svelte` markup and in `BADGE_CATALOGUE` / `ROUTE_MARKER_KINDS`, so the used set is derivable from source rather than guessed, and a build-time subset (or self-hosting only that subset) should land the font in tens of KB. That is `apps/web` source work plus a guard that the derived glyph set still covers every ligature the tree names — deliberately out of scope for the CI slice that measured it. Deleting the exemption entry is part of closing this: the budget fails on an exemption whose asset has shrunk back under the general ceiling.
- [x] **`unplugin-icons` is configured for `apps/web` with `autoInstall: true` and nothing imports it.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 786](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The filing is right in every particular, and three more of the same class sat beside it.** Verified independently: no `~icons/` specifier exists in any extension the build compiles, and the only two occurrences of the name under `apps/web/src` are a licence row and its spec mirror, both strings. Deleting the plugin and `@iconify-json/material-symbols` took **21 entries out of the npm tree (423 → 402)** — the transitives are the `autoInstall` machinery itself (`@antfu/install-pkg`, `package-manager-detector`, `tinyexec`, `local-pkg`), so the thing that could have installed an unlocked package at build time is gone rather than merely unused. Sweeping the class found **`normalize.css`** (declared in the scaffold commit `943f6af6e`, imported by nothing ever, and called the styling stack by `apps/web/CLAUDE.md` and `README.md` for the life of the repo), a stray root **`html-to-image`** (only `apps/web` imports it and only `apps/web` needs to declare it; `10b5ec519` added both at once), and **`apps/web/vercel.json`** (a deploy config for the platform [§ 53](../architecture/decisions.md) decided against, read by nothing). Everything else is genuinely indirect and stays: `material-symbols` is read out of `node_modules` by `scripts/web_icon_font.mjs`, the three `@types/*` back modules the tree uses, `tsx`/`typescript`/`svelte-check` are binaries whose names differ from their packages, and root `svelte` is the documented hoist pin. `scripts/check_dead_dependencies.mjs` (new, in `parity-types`) makes the class non-returnable: derived where it can be, four falsifiable `INDIRECT` entries where it cannot. Run against the pre-change tree it fails on `@iconify-json/material-symbols` rather than on the plugin — the honest limit, recorded in the ADR. Production build unregressed: largest asset still 74 KB, `ASSET_EXEMPTIONS` still empty. Original filing below. **`unplugin-icons` is configured for `apps/web` with `autoInstall: true` and nothing imports it.** Found while subsetting the icon font ([decisions § 780](../architecture/decisions.md)). `vite.config.ts` registers the plugin and `@iconify-json/material-symbols` is a devDependency, but there is no `~icons/` import anywhere under `apps/web/src` — every rendered icon comes from the Material Symbols webfont via a ligature. Two docs described the plugin as how web icons work, which was never true of a single icon; those are corrected, the dead configuration is not. `autoInstall: true` is the part that argues for removing rather than leaving it: it lets a build install a package that was not in the lockfile. Delete the plugin and the `@iconify-json` devDependency, or adopt it deliberately for the icons the webfont cannot serve.

## watchOS deferred target

- [x] **CheckpointStore.loadTrackPoints buffers the whole track file at finish** — SHIPPED 2026-08-03 (issue #664, [decisions.md § 467](../architecture/decisions.md)). The read now mirrors the streaming save: `forEachTrackPoint` walks the NDJSON 64 KiB at a time, carries at most one partial line and yields a point at a time, still skipping an undecodable tail. `FinishedRun` stopped carrying `[TrackPoint]` — it holds the file URL + point count — so `writeTrackJSON` streams into a staged file it renames and the DEBUG upload maps that artefact rather than re-encoding; streaming the read alone would have moved the peak, not removed it. **The ~60 MB figure here was an undercount**: recomputed at the 100 h / ~360k-fix target it is ~73 MB *inside `loadTrackPoints` alone* (~27 MB file + ~11.5 MB `Substring` headers + ~17.3 MB records + ~17.3 MB of heap `ts` strings — the 20-byte ISO stamp is past Swift's 15-byte small-string cap, the term the old estimate missed), plus the resident array and its encoding. Pinned by new XCTest cases (chunk-boundary walk, truncated vs whole final line, flush-boundary JSON validity, file lifetime); the Swift is **Mac-CI-compiled only**, and an Instruments trace over a long recording is still a bench item.

## Live-tracking residual (wave-1 follow-ons, 2026-06-13)

The headline live-tracking bugs (staleness honesty, name-leak on the event leaderboard, retention, reconnect JWT, tie-order/cap, in-zone last-seen carve-out) all shipped. Residual scope edges:

- [x] **Label the coarse last-seen ping in the spectator/SAR UI (web + mobile).** Done 2026-06-20. The web `/live/[id]` page + mobile `live_spectator_screen.dart` (+ iOS twin) now read the `coarse` flag on the latest ping and render a distinct marker (web `.runner-dot.coarse` hollow amber ring; mobile `LiveRunMap.coarsePosition` → `_CoarseDot`) plus an "Approximate / last seen near here" badge + sub-line, gated on a live (non-terminal) `coarse=true` fix. i18n added to all six web locales + all mobile ARBs; Playwright (`tests-e2e/live/spectator.spec.ts`) + a Flutter widget test pin the badge. Note: the **event live pages** were NOT in scope after reading the code — they render `race_pings`, which has no `coarse` column (the `20270121_001` carve-out covers `live_run_pings` only). Extending the carve-out to `race_pings` is the separate follow-up below.
- [x] **Extend the privacy-zone last-seen carve-out to `race_pings`.** SHIPPED 2026-07-02 (migration `20270309_001`, [decisions.md § 196](../architecture/decisions.md)). The sibling `race_pings` BEFORE-INSERT trigger now retains an in-zone ping coarsened to a ~2-dp grid + flagged `coarse=true` (at most one per `event_id/instance_start/user_id`) instead of dropping it; only lat/lng are coarsened (distance/elapsed/bpm kept for ranking). The competitive-board-semantics concern resolved in favour of the same last-seen honesty the solo feed provides — the web event live page renders the coarse "last seen near here" chip + hollow-amber marker. Web-only (no mobile event-live spectator reads `race_pings`); pgtap + Playwright pinned.
- [x] **Mirror the next-cut-off "signal lost" vs "still connecting" copy split to mobile.** Done 2026-07-11. `live_spectator_screen.dart`'s `_CutoffCard` now takes the screen's `stale` flag and branches the suppressed (`unknown`) verdict like web `/live/[id]`: a stale fix reads the amber w700 `liveCutoffSignalLost` ("Signal lost — can't project arrival", matching the screen's existing Delayed/amber freshness treatment) while a fresh still-connecting fix keeps the neutral italic `liveCutoffWaitingSignal`. ARB key added to all seven mobile catalogues (six locales + base `pt`); pure UI, no helper change (the `live_freshness` + `live_cutoff_eta` twins already exposed the flag). Both branches pinned in `test/live_spectator_cutoff_test.dart`; mirrored to the iOS twin. See [predictive_live_tracking.md § Design](../features/predictive_live_tracking.md).
- [ ] **Validate the Strava-CSV-UTC + FIT-compressed-timestamp fixes against real files pre-prod.** Both landed (Strava `Activity Date` parsed as UTC; FIT compressed-timestamp records decoded) but only against the documented format + a synthetic fixture. Get a real Strava bulk export + a real older-Garmin/Wahoo compressed-timestamp FIT and confirm the format string(s) + field layout before relying on them in prod (the parse is fail-safe, so the risk is "no improvement", not corruption).

## Pure-logic-helper hunt deferrals (2026-06-14)

Low-reach TS↔Dart twin divergences found by the 2026-06-14 helper hunt; the higher-impact bugs from that round were fixed + pinned. Pick these up the next time the file is touched:

- [x] **`macroBudget` negative-tie rounding diverges TS↔Dart.** `nutrition_budget.ts` uses `Math.round` (rounds negative `.5` toward +∞); the Dart twin uses `.round()` (half away from zero). Latent in production (inputs are exact integers, so `.round()` is a no-op) — only reachable via direct fractional calls. Pick one convention and align both twins. **Done:** the Dart twin now rounds via `_roundHalfUp(value) => (value + 0.5).floor()` (matches JS `Math.round`); negative-half-tie parity test added both sides + mirrored to the iOS twin.
- [x] **`computeSessionAdherence` can report >100% adherence.** `session_steps.ts` (+ `.dart`) computed `completedSteps / totalSteps` without clamping or matching results to steps by `itemId`; a results array longer than the steps array yielded `>1` and still verdicted `'completed'`. Fixed: completed-result counting is keyed to the set of step `itemId`s (extra/duplicate/unmatched results no longer inflate), the count is capped at `totalSteps`, and the fraction is clamped to `[0,1]`. Mirror tests added on both sides (more-results-than-steps → 1.0; unmatched itemIds ignored).
- [x] **`gym_routine.prefillFromRoutine` RPE string diverges for whole-number doubles.** Done 2026-06-20. Web is canonical (`String(s.targetRpe)` → `"8"`); the Dart twin (`apps/mobile_android/lib/gym_routine.dart` + iOS) now formats RPE via a `_rpeString` helper that drops the trailing `.0` on a whole-number double (8.0 → `"8"`) and keeps the decimal on a half-step (7.5 → `"7.5"`), matching JS `String(number)` semantics. The TS file was not touched. Pinned by a mirror test on both sides (`prefillFromRoutine: whole-number RPE drops the trailing .0, half-step keeps it`); test counts stay in lockstep (23 each).

## Pure-logic-helper hunt deferrals (2026-06-15)

Verified-but-deferred findings from the 2026-06-15 five-worker bug hunt (the three clear bugs that round — checkpoint-board tie-break jitter, roadbook equal-clock 0s cutoff, plan-progress zero-distance long run — were fixed + pinned). Both parked on a product call were resolved + shipped 2026-07-02:

- [x] **`gym_adherence` fails a weighted-duration set on an unlogged weight even when the full duration was held.** RESOLVED 2026-07-02 ([decisions.md § 194](../architecture/decisions.md)). Product call: duration is the primary axis while the weight is unrecorded. The weight gate now fails a set only when a weight was actually logged and fell short, or when the set has no duration axis (weight-only, unchanged); a both-axes set with the weight unlogged is graded on duration. Twin-pinned (`gym_adherence.ts` ↔ `.dart`, 20 tests each).
- [x] **`gym_progression` percent_cycle labels a real prescription `'hold'` when there's no prior top weight.** RESOLVED 2026-07-02 ([decisions.md § 195](../architecture/decisions.md)). Product call: added a dedicated `establish_baseline` `ProgressionReason` for the first/bodyweight session (concrete 1RM-percentage weight, no prior top weight) instead of the misleading `'hold'`; the suggested weight is unchanged. Localized in all six web locales + all mobile ARBs, exhaustive `gym_detail_screen` switch extended, twin-pinned (20 tests each).

## Mobile bug hunt deferrals (2026-07-24)

Findings from the 2026-07-24 three-worker mobile hunt that were parked for a
product call, a schema change, or a coordinated web+mobile change. The nine bugs
fixed that round (route-progress floor poisoned by a rejected fix, treadmill
console reset, pace across a pause, silent turn cues on a second run, negative
splits, run-index ordering, DST week boundaries, backup-restore abort,
route-sidecar fail-open) are in git history on the same PR.

**All twelve were resolved on 2026-07-25**, on the same PR, each with the
decision it needed recorded in [decisions.md](../architecture/decisions.md)
§§ 299-305 and a pinning test confirmed to fail against the old behaviour. A
follow-up hunt the same day found that the § 303 route-store half had been
claimed but not shipped, plus seven further defects in the same subsystem; those
are folded into the list below and recorded as §§ 308-313 (and an amendment to
§ 301). Two
carry a judgement the owner may want to revisit rather than a defect: the
hydration ceiling's specific value (§ 300) and the run-store's
re-upload-rather-than-presume-synced asymmetry (§ 299). Kept here rather than
pruned so the reasoning stays next to the finding it answers.

- [x] **`OfflineSyncStore.markSynced` re-reads the live row instead of the pushed snapshot.** RESOLVED 2026-07-25 ([decisions.md § 302](../architecture/decisions.md)). The compare key is **identity**, not `lastModifiedAt`: entries are immutable and every mutation installs a new instance, so `identical` is exact, while a clock comparison would miss two mutations inside one tick. `markSynced` now takes the pushed entry and marks only while it is still resident; the `pendingDelete` branch applies the same test before `dropRow`, so a tombstone raised mid-push is no longer flipped to synced and left with a live server row. `syncWithServer` also gained the drain guard (a second concurrent call returns 0). All eight subclasses inherit both. Three pinning tests, each confirmed failing against the old behaviour.
- [x] **Two `LocalRunStore` / `LocalRouteStore` instances write the same whole-file sidecars from independent snapshots.** RESOLVED 2026-07-25 ([decisions.md § 303](../architecture/decisions.md)). **This entry was wrong when first written and is corrected here: only `LocalRunStore` was changed that day.** The merge and `_withSidecarLock` never reached `local_route_store.dart`, whose three sidecars (`synced_route_ids.json`, `route_owner_tags.json`, `offline_pinned_route_ids.json`) stayed unlocked whole-file replaces even though `background_sync.dart` builds a second `LocalRouteStore` over the same directory and calls `markManyRoutesSynced` + `tagRoutesOwner` from it. Both stores are now covered. Run sidecars: `synced_ids` keeps an unknown id only while its run file exists (the process-independent ghost prune); `pending_remote_deletes` lets additions win and carries removals in a cleared-ledger. Route sidecars: all three resolve a shared id the `pending_remote_deletes` way, because for routes the losing direction is a cross-account transfer (an un-tagged route is drainable by any account on the device) rather than a re-upload — § 299's asymmetry applied to the other store. Pinned by three two-stores-over-one-directory tests per store, each confirmed failing against the whole-file write.
- [x] **`LocalRunStore._persistIndex` is an unlocked whole-file replace, and the cold-load gate only compares the id set.** RESOLVED 2026-07-25 ([decisions.md § 309](../architecture/decisions.md)).
