If a mobile dashboard ever grows a load card, port `self_load`, `plan_ramp` and `comeback` **together** — they share `recentRunVolume` / `volumeSample` and the `activeWeeks` gate that makes the ratio card and the comeback card mutually exclusive, so porting one without the others would either duplicate the reduction or let a phone show both cards at once.

### Test-hygiene items surfaced by the same batch

- [x] **Flaky: `apps/web/tests-e2e/challenges/list-load-error.spec.ts:43`** Fixed 2026-08-18. The cause was not two competing endpoints — Browse goes through an RPC and never matches the interceptor — but `loadMine` running inside an `$effect` that reads `auth.user`, so it re-runs when auth hydrates and the one-shot `failNext` was consumed by whichever load won. The handler now fails every GET until the retry is armed. ("Retry re-fetches and clears the error on a healthy response") fails ~50 % of runs — its `page.route` handler arms `failNext` once, but `/challenges` issues more than one `GET /rest/v1/challenges` (My challenges + Browse), so which request receives the simulated 500 is a race. Scope the interceptor to the My-challenges request rather than adding a retry.
- [x] **`tests-e2e/dashboard/load-ramp.spec.ts:111` passes vacuously.** Fixed 2026-08-18 — the no-card assertion is anchored on `dash-total-runs`, so the absence only counts once the dashboard has rendered. Its "no card" assertion would also pass on a page that never loaded. The `dash-total-runs` testid added on `/dashboard` in this batch exists to anchor it; the comeback spec already uses that pattern.
- [x] **`RouteMarkerEditor.detailLine` drops a clock-only target.** Fixed 2026-08-18 — the chip prefers elapsed and falls back to `target.clock`, matching the `cutoff` branch above it and the editor's own read-back precedence. Pinned by `tests-e2e/routes/markers.spec.ts`. The mobile twin (`route_markers_panel.dart`) already had the fallback, so this was a web-only divergence. It renders the target chip under `if (target?.elapsedS != null)`, so a marker saved with `target_clock` and no `target_elapsed_s` shows no chip in the marker list — even though the editor can author that form and the roadbook now resolves it correctly ([decisions § 614](../architecture/decisions.md)). A one-line fix plus a test.
- [x] **Playwright reuses a stale dev server.** Fixed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 631](../architecture/decisions.md)). `globalSetup` probes the server through Vite's `/@fs/` route before signing anyone in; a `403` proves it is rooted in another checkout and fails the run with that reason. `docs/testing/testing.md` now names the connection-refused symptom too. `playwright.config.ts` sets `reuseExistingServer: !CI`, so a long-lived `vite` process on `:7777` from an older checkout serves a bundle that predates a new component — a new-surface spec then fails with no console error while every pre-existing spec passes. Two rounds in this batch lost cycles to it. Worth a harness guard that asserts the served build matches the working tree, and a line in `docs/testing/testing.md`.

## Mirrors and fixes owed from the 2026-08-16 improvement batch

Five parallel `/improve-round` passes landed web-first in one PR ([decisions § 617](../architecture/decisions.md)–§ 621). Each deferred its mobile half deliberately rather than shipping a Dart twin with no caller.

- [x] **Mobile mirror: the gym routine-history panel.** Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 617 amendment](../architecture/decisions.md)). `routine_history` is a registered TS<->Dart parity pair (`apps/mobile_android/lib/routine_history.dart`, 13 mirror tests each) and `routine_detail_screen.dart` carries the author-gated panel over a new `ApiClient.fetchGymRoutineSessions`. Read from the SERVER, not `LocalGymStore` — that store holds only the most recent page of workouts, so a routine last run months ago would have read as never run, and a "you have never done this" claim made from a cache that does not reach back far enough is worse than no panel. The Dart sort carries an input-index tiebreak the TypeScript does not need, because `Array.sort` has been stable since ES2019 and `List.sort` is not. `parity.md` row flipped.
- [x] **Mobile bulk importers report a bare failure count.** Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 650](../architecture/decisions.md)). `import_failures` is a registered parity pair (22 mirror tests each) and `widgets/import_failure_report.dart` renders it behind all three importers. Every swallow was fixed AT ITS CATCH, never reclassified downstream of a discarded error — and the filed counter was the least of it: Health Connect dropped an unmappable workout into a `debugPrint` with **no counter at all**, so those activities never appeared anywhere; its `fetchRoutes` returned exactly the shape a source holding no routes returns, so a failed read was indistinguishable from an absence and the import asserted "these runs have no map" without knowing it; `csv_run_importer.dart` used `catch (_)` and discarded the error object outright; and the shared save loop named a failed run by opaque UUID. One mobile-only disclosure has no web twin — mobile saves locally then batch-pushes, so a push that throws is one deferred upload of runs safely on disk, reported as a status note rather than as N per-item failures. **`_backfillHealthConnectTracks`'s push swallow closed 2026-08-18** — it reports `pushDeferred` through the same `_cloudPushDeferred` flag and the same `importStatusCloudPushDeferred` string, because that string's claim (on this device, upload did not go through, SyncService retries) is true verbatim for the backfill. No new key, no second vocabulary.
- [x] **Mobile mirror: run-detail pacing halves + the grade-adjusted split column.** Shipped 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 648](../architecture/decisions.md)) — `pace_analysis.dart` is a registered parity pair (13 mirror tests each); `run_detail_screen.dart` renders the pacing card above the splits list and a grade-adjusted lane on each split row, both gated on the same 2 s/km margin the key-stat GAP tile uses. `parity.md` row flipped. Original text: Web ships `apps/web/src/lib/runs/pace_analysis.ts` behind the Pacing card and the extra splits column on `/runs/[id]` ([decisions § 619](../architecture/decisions.md)). `run_detail_screen.dart` already computes splits (`_computeSplits`) and whole-run GAP, so the Dart port is additive. Doing it makes `pace_analysis` a registered parity pair — add it to BOTH registries in the same change, and flip the `docs/product/parity.md` row when it lands.
- [x] **Mobile: the club event listings still advertise a cancelled occurrence.** Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 629](../architecture/decisions.md)). `event_occurrence` is now a registered TS<->Dart parity pair carrying `isOccurrenceCancelled` + `nextLiveInstance`; `upcomingCancelledOccurrences` stays web-only because it backs an organiser reinstate picker mobile does not have. `SocialService._enrichEvents` and `fetchNextRsvpedEvent` both resolve through it off a new batched, chunked `fetchCancelledInstancesForEvents` (one `in`-filtered read per list, `gte now`, fail-closed to none-cancelled), and the RSVP fetcher walks 10 candidates like web's `RSVP_CANDIDATE_LIMIT`. `EventView.hasLiveOccurrence` (default `true`) is the non-nullable encoding of web's null `next_instance_start`. **Still open:** those three fetchers have no unit-test reach — `SocialService` resolves its client inline — so only the decision logic they delegate to is pinned. `SocialService._enrichEvents` and `SocialService.fetchNextRsvpedEvent` both resolve an event's next instance with a bare `nextInstanceAfter` and never consult `event_exceptions`, so the mobile club Events tab and the Run-tab `widgets/upcoming_event_card.dart` keep pointing at an occurrence the organiser called off — the leak web closed in [§ 620](../architecture/decisions.md). Mobile already has the primitive (`fetchCancelledInstances`, used today only by `event_detail_screen.dart`), so the fix is to feed it into both fetchers. If the Dart side takes the shaping helpers too, `social/event_occurrence.ts` becomes a registered parity pair.
- [x] **Mirror the spectator stopped-runner readout to mobile.** Shipped 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 647](../architecture/decisions.md)) — `live_motion.dart` ported test-for-test (18/18) with all three fail-closed branches and the `atLeast` floor, registered in both registries; `live_spectator_screen.dart` renders the neutral chip off a 1 h ping buffer stamped from each ping's own `at` clock, not the device clock. 4 widget tests. Original text: `apps/web/src/lib/safety/live_motion.ts` + the `/live/[id]` chip shipped web-only ([decisions § 621](../architecture/decisions.md)); mobile's `live_spectator_screen.dart` renders the same `live_run_pings` stream but has no motion readout, so a runner still pinging from one spot reads there as a plain live dot with the pace row simply absent. Carry all three fail-closed branches and the `atLeast` floor; the screen already holds the `stale` flag it needs from the `live_freshness` twin. Relevant personas: `moab240-spectator`, `ws100-spectator`, `utmb-spectator`.

### Bugs and hygiene surfaced by the same batch

- [x] **`expandInstances` silently drops the first occurrence of a weekly / biweekly series when `starts_at` carries non-zero milliseconds.** Closed 2026-08-18 on web + Dart + the `mobile_ios` twin ([decisions § 628](../architecture/decisions.md)). The not-before-start check compares against the start truncated to the second, the resolution the stamp itself has, rather than against the raw instant. One mirror test each, both verified to fail against the old comparison. Both halves of the registered `recurrence` parity pair stamp each candidate with the start's h/m/s and a **zero** millisecond (web `recurrence.ts` `stamp()`, Dart `recurrence.dart`), then reject it with `stamped < start`. A `starts_at` of `…T02:49:18.132Z` therefore yields a first candidate of `…:18.000`, which loses the comparison, and the series' own first occurrence never appears — in the picker, the listings, or any RSVP key. Reproduced on web (`ms=0` → first 2026-08-23; `ms=132` → first 2026-08-30). Production `starts_at` values come from a `datetime-local` input (whole minutes), so this bites API-written and fixture-written rows rather than user-created ones — but it silently swallows a real occurrence. The fix is a millisecond-tolerant comparison applied to web + Dart + the `mobile_ios` twin, with a mirror test on both sides.
- [x] **`fetchGymRoutineSessions` under-reports past ~500 sessions, now on two platforms.** Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 658](../architecture/decisions.md), migration `20270528_001`). The durable fix, not the mobile-only one the entry warned against: `gym_routine_history(p_routine_id, p_recent_limit)` aggregates server-side — `session_count` / `last_performed_at` / `graded_count` / `completed_count` — and returns an explicitly bounded page of recent sessions (`p_recent_limit`, clamped to `[0, 50]`) in the SAME row, so the five rows the panel lists and the count above them come from one snapshot and one `WHERE`. SECURITY INVOKER with a load-bearing `auth.uid()` filter, because the `gym_workouts` select policy also admits other users' `is_public` rows. Both § 617 exclusions are reproduced in SQL — an in-flight `gym_session_draft` row is dropped from tallies AND page, a "save as is" row counts as a session but stays outside the graded denominator. Days-since-last deliberately stayed client-side (it is floored against the READER's clock and clamped, a display rule not a stored fact), so the `routine_history` parity pair keeps a real job: `summariseRoutineHistory` became `routineHistoryFromAggregate` and `RoutineHistory.sessions` became `recentSessions`, named so no surface can read the page as everything — 16 mirror tests each, web + Dart + the `mobile_ios` twin landed together. pgTAP `gym_routine_history_test.sql` (12 tests) pins a 1200-session routine past the PostgREST cap, both exclusions, and the cross-user RLS case against a deliberately-public row. **Left open deliberately:** no `(user_id, metadata->>'routine_id')` index — the aggregate walks the same `gym_workouts_user` index rows the old query did, so it is not a regression, and a non-concurrent `CREATE INDEX` takes `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` while `CONCURRENTLY` cannot run in Supabase's wrapped transaction; it belongs in its own change.
- [x] **The displayed "Elapsed" stat on `/live/[id]` froze at the last ping while the page internally knew the true race clock.** Closed 2026-08-24 on web + Dart + the `mobile_ios` twin ([decisions § 712](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The framing was the defect**: one tile was being asked for two quantities, which is why neither answer to "should it advance?" was right. The **race clock** (wall time since the start) and the **runner's timer** (`elapsed_s`, the recorder's stopwatch as of the last fix) are now separate tiles under their own labels on both surfaces. No estimate was needed for the first: `beginLiveBroadcast` pre-creates the `runs` row with the recorder's real `started_at` and `is_public = true`, `public_runs` projects `started_at` with `grant select … to anon`, and **both surfaces were already reading it** (web `visibleRun.started_at`, mobile `run.startedAt`) — for a `cutoff_clock` marker's time of day and the `isFinishedStale` / `runIsFinished` end inference, but never subtracted from now — so the race clock is a subtraction of two values the page already holds. `raceClockS` joined the `live_freshness` parity pair (16 web / 13 Dart mirror tests, the pre-existing 3-test asymmetry unchanged); `liveElapsedS` stays as the fallback and both suites pin that it can only be a **lower bound** on the same clock. Cut-off maths runs on the race clock wherever the start resolves, which also cancels recorder clock skew (a `cutoff_clock` limit is `cutoffClockMin - startClockMin` off that same stamp, so both sides of the comparison shift together). **The gate the entry named no longer exists**: `elapsed` does not exclude auto-paused time, because there has been no auto-pause layer since moving time became a track-derived post-run metric — `run_screen.dart` says so and `RunRecorder.pause()` has exactly one caller, `_toggleManualPause`. So no product call about auto-pause semantics was needed, and labelling the tile "Moving" would have been a fresh lie; it is labelled **Timer**, muted and relabelled "Timer, last fix" under staleness — qualify, not withhold, matching the recent-pace tile and distinct from the projections (cut-off verdict, motion) that withhold because they extrapolate. A terminal run measures its race clock to `concluded_at`; one that concluded before that marker existed **withholds** the tile rather than ticking on. **The watch rail does not share the bug**: `watch_core::record`'s `elapsed_s()` is `now_s - start_s` with `moving_s` a separate accumulator, and `cutoff_snapshot` already feeds the cut-off the race clock — no port owed.
- [x] **Local Playwright is unusable when several agents share the checkout, and the failure is worse than the documented one.** Closed 2026-08-19 ([decisions § 697](../architecture/decisions.md)) — the build-matches-tree assertion § 631 left owed. `/@fs/` only proved the reused server's `fs.allow` admits our path; a server rooted in an ancestor (or with a widened allow list) answers `200` for our file while serving its own copy of everything the app imports. `dev_server_guard.ts` now compares CONTENT through Vite's `?raw` route — the file by absolute path and the file at the same path relative to the server's own root must both match disk — over this tree's own uncommitted `apps/web/src` sources, falling back to a fixed module on a clean tree. A mismatch names the file; anything inconclusive (404 on both routes, a non-Vite server, an unreachable port) still only warns. Pinned by `dev_server_guard.test.ts` (10 cases: foreign root, stale same-path copy, a file the served tree lacks, a differing copy, each inconclusive branch, the git probe-set derivation), which runs under the web unit suite because every Playwright config ignores `tests-e2e/fixtures/**`. The `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` half of the symptom list was already named in `docs/testing/testing.md` by § 631. Original text: `playwright.config.ts` sets `reuseExistingServer: !CI`, so a dev server started from another worktree serves *its* bundle — and when that server dies mid-suite the result is `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`, so unrelated specs fail in ways indistinguishable from real breaks. Three rounds in this batch lost cycles to it. The harness guard should assert the served build matches the working tree, and the symptom list in `docs/testing/testing.md` should name the connection-refused case, not just the stale bundle.

## New, surfaced by round 7 (2026-08-18)

Each of these was found while closing something else and was deliberately left
rather than tacked onto an unrelated change.

- [x] **`hasSessionDraft` disagreed between web and Dart on a JSON array.** Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 662](../architecture/decisions.md)) — one `isJsonObject` predicate on web, matching Dart's `is Map` and the RPC's `jsonb_typeof(...) = 'object'`. It was worse than a latent divergence: a **fourth, looser copy** lived in `fetchGymSessionDraft`, whose PostgREST `.not(..., 'is', null)` filter also matched JSON null and arrays (a jsonb `null` under a key is not SQL NULL), so the row it returned was adopted as the draft and `replaceGymSets` ran over a workout the other two rails counted as performed; that filter is now a prefilter and the helper decides. `stripSessionDraft` separately spread an array bag into an index-keyed object that `saveDraftAsIs` wrote back. Pinned on all three rails, each verified to discriminate by temporarily loosening the other two implementations. **The "unreachable in production" note in the original entry was half wrong**: every app writer emits an object, but `metadata` is unconstrained jsonb and `backup.dart`'s restore passes a user-supplied archive's bag through to the server verbatim, as can any row owner via PostgREST.
- [x] **`OfflineSyncStore.rewriteAll()` still returns silently on a null `dir`.** Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 663](../architecture/decisions.md)). Filed as "one severity down" because it drops a cache fill rather than a user write — true of the data, wrong about the state: all eight `replaceFromServer` overrides rebuild `rowsById` from the fetch *before* calling down, so a silent return left the screen showing rows nothing on disk agreed with. The gate is therefore the literal first statement of every override as well as of `rewriteAll`. **The predicted ripple through ~19 test files did not exist** — all of them already `init()` with an `overrideDirectory`, so no existing test changed and none swallows the new `StateError`. Auditing the callers found one real reach: `gym_screen._initRoutines` set `_routineStoreReady = true` after *catching* a failed `init()`, offering the Routines destination over a directoryless store where every write refuses; it reads `_routineStore.dir` now. New `architecture_guards_test.dart` group asserts `requireInitialised` is the FIRST statement of each override and that at least 8 exist (7 since [§ 707](../architecture/decisions.md) deleted `LocalSessionStore`), so a re-ordering or a new store fails the PR.
- [x] **A failed `markManySynced` still over-reports the deferral.** Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 664](../architecture/decisions.md)). The catch is split, and the second branch gets **no disclosure at all** — verified rather than assumed: `markManySynced` runs only after `saveRunsBatch` returned, so the runs are already on the server with the `failed` set excluded; `LocalRunStore` mutates `_syncedIds` in memory before awaiting the disk write, so a persist failure costs only this device's record, the next cold start re-reads them as unsynced, and the re-push upserts on `id`/`external_id` onto the same rows. `importStatusCloudPushDeferred` claims the upload did not go through, which is false here, so reusing it would have been the wrong reuse and a sibling key would have been inventing a message to fill a branch.
- [x] **The Strava / CSV / backup-ZIP import paths still cannot be driven end-to-end in a widget test.** Closed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 664](../architecture/decisions.md)). `pickFilesFn` is the fourth `...Fn` constructor seam on `ImportScreen`, defaulting to the real `FilePicker.pickFiles`, backed by 15 behavioural tests covering each path's own parsing and error handling. **It found the data-loss bug it existed to find**: Strava leaves `Filename` empty for every manually-entered or indoor activity, and `_parseStravaZipBytes` `continue`d past those rows before any counter — so a migrating runner's whole treadmill history vanished from an import that reported "0 runs" with no failures and no explanation. Web already imports those rows trackless off the CSV's own numbers (`importOne` guards inside), so this was a parity gap, not a product choice. Fixed at the root; the existing test asserting the drop was named `row with empty filename is silently skipped` — encoding the bug in its own title — and was replaced rather than relaxed. Also fixed: `_importStrava` dereferenced the picked path with a bare `!` where both siblings guarded, showing a null-check crash on a path-less pick.
- [x] **No index on `gym_workouts (user_id, metadata->>'routine_id')`** — measured and **rejected**; no index added ([decisions § 665](../architecture/decisions.md)). The RPC is `security invoker`, so the query runs under RLS, and the planner may not evaluate a non-leakproof qual below an RLS security qual: `jsonb_object_field_text` is not `LEAKPROOF`, so `(metadata ->> 'routine_id') = $1` stays a heap `Filter` and only `user_id` reaches `Index Cond` whatever the index is on. Over 60,000 synthetic rows the existing `gym_workouts_user` index reads 1,278 heap blocks / 1,296 buffers in 1.7 ms; the proposed partial expression index reads 1,277 / 1,300 in 2.5 ms — strictly worse, plus a write cost on every gym workout. A GIN `@>` index is ignored outright (`jsonb_contains` is not leakproof either). The only usable shape is a promoted plain `routine_id uuid` column (154 heap blocks / 158 buffers / 0.37 ms) — a column-vs-bag call, not an index one, and not taken yet since 1.7 ms on a page-open path does not earn a `runs`-style promotion plus its batched backfill. Revisit when a typical account reaches five figures of `gym_workouts`. The general rule is now in [conventions.md § Activity-table column checklist](../architecture/conventions.md).

### Residues from the round-7 fixes (2026-08-18)

- [x] **The mobile gym resume-card scan still tests the draft marker for mere presence.** Fixed 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 681](../architecture/decisions.md)). The three Dart copies of the predicate are now one `hasGymSessionDraft` in `lib/gym_session_draft.dart`, asking the JSON-object question the RPC, web and `routine_history.dart` all ask. "Harmless today" was true of the data and false of the offer: the card advertised "Workout in progress · Resume" for a row the routine history counts as performed, and the resume then landed in the fresh-start fallback. Pinned by a widget case over an array / string / number / null marker (verified failing against the old condition) plus a helper suite; mirrored to the iOS twin.
- [x] **Web's Strava importer is lenient where mobile is now strict.** — DONE 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 676](../architecture/decisions.md)). The decision moved into the pure `integrations/strava_track_member.ts`: a `Filename` naming a member the archive does not hold, or one in a format neither parser reads, throws into the `ImportFailureReport`; an empty `Filename` still imports trackless, and a failed gunzip stays lenient on purpose (the helper cannot tell a corrupt member from a browser with no `DecompressionStream`). Both platforms' messages were re-worded so `classifyImportFailure` buckets them as `unparseable` rather than `unknown`.
- [x] **`LocalSessionStore` has no production consumer.** Closed 2026-08-24 by deleting it ([decisions § 707](../architecture/decisions.md)). It was never wired **and never had been** — `session_detail_screen.dart` carried no import to it in the very commit (`19e377e6`) that added the store and the mobile follow-along runner together, and `sessions_screen.dart` has always read `fetchSessionPlans` from the server; four docs said the runner was "backed by" it anyway, inheriting that commit's own message. Wiring it up was checked against the spec rather than assumed: `session_planner.md` does require an offline runner, but P2 and P3 shipped without it and P4's remainder does not need it, so the offline slice is design of record with nothing pulling on it — and the store as written already lagged the schema it would cache (`createLocal` mints no `club_id`, added by the P3 club-template slice). Deleted whole: the store, `StoredSessionPlan` / `StoredSessionBlock` / `StoredSessionItem`, its suite, its `offline_store_wipe.dart` registration, its row in the crash-atomic-write guard's store list, and the wipe registry test's expectation; the sign-out wipe still covers the remaining four screen-owned stores and the two guards that enumerate stores were moved in lockstep, not relaxed. `ApiClient.createSessionPlan` / `deleteSessionPlan` stay — a typed client's surface is its API, ~10 of its methods already have no mobile caller, and both are covered by the package's integration test. `session_planner.md § Persistence` now says the offline store is unbuilt.
- [ ] **The seven `replaceFromServer` overrides repeat their gate line by line.** (Eight until `LocalSessionStore` was deleted, [§ 707](../architecture/decisions.md).) A `replaceResident(build)` base-class template would remove the repetition, but the overrides differ in signature and in how they read `rowsById` while building, so a behaviour-preserving rewrite of seven cache-fill paths is a much larger blast radius than the bug warranted ([§ 663](../architecture/decisions.md)). The architecture guard enforces the duplication rather than leaving it to convention (its floor moved 8 → 7 with the deletion, so it still fails on a re-ordering or a new store), which buys the durability without the risk — **the trigger is the count GROWING**. A store leaving takes the condition further away, not closer: reach for the template when the next one lands, not because one went.

### Surfaced by round 8 (2026-08-18)

- [x] **The web backup writer has the same undisclosed per-track swallow mobile just fixed.** — DONE 2026-08-18 ([decisions § 675](../architecture/decisions.md)). `buildBackupZip` returns a `BackupArchive` and stamps `complete` + `incomplete` into `manifest.json` in the Go writer's vocabulary; `/settings/account` discloses the shortfall on download and carries an archive's own `complete: false` through restore. The same pass found web's `createBackup` sharing § 668's actual root cause — unranged `runs` + `routes` selects clamped to 1000 rows — and paged both, and stopped the restore nulling `track_url` / re-homing `hr_series_url` when it uploads no blob.
- [x] **`parity.md`'s whole iOS "Sync and backup" column reads `✗`.** Closed 2026-08-24 ([decisions § 707](../architecture/decisions.md)) — settled at the level the code can settle, without a device. The column could not have been "right": `parity.md` marked "Backup ZIP restore (lossless)" iOS `✓` under Import while marking the same `BackupService` `✗` under Sync and backup. Nothing obstructs iOS on that path — no `Platform` gate on either tile, its handler, the containing section (`runStore != null` is its only condition), the screen, or the screen that pushes it; no method channel; `archive` is pure Dart and `file_picker` / `share_plus` / `path_provider` each declare iOS; `Info.plist` already carries the photo-library keys `file_picker` transitively forces and no entitlement is owed for a document picker or a share sheet; even the background-sync row is wired both ways (`registerProcessingTask` against a declared `BGTaskSchedulerPermittedIdentifiers`). So both `✗` ("not started") and `✓` ("working end-to-end") were false, and all eight rows now read `Partial` with the matrix's own wired-but-unverified notes idiom. `apps/mobile_ios/CLAUDE.md`'s omitted-tiles list is gone — it cited a `settings_screen.dart` comment that no longer exists, and all five tiles it named render on both apps out of the one shared file; `docs/ops/backup_restore.md` no longer calls the contradiction unresolved. The device run itself stays owed, recorded once on the iOS-verification item above rather than per-row.
- [x] **The challenges feature has no rows in `docs/product/parity.md` at all.** Closed 2026-08-18 — seven rows added to the Social section (create, join/leave, goal progress, on-pace hint, individual board, club-vs-club board, completion badge), every cell verified against the surface that renders it. Create is the one gap the exercise found: `SocialService.createChallenge` exists on mobile with **no caller anywhere**, so authoring is web-only and the row says so ([decisions § 686](../architecture/decisions.md)). Create, join, leave, goal progress, the on-pace hint, individual and club-vs-club boards, and the completion badge all ship on web and both mobile twins, and none of them has ever had a matrix row — which is exactly the silent drift the matrix exists to make visible. Surfaced while adding the standing-card row ([decisions § 615 amendment](../architecture/decisions.md)). Add them as a group rather than one at a time; `apps/web/src/routes/challenges/` and `challenge_detail_screen.dart` / `challenges_screen.dart` are the two ends to walk.
- [x] **§ 653's stated `in`-filter failure mode does not reproduce locally.** Closed 2026-08-18 by amending § 653 in place with a measured correction ([decisions § 685](../architecture/decisions.md)): the boundary is the gateway's **8 KiB request-line buffer**, not an id count — 207 uuids (8128 B) answered 200, 209 (8206 B) answered 414 — which also retires the "~15 KB" figure below. `chunk.dart` and `api_client.dart`'s badge-feed comment were corrected with it. **Still owed: nothing has been measured against the prod gateway.** The web call-site comments were corrected 2026-08-24 ([decisions § 707](../architecture/decisions.md)) — three in `core/data.ts`, plus `social/feed_merge.ts`'s header and its `mergeProfilePages` doc, and the `data.test.ts` guard whose own test name asserted the retired form; each now says the read is lost past the request-line budget and that which way the gateway refuses is a property of the deployment. It records that an over-long PostgREST `in` filter "returns an empty result rather than an error"; measured against the local stack while closing [§ 667](../architecture/decisions.md), the DELETE is refused with a **414 URI Too Long** past roughly 200 ids (~15 KB of URL) and answers 204 below it. Both failures lose the whole operation, so no chunking decision changes — but the mechanism is a property of the gateway in front of PostgREST rather than of the code, and § 653's wording is now the only place still asserting the empty-200 form as general. Worth confirming which form prod's gateway produces before any future work leans on either.

## deno.lock workspace-section drift (2026-08-24)

- [x] **Ask whether `deno.lock` needs a `workspace` section at all.** **Answered 2026-08-25 — no, it records one wherever the lock sits inside the npm workspace, so `sync:deno-lock` stays** ([decisions § 730](../architecture/decisions.md)). Closed as a question answered rather than as work done: nothing was built, and the measurement is what justifies keeping the guard instead of an unexamined assumption that it must be kept. `parity-types` now fails on a stale one ([decisions § 706](../architecture/decisions.md)), Dependabot cannot regenerate it, and the auto-merge job only queues on the required checks — so each weekly minor/patch `chore(deps)` PR now waits for someone to run `pnpm sync:deno-lock` and push. That recurring cost is **accepted, not pending**: having CI do the sync and commit onto the bot branch was refused outright in [§ 711](../architecture/decisions.md), and the reasons are stronger than a token scope — a `GITHUB_TOKEN`-authored push starts no workflow run, so `--auto` would wait forever for a `CI gate` context that can never appear, and the durable credential that would fix that carries `contents: write`, which is also what publishes a GitHub Release and so reaches `release-web.yml` / `release-backend.yml` / `release-ios.yml` in production. (The claim that `dependabot-auto-merge.yml` "runs with a read-only token today" was wrong in both directions: it already declares `contents: write`, and that is not what blocks the automation.) § 711's closing question — whether deno records the section at all when the lock does not sit at the workspace root — was the filed experiment, and it was run (deno 2.9.3). **A copy staged at `apps/backend/deno.lock`, checked with the same `deno check --no-remote --node-modules-dir=none` the sync script runs, comes back with all four sections**: `redirects`, `remote`, `version`, `workspace`, the `workspace` half carrying the same 32 specs, the same `members: ["apps/web"]` and the root `overrides` block — byte-for-byte the baseline's shape. Adding a sibling `apps/backend/deno.json` naming `{"lock": "./deno.lock"}` changes nothing beyond a `"lock" field can only be specified in the workspace root deno.json file` warning. The mechanism was established rather than inferred: a third variant removed the root `package.json` — the one declaring `workspaces` — from above the nested lock and left everything else identical, and deno then wrote `["redirects", "remote", "version"]` and no `workspace` section at all. So the section is a function of an npm workspace existing anywhere *above* the lock, not of the lock sitting at its root; relocating within the repo cannot remove it, and only taking the lock out of the npm workspace entirely would — which is not on the table, since the lock's load-bearing half is the Edge Functions' integrity pins the Supabase CLI writes at the repo root. The guard, the weekly chore and the bot problem therefore all stand, on the terms above.

## Surfaced by the #789 mobile runners-nearby build (2026-08-21)

- [ ] **Deploy gate: turn the runners-nearby flag on.** The whole surface now exists on every client — backend reader, web list + area setter, and (as of 2026-08-21) the mobile nearby list + `discoverable_nearby` opt-in + coarse-area setter — and every one of them is inert because the flag defaults off ([decisions § 270](../architecture/decisions.md) + [§ 701](../architecture/decisions.md)). What remains is a **deploy/config step, not code**: obtain owner + CISO/counsel sign-off on exposing person-location (Art 9-adjacent), then set `PUBLIC_ENABLE_NEARBY_RUNNERS` for the web env and pass `--dart-define=ENABLE_NEARBY_RUNNERS=true` to the mobile release builds. Flip web and mobile **together** — a runner who opts in on the phone becomes discoverable to opted-in web viewers immediately, since the gate is client-side render/read only and the eligibility rules live in the SECURITY DEFINER reader. Reviewers: the code path to read is `discoverable_runners_near` in migration `20270424000005` (every filter, coarse bucket output, reciprocity), `apps/web/src/lib/social/nearby_flag.ts`, and `apps/mobile_android/lib/nearby_flag.dart`.
- [x] **`ENABLE_NEARBY_RUNNERS` is the fourth inline copy of the same fail-closed flag parse on mobile.** Closed 2026-08-24 as the matched cross-platform change it had to be ([decisions § 709](../architecture/decisions.md)). `apps/mobile_android/lib/env_flag.dart#isTruthyFlagValue` is the new Dart twin of web's `core/env_flag.ts`, registered as a parity pair in both registries; all four mobile gates and the three web gates that were *also* carrying private copies (`off_route_alert.ts`, `cycle_plan_flag.ts`, `weigh_in_flag.ts` — this item's premise that every web `PUBLIC_*` gate already routed through the canonical parser was itself wrong) now delegate to their platform's canonical parser. `offRouteEscalationEnabled` and `adaptiveFitnessGateEnabled` keep their names and delegate on both sides symmetrically, so neither registered pair diverges or changes test count. `_weighInGate` was the real defect and is fixed: a widget test pins that `WEIGH_IN_GATE=yes` now opens the weigh-in sheet, and it fails against the old parse. Both suites carry a source guard against a ninth copy, and the house rule is in `conventions.md § Feature gates`.
- [x] **Three mobile deploy gates were unreachable in a release build.** Closed 2026-08-24 in the same round ([decisions § 709](../architecture/decisions.md)). § 701 recorded that `OFF_ROUTE_ESCALATION_ENABLED` and `ADAPTIVE_FITNESS_GATE` were absent from `main.dart`'s `String.fromEnvironment` bridge — and since release builds never load `.env.development` ([§ 13](../architecture/decisions.md)), neither could ever have been flipped on however a deploy was configured. Verified, and `WEIGH_IN_GATE` was in the same state; the audit found `USDA_FDC_API_KEY` (so the second food-search source was dead in every shipped build) and `TILE_URL_TEMPLATE` ([§ 68](../architecture/decisions.md)) too. All five are bridged, and `env_flag_test.dart` now scans every `dotenv.env[...]` read under `lib/` rather than asserting one flag at a time — `nearby_flag_test.dart`'s per-flag version passed the entire time the other three were broken. Passing the defines in `release-android.yml` / iOS `dart_defines.json` remains the deploy-time step each flag's own sign-off unlocks; `apps/mobile_android/deployment.md` lists them.
- [x] **The mobile nearby list has no "open Settings" affordance in its empty state.** ~~Web's empty card links to `/settings/preferences`; the mobile empty state names the path in prose instead, because `PeopleScreen` is embedded in `SocialScreen` and holds neither the `Preferences` nor the `SettingsSyncService` that `SettingsPreferencesScreen` requires.~~ Closed 2026-08-24 by building the seam rather than the thread-through: `settings_destination.dart` names the pushable Settings sub-screens and `HomeScreen` drains the parked intent with the dependencies it already holds ([decisions § 710](../architecture/decisions.md)). The nearby empty state now carries an "Open Preferences" button and its body no longer recites the path. A second caller went with it — `IntensityCard`'s age-estimated-max-HR caveat, which showed run-detail's exact copy with nothing to tap because it holds only a `SettingsSyncService`. Two candidates were refused on purpose and the reasons are in the ADR: `run_detail_screen.dart` already holds its own dependencies, and the nutrition screens await the pushed route to refresh on return, which a fire-and-forget intent cannot report.

## Surfaced while settling the iOS backup/restore contradiction (2026-08-24)

- [x] **All nine `Share.shareXFiles` call sites omit `sharePositionOrigin`, and the iOS target includes iPad.** Closed 2026-08-24 ([decisions § 714](../architecture/decisions.md)) — see the section below, which carries the measured facts. Two premises here were wrong: the reach is **21** call sites, not nine (ten `Share.share` text/link shares and two already-modernised `SharePlus.instance.share` calls go through the same `atSource:originRect` check in `FPPSharePlusPlugin.m`), and the pinned `share_plus 12.0.2` does not crash — it returns a `PlatformException` and presents no sheet at all, so the symptom on iPad was a silently dead share, not a crash.
- [x] **`parity.md` contradicts itself about what an iOS cell means.** Closed 2026-08-25 ([decisions § 739](../architecture/decisions.md)). The rule adopted is the one this entry proposed — `Partial` = wired but unverified, `✓` reserved for an observation, `✗` for genuinely absent code — stated once as a **derivation** from the Android cell inside a `<!-- parity-ios-rule -->` block, with the banner and the intentional-asymmetries copies deleted and the column key reduced to a pointer. The whole column was then swept in one pass: 268 of 337 cells moved, leaving 313 `Partial` / 11 `✗` / 13 `N/A` and no `✓`. Five rows depart from the derivation and each names its obstruction behind an `**iOS <symbol>:**` marker. **This entry's counts were right on their date** — 133 / 47 / 140 reproduces exactly on the 2026-08-24 commit — but two `✗` rows landed after it, so the base for this round measured 133 `✓` / 47 `Partial` / 142 `✗` / 15 `N/A` over 337 rows; the entry omitted the `N/A` bucket and the row total. `scripts/check_parity_ios_column.mjs` (21 unit tests) now reads the derivation table out of the document and enforces the column against it, refuses a second statement of the vocabulary anywhere in the prose, and refuses a `✓` outright; it runs in the ungated `parity-matrix` job, because `parity-types` is gated on `needs.changes.outputs.code` and a parity.md edit is a docs-only diff.

## Surfaced by the iPad share-anchor fix (2026-08-24)

- [x] **Every mobile share reached `share_plus` without a `sharePositionOrigin`, and the app ships to iPad.** Closed 2026-08-24 ([decisions § 714](../architecture/decisions.md)). All 21 call sites — nine `Share.shareXFiles`, ten `Share.share`, two `SharePlus.instance.share` — omitted the anchor while `apps/mobile_ios/ios/Runner.xcodeproj` sets `TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY = "1,2"` in all three configurations. On the pinned `share_plus` 12.0.2 the iOS plugin does not crash: it detects the empty rect and returns a `FlutterError` whose message is `sharePositionOrigin: argument must be set`, so the share throws a `PlatformException` into the call site's `catch` and **no sheet opens**. All three native rails (`shareUri`, `shareFiles`, `shareText`) pass through the same `atSource:` check, so the text shares were as broken as the file shares. Replaced by one `lib/share_sheet.dart` whose `shareFilesFrom` / `shareTextFrom` take the anchoring `BuildContext` as a required positional parameter — there is no `Rect`-taking overload and no un-anchored path — plus the migration off the deprecated `Share` statics onto `SharePlus.instance.share(ShareParams(...))`, four source guards in `architecture_guards_test.dart`, 6 derivation tests, and the byte-identical iOS mirror.
- [ ] **Device gate: verify the iPad share sheet on real hardware.** Mac- and iPad-only, and nothing above was observed on a device — the fix was derived from the plugin's own `FPPSharePlusPlugin.m`, the Xcode target's device family and the workspace lockfile pin, then host-tested. Owed: on an iPad (and under **Split View / Stage Manager** specifically), confirm each of the four share shapes actually presents — a file share from a modal card sheet (run / route / period / certificate PNG), a file share from a full screen (CSV export, account backup, GPX/KML route export), a text share after a dialog has popped (route detail "share link"), and a text share from a plain button (live link, coach invite, recap). The Stage Manager case is the one with a known residual: `share_plus` compares the anchor against `controller.view.frame` rather than `bounds`, so a correct view-local rect can still fail containment when the Flutter view is not anchored at the screen origin. If it does, the fix is upstream (a plugin issue/PR), not another parameter — do not paper over it with a guessed offset. Nothing ships disabled on this; the current behaviour on iPad is strictly worse than the fixed one.

## Surfaced by running round 12's export-hub e2e lane (2026-08-25)

- [x] **The queued export rail's CORS preflight was refused, so no browser could enqueue** — closed 2026-08-25 ([decisions § 725](../architecture/decisions.md)). `OPTIONS /v1/export/jobs` answered 405 with no allow headers; the web client posts from the site's origin and never the worker's, so the POST never left the browser and the worker logged nothing. Exact-origin allowlist (a wildcard is invalid on a credentialed request), sharing `LIVEHUB_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`.
- [x] **A signed Storage URL was fronted with the project base URL instead of the storage API path**, so every queued-export download answered 404 — closed 2026-08-25 ([decisions § 725](../architecture/decisions.md)). Storage returns `/object/sign/...` with no `/storage/v1` prefix; both shapes are now accepted.
- [ ] **`LIVEHUB_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` must be set in the worker's production environment before the queued export rail is usable.** It already gates the live hub's WebSocket origin check, and now also gates the export endpoints' CORS. Empty means no CORS headers, which is correct for a server-to-server deployment and fail-closed for the browser one — so a deploy that forgets it leaves the export button silently inert. Deploy-time checklist item for `apps/job_worker/deployment.md`, not code.
- [x] **Both boot scripts inherit the workstation's `GOTOOLCHAIN`.** `start-exporthub.sh` and `start-livehub.sh` both run a bare `go build`; on a machine with `go env GOTOOLCHAIN=local` and a Go older than `go.mod` requires, the lane fails at boot with `go.mod requires go >= 1.26.6`. CI installs the right Go so it does not bite there, and the failure is loud rather than silent, but a one-line `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` in both scripts would remove a local-only stumble. **Done 2026-08-25** — set on the `go build` in both scripts, with the reason as a comment above it.
- [x] **Seven nutrition Playwright specs fail on any machine whose local calendar date differs from UTC's.** Found 2026-08-25 while running round 12's named specs, and confirmed to fail identically on an untouched `main` worktree against a freshly reset database, so it is neither this round's doing nor accumulated test residue. The mechanism: the specs build day-relative timestamps with `new Date(y, m, d, h, 0)` — the **Node process's** local zone — while `playwright.config.ts` pins the browser to `timezoneId: 'UTC'`. Run at 23:35 EDT the seeded entries carry a `started_at` on the local day, which the page (reading the UTC day) renders as *yesterday*, so nothing appears and the visibility assertions fail. The affected specs are exactly the ones that seed a day-relative `food_log` row: `daily-view` (grouping), `diary-date-navigation` (both), `dynamic-budget-journey`, `extended-nutrients`, `nutrition-day-journey`, `nutrition` (water goal). CI never sees it because hosted runners are UTC, so local and UTC dates always agree there. The durable fix is for the specs to build their timestamps in the same zone the browser is pinned to rather than in the runner's — not to widen a timeout or pin the suite to a timezone, which would only move the window. Worth doing because a suite that is green for sixteen hours a day and red for the other eight trains people to re-run. **Done 2026-08-25** ([decisions.md § 728](../architecture/decisions.md)) — `tests-e2e/fixtures/dates.ts` now builds every day-relative timestamp in the zone the config pins the browser to, the three separately-re-derived `noonUtcToday` helpers collapsed onto it, and `fixtures/dates.test.ts` scans the whole `tests-e2e` tree for local-zone day derivation so a new spec cannot re-derive it. The hand-enumerated list above missed one: `meal-detail` was affected too, and `dynamic-budget-journey` + `extended-nutrients` were already correct. Verified by running the pre-fix specs under `TZ=Pacific/Kiritimati` (6 of 32 red) and `TZ=Pacific/Midway` (the evening-seeded grouping test red) and the converted ones under both (32/32 green).

- [x] **Four non-nutrition Playwright specs still derive a day-relative date in the runner's zone** — **CLOSED 2026-08-25** ([decisions.md § 738](../architecture/decisions.md)), and the entry was wrong in three ways. Round 14 (#816) had already converted the three `dashboard/*` specs without updating this entry, so only `cross-cutting/dashboard-journey.spec.ts` was still listed from its four; and the count was never right — `settings/gear-backfill.spec.ts` carried a `not yet converted` reason from the first commit and appears in neither the enumeration nor the total (§ 728's prose names "the dashboard, gear and dashboard-journey surfaces", its numeral says four). **The `datetime-local` exemption did not survive**: `RunEditor` submits `new Date(startedAt).toISOString()` and the control holds no zone, so the browser resolves it in UTC — swept hour by hour, the submitted run lands outside the goal week the browser computes for 4/168 UTC hours in `America/New_York`, 11 in `Pacific/Midway` and 14 in `Pacific/Kiritimati`, where both goal assertions fail. **And the "deliberate" half was not**: the three coaching specs shifted LOCAL days (168 mismatches per zone-year in every DST zone tested, 0 in the three without DST), and `routes/segments.spec.ts` built local Jan 1 and read it back in UTC — `1993-12-31` instead of `1994-01-01` for every zone east of Greenwich, every day of the year, not only across New Year. Nine of the ten entries are converted; `plans/calendar.spec.ts` is the one genuine exemption (its formatters hash identically across eight zones over all 366 days of 2026), joined by `fixtures/plan-today.ts` for its explicit `timeZone: 'UTC'`. Four holes closed in the scan itself, each proved to fire: only one of the four lane configs was checked for the UTC pin, `getTimezoneOffset` was unbanned, a zoneless date-time literal handed to `new Date` / `Date.parse` was unbanned, and `.mjs` under `tests-e2e` was unscanned. Every converted spec re-run green under the ambient zone plus UTC−11 and UTC+14.

- [x] **`gear-lifecycle-journey.spec.ts` fails locally on the `service_role` read, never in CI — same workstation CLI divergence as the two known pgtap failures.** **CLOSED 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 777](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The filed instance was already fixed** — [§ 735](../architecture/decisions.md)'s round landed `tests-e2e/fixtures/db-read.ts` and converted both `gear_with_distance` rollup reads, so neither line this entry quotes exists on `main`. What survived is the filing's real point, and the class is much larger and differently shaped than filed. The guard shipped beside that fixture banned **one spelling**: a `??` with a literal default on the same line as an `expect`. An AST sweep of `apps/web/tests-e2e` for the actual defect — an awaited Supabase read whose `error` is neither destructured nor consulted, whose data is then judged by an `expect` — found **521 assertions across 397 reads in 147 files**, in three waves the old scan could not see: 368 absorbed the absence into a value (`?.`, `?? 0`, `?? []`), 51 needed no absorber because the read's own `null` was already what the matcher wanted, and 102 forced it with a `!`, a cast, or a null `count` from a `head: true` probe. **62 of the 521 PASS on a read that never reached the table**, which is the headline: 30 are `expect(row).toBeNull()` in the specs that exist to prove a boundary holds (`private-rls-negative`, `cross-user-mutations`, `public-runs-view`, `follow-engage-block`, `people-follow`), and the rest include the Art 17 cascade assertions in `account-deletion.spec.ts` / `account-data-rights-journey.spec.ts`, where a failed service-role read supplies the `[]` that `expect(cm.data ?? [], 'coach_messages must cascade').toEqual([])` wants. The workstation CLI divergence this entry describes fails **every** such read at once, so it costs an hour on the four loud specs and nothing visible on the sixty-two quiet ones. All 397 reads now go through `readRow` / `readRows` / `readMaybeRow` / `readCount`; the two `admin.auth.admin.getUserById` reads keep a raw shape because GoTrue reports a deleted user with a 404 rather than a null row, and now assert that status before reading the absence. `db-read.test.ts` bans the shape rather than the costume — a dropped-error binding may not appear inside an `expect` at all — and is proved to fail on the pre-fix tree (138 files, including this spec) and on the pre-§ 735 tree (naming lines 175 and 265, the two quoted here), and to pass now with no exemptions. `tsc -p tsconfig.tests-e2e.json` exits 0; the guard runs in the existing `test:unit` glob, so no new CI job. **The underlying CLI divergence is NOT fixed** — the four specs still fail locally — but the failure now names the read instead of the feature. The 147 converted specs are owed a Playwright run at integration. Original filing below. Found 2026-08-25 while verifying round 13's integrated branch, and **confirmed to fail identically on an untouched `origin/main` worktree**, so it is neither round 13's doing nor test residue. The failure is precise and worth recording because it reads like a broken derived cache and is not one: the UI assertions immediately above it PASS (`.gear-meta` renders `6.0 / 500` and `1 run`, so the `gear_with_distance` rollup is correct and the trigger fired), and only the backend cross-check fails — `admin.from('gear_with_distance').select(...).single()` returns no row, so `Number(rollup?.total_distance_m ?? 0)` collapses to `0` and asserts against `6000`. That is a `service_role` grant/visibility artifact of this workstation's Supabase CLI 2.109.1 against CI's pinned 2.84.2, the same family as `coach_roster_summary` test 10 and `donations_status_lock`'s `fundraiser_totals`, which fail locally for the same reason. **The trap is the shape of the failure**: an `.single()` that returns nothing is indistinguishable from a zero once `?? 0` absorbs it, so the assertion accuses the rollup instead of the read. Whoever fixes the CLI divergence should re-run all four together; whoever does not should not spend an hour on the gear triggers. A `.single()` whose error is asserted rather than discarded would make the real cause visible at the point of failure.

## Surfaced by the iOS parity-column sweep (2026-08-25)

- [x] **Spoken cues stopped on iOS the moment the run screen backgrounded, because `UIBackgroundModes` omitted `audio`** — closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 742](../architecture/decisions.md)). The premise held exactly as filed: `audio_cues.dart` calls `setIosAudioCategory(playback, [mixWithOthers, duckOthers], voicePrompt)` and the plist declared only `location` and `processing`. `<string>audio</string>` added, with the reason as a plist comment. **What the sweep could not have known is why nothing caught it:** `group('iOS Info.plist')` in `architecture_guards_test.dart` had twelve tests reading these very files behind `if (!file.existsSync()) return;`, and `mobile_ios` — the only package where they exist — is absent from the `test-packages` job's `melos exec --scope=` list, so not one of those assertions had ever executed. `apps/mobile_ios/deployment.md` had meanwhile said "Background Modes → Location updates + **Audio (for TTS)**" all along; the requirement was written down and the declaration file never followed. Replaced by `scripts/check_ios_native_declarations.mjs` + the `ios-native-declarations` CI job, which derives the requirement from the `playback` call rather than listing it, and verifies 43 declarations the same way. Still owed: the device run — background audio is not host-testable, and no Mac has been in the loop.
- [x] **`Runner.entitlements` carried no `com.apple.developer.aps-environment`, so an iOS build could not register for APNs** — closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 742](../architecture/decisions.md)). Declared, as `$(APS_ENVIRONMENT)` rather than a literal: one entitlements file serves all three Runner build configurations, and the sandbox and production APNs hosts reject each other's tokens, so `APS_ENVIRONMENT` is defined per configuration in the pbxproj (`development` on Debug + Profile, `production` on Release — what `flutter build ipa --release` archives). Pinning `development` and trusting the distribution export to rewrite it is the shape that ships a TestFlight build whose every push answers `BadDeviceToken`; an undefined setting expands to the literal string instead, which no profile carries, so the failure is loud at signing. The entry was right that this is a declaration and not a credential — the pubspec comment and `native_push.md` both said otherwise and are corrected. **Not a Mac-side action after all:** Xcode writes this key, but so can a text editor, and the guard now requires it. Still gated on the operator's Firebase / APNs artifacts for delivery, and on a signed build for verification (`codesign -d --entitlements` on the exported IPA).

## Surfaced by round 15's pgtap refusal-assertion audit (2026-08-25)

- [x] **A third vacuous refusal existed and only CI could see it.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 741](../architecture/decisions.md)). `rls_fitness_snapshots_test`'s "anon cannot read fitness_snapshots" reads a table that **test 5 of the same file has just emptied**, so it passed whether or not the policy existed. The workstation audit scored it *killed* because the shared dev database held 41 committed `fitness_snapshots` rows left by Playwright and api_client runs — pgtap never rolled them back because they were never in its transaction — and with RLS removed those rows failed the assertion, which looks exactly like a policy working. On CI the table is genuinely empty (`seed.sql` files one row as a source-gate self-test and deletes it again at line 2597), so the mutation survived and the guard reported it on its first run. **The lesson is not about this test: a mutation operator is only as honest as the database it runs against, and ambient test debris can invert its verdict.**
- [x] **Two private-route refusal assertions had no row to refuse.** Closed 2026-08-25 ([decisions § 741](../architecture/decisions.md)). `rls_route_conditions_test`'s tests 10 and 13 asserted a non-owner and anon read no `route_conditions` rows on the private route while no fixture ever filed one there. **The policy itself holds** — a real report filed on that route by its owner is invisible to both, verified directly — so this was a vacuous test, not a leak. The owner now files that report and reads it back, and both assertions die under the mutation that previously left them green.
- [x] **One pgtap negative pinned neither a SQLSTATE nor a message.** Closed 2026-08-25. `activities_view_windowed`'s view-write refusal passed `null` for both, so any error satisfied it; its comment guessed 42501 or 0A000 and the real code is 55000 (a UNION view is not auto-updatable, and the rewriter answers before the privilege check). Pinned, with the missing INSERT grant asserted separately against the catalogue since no write attempt can ever observe it.
- [x] **The definer-relation refusals hold too — and the premise's boundary was wrong.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 745](../architecture/decisions.md)). A second mutation operator now replaces the relation itself with a permissive definition inside a savepoint, one per relation in `apps/backend/scripts/pgtap_definer_neutralisers.mjs`, each keeping every predicate that says *which* rows the assertion asked about and dropping every predicate that says *whether the caller may see them*. **Nothing was vacuous: 50 of the 51 assertions the new operator measures die under it, and the one survivor is a real zero** (an age band cannot contain a runner who has no age, with its positive control in the assertion two above it). So §741's finding holds a second time — no RLS hole was hiding behind an unmeasured refusal. **The finding is about the boundary, not the tests**: the split is not definer-vs-invoker, which is what `prosecdef` records, but whether the relation carries an access predicate of its own — and **eight of the 24 relations that needed a replacement are `SECURITY INVOKER`**, filtering with a `= auth.uid()` or an `is_public = true` written into the body. `search_public_events` is the sharpest: with RLS fully bypassed the base-table read returns the members-only event while the RPC still returns nothing. The old `baseTables` filter was itself doing damage beyond the definer class: it excluded two `activities`-view reads the *first* operator could have measured all along, and seven more that turned out to need the second. Guard population 104 → 168. A second CI step validates each replacement against a subject its real relation provably hides, plus an end-to-end control that must kill a known-good refusal and leave a known-bad one standing.
- [x] **The vocabulary trade measured, then half-closed.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 745](../architecture/decisions.md)). Both operators were run over **all 294** zero-or-empty assertions in the suite, not just the ones worded as refusals, which is the only way to see what the selector misses. The regex selected 126 and killed 121 of them, so its **precision is 96 %**. Of the 168 it did not select, **28 died too** — the measured recall gap, and it splits cleanly. Seven unambiguous words (`never sees`, `unfindable`, `enumerate`, `exposes no`, `reads nothing`, `gets nothing`, `absent from`) close 11 of those for free: every assertion they select dies, so not one new `EXPECTED_SURVIVORS` entry was needed. The largest remaining word is **`excluded` / `excludes`: 21 assertions, roughly half access control (a declared minor, a search opt-out, a block, a members-only event) and half ordinary filters (`byday=MO excludes it`, `free filter excludes the priced class`)**. One word, two claims — so the trade does stand, quantitatively: no regex separates them, and the alternative is not a 207-entry allowlist but a **117-entry** one, since 117 of the 157 assertions outside the vocabulary legitimately survive (cascade deletes, triggers that correctly did not fire, positive DELETE controls). The durable fix is neither: an opt-in `-- refusal: <why>` comment on the line above the assertion, said once by the author where the claim is. 31 assertions carry one today, all killed. Two of the 28 stay outside deliberately — `distance is returned as coarse bucket 0` and `a long-broken streak reports current = 0` expect a zero *value*, not an empty result, and a widening mutation moves a value, so a "kill" there would mean nothing.
- [x] **A kill over an owner-scoped RPC proves a subject exists in the transaction, not that the test built one.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 751](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The premise was right and its boundary was wrong in both directions.** It is not confined to identity-only selectors: `gym_exercise_set_history('Bench Press')` carries an exercise name as its selector, the RPC normalises it, and `seed.sql` commits **six `Bench press` sets** — so that assertion was killed by committed data whether or not its fixture existed. And "seven assertions" was seven *relations*; the assertions over those seven number **eleven**, five of them `coach_roster_summary`'s alone. The `xmin` objection is an argument for scoping the right relation, not against scoping: every replacement now names a `subject` alias and reveals only that relation's rows carrying the current transaction's `xmin`, and the alias is **the relation the replacement's rows come from**, not the one the dropped predicate guarded — `run_gear rg` for `public_run_gear`, `coach_athletes ca` for the roster, `gym_sets s` for the sets view. **All 51 definer-measured assertions still die**, so every one had already filed its own subject; no pgtap fixture changed and the population is unchanged at 168 / 5 expected survivors. That the guard *would* have caught something is shown by deleting a fixture in a rolled-back transaction: the old operator still kills, the new one lets the assertion survive (demonstrated on `gym_sets` twice, `coach_athletes` and `runs`). Six of the 24 witnesses were `select 1;` and proved only that the replacement revealed the *seed* — they now file their own subject — and a third end-to-end control (a private route the seed already committed, which must survive) fails by name if any replacement loses its scoping.
- [x] **Four definer relations still have no permissive replacement, and none of their assertions is a refusal.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 751](../architecture/decisions.md)). **It was two relations, not four**: `get_event_meet_point` and `public_run_counts` *are* registered and their refusal assertions die under mutation — what they contribute is one non-refusal shape claim each (a coord-less event, an empty input array), which is a different fact from the relation lacking a replacement. The genuinely unreplaced pair is `fetch_pending_reports` (1 assertion) and `challenge_leaderboard` (1). Both stay out, with the reason recorded rather than rediscovered: the first's zero is the RPC's own `status = 'pending'` filter, so a replacement may only drop the admin gate and the assertion would survive — an `EXPECTED_SURVIVORS` entry bought with a second copy of the queue query; the second expects a zero *value* rather than an emptiness, the class § 745 already excluded. They are declared in `UNREGISTERED_DEFINER_RELATIONS`, which the `--validate-operators` phase re-derives from the suite and fails in both directions, so a third such relation is now a decision someone makes on purpose rather than a chore the guard hands them at merge.
- [x] **The same debris exposure on the FIRST mutation operator is measured but not closed.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 753](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The two counts are exactly right on a seed-only database and everything concluded from them is wrong.** 63 and 54 re-measure precisely after a `db reset`, `runs` at 9 assertions over 251 rows — but that is a census of TABLES, and the assertions carry the selectors: run all 117 candidates' own SQL against a seed-only database with RLS off, which is what the old operator's kill was worth, and **not one returns a row**. The residue was zero assertions wide, not 63. The 54 is wrong the other way — **three of them read `activities`**, a `security_invoker` view over `runs` / `gym_workouts` / `food_log`, so it is absent from the table census while being answerable by debris. And "a role change with no query to add a predicate to" is false: RLS is a set of policy EXPRESSIONS, and a policy is a query to add a predicate to. Neither proposed mechanism was needed. Operator 1 is now one extra permissive `SELECT` policy per RLS-enabled table (90 of them, 14.8 ms, created once per file and GUC-gated so it is inert outside a mutated span), revealing only the rows this transaction wrote, with the assertion running as the role the test gave it. Underneath sat a defect in § 751's own predicate: `xmin = pg_current_xact_id()::xid` matches only a top-level write, and pgtap's `lives_ok` / `throws_ok` run in a subtransaction — **11 false survivors across 9 files** on the first scoped run. A `SECURITY DEFINER` wrapper over `pg_xact_status` makes the test exact (visible + in progress = written here), and both operators now share it. Population unchanged at 168 / 5 expected survivors, no fixture changed, pgtap still 258 files / 2188 tests green.

## Surfaced by #789 round 19 (2026-08-27)

- [x] **`npm run test:unit` cannot run the web unit suite from a clean checkout, and CI fetches `tsx` over the network on every run instead.** Closed 2026-08-27 ([decisions § 764](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The premise reproduced exactly and the boundary was wrong twice.** `npm run test:unit` in a clean `npm ci` tree exits `127: tsx: command not found`, confirmed in an isolated checkout — but this is not "the one step in this job that is not hermetic": `parity-types` also pulls a Deno runtime, the Supabase CLI and a Docker stack over the network, and what is unique to `npx tsx` is that it fetches an **unpinned package with no lockfile entry and no integrity hash**. And it is not one call site: `cross-client-roundtrip` runs `npx tsx` four more times, so five in all, every one fixed by the same declaration. `tsx` is now an `apps/web` devDependency and the CI step runs the package script, so the command CI runs and the command its diagnosis says to reproduce with are one string. The addition moves no other resolution — the only new `name@version` across both lockfiles is `tsx@4.23.12`. A guard, `scripts/check_workflow_binaries.mjs`, now refuses any binary a package runner is handed unless a declared package provides it. Surfaced fixing § 757's CI failure (2026-08-27). `apps/web/package.json`'s `test:unit` invokes a bare `tsx`, which no lockfile installs — `tsx` is a declared dependency of nothing in this repo, so on a clean `npm ci` the script exits `127: tsx: command not found`. CI does not use the script: the `Web TS unit tests` step runs `npx tsx --test ...` directly, and `npx` resolves it by downloading it from the registry, which is why the lane is green while the documented command is broken. Two costs: the reproduce line the step itself prints (`npm ci && npx tsx --test ...`) is the only invocation that works, and every CI run takes an unpinned third-party binary off the network at test time — the one step in this job that is not hermetic. The durable fix is to declare `tsx` as an `apps/web` devDependency and point both the script and the CI step at the installed copy; it is a lockfile change on both `package-lock.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` plus a workflow edit, so it wants its own change rather than riding a CI hotfix.

## Surfaced by #789 round 21 integration (2026-08-28)

- [x] **A parity.md table row written without its LEADING pipe is invisible to all four guards that read the file.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 779](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The count of four is right and one of the names is not.** The four are `check_parity_ios_column.mjs`'s three pipe-gated readers plus `check_parity_matrix.dart`, as [§ 774](../architecture/decisions.md) had it; `check_doc_checkboxes.mjs` gates on nothing of the kind and `parity.md` carries zero checkboxes, so it was never one of them. The real fourth copy of the rule is `check_parity_pair_registry.mjs`'s `parseSyncerRows`, over a different table in the same job, still carrying the pre-§ 774 `slice(1, -1)` besides. **Worse than filed on the iOS guard**: `readRows` reset its header on any non-pipe line, so one such row dropped the rest of its table too — 337 rows to 329, exit 0. The detector did not need widening (the risk the filing named), it needed GFM's own state: a table opens on a header plus a delimiter of the same width and every line after it is a row. That walk lives in `scripts/markdown_lines.mjs` beside the soft-wrap folder, which had the same blind spot from the other side, with a Dart half in the matrix guard. Both guards now refuse a row they cannot grade and a pipe-leading line in no table; the matrix guard got its first test suite, seven of whose ten cases fail against the old walk.

- [x] **The test that forces `GRANDFATHER_CUTOFF` to be bumped is also the way past the guard it protects.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 775](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The premise held exactly and the proposed fix does not work as stated.** With the cutoff at `20270619` and 436 committed migrations, `isAfterCutoff` selected **0** of them — the scanned set was empty, measured. But scanning the whole tree with the cutoff ignored finds **32 violations across 30 files** (twelve `notifications_kind_check` widens `20260903_001`-`20270218_001`, thirteen `jobs_kind_chk`, four `runs`, one `rate_limits` FK), the newest at `20270410_001` — so "a frozen legacy boundary" would have to sit at `20270410`, still a date exempting 200-odd files nobody has read, and would still have to move the first time a violation shipped. So `GRANDFATHER_CUTOFF` is **gone**, not frozen: the 32 are grandfathered one at a time as `{filename, table, constraint}` in `GRANDFATHERED_VIOLATIONS`, every migration is scanned on every run (23 ms over 436), an entry exempts only what it spells, an entry matching nothing **fails** the guard, and **adding a migration needs no edit to the guard at all** — the bookkeeping edit was the bypass. An anonymous `add check (...)` gets a null key no entry can match, so it fails closed. 20 tests -> **23**, including the one the retired design made impossible (the scanned set is the whole tree and is never empty) and one that drops each of the 32 entries in turn and asserts the tree goes red. Original filing below. **The test that forces `GRANDFATHER_CUTOFF` to be bumped is also the way past the guard it protects.** Found integrating round 21, and it is the round's own theme one level up. `check_migration_online_safety.mjs` inspects only migrations NEWER than the cutoff, and `check_migration_online_safety.test.mjs`'s "GRANDFATHER_CUTOFF is the max version in the committed tree" asserts the cutoff is at least the newest migration — so every migration-bearing PR must bump it, and the bump removes that migration from the scan. The order is what decides whether the guard ran: bump after the check passes (what round 21 did, and what the cutoff's eight-round history shows every previous round did) and the migration was inspected; bump first and it never was, with both orders leaving an identical tree and an identical green test. At rest the scanned set is therefore empty by construction. The durable fix is to make the cutoff mean what its name says — a frozen legacy boundary that moves only to grandfather a *known* violation — and to scan everything after it on every run, which is what the guard already does and what nothing then needs to bump.

## Surfaced by #789 round 20 (2026-08-27)

- [x] **Ten more guards misread their own input, all latent, each with a reproduction.** **All ten CLOSED 2026-08-28** across [decisions § 773](../architecture/decisions.md) and [§ 774](../architecture/decisions.md), split five and five. "Latent" held for all ten on the committed tree, and it was the right thing to distrust: **three of the ten are false passes, not noise** — the GATT guard certifies § 410's own bug, the lambda guard reads a decoy inside the alias call's own arguments, and the pgtap guard scored a vacuous refusal *killed*, which is § 741's inversion reproduced at the instrument. The two guards § 770 never probed were probed here and **both misread too**, so the population is 17/17 measured rather than 15/17 measured and 2 assumed; `check_production_env.mjs` was the worst of the twelve, passing a Postgres connection string that Vite would have inlined into every client bundle. ORIGINAL FILING: Derived membership from the § 770 sweep: all **17** `check_*.mjs` guards were probed by running their own exported functions against a crafted input. Three were the filing, **ten more misread**, one is verified clean (`check_migration_versions.mjs`), two were not probed (`check_doc_checkboxes.mjs`, `check_production_env.mjs`). Every one of the ten exits 0 on the committed tree today, and each was checked for why — but three of the four filed items were understated in the same direction, so treat "latent" as unproven rather than safe. Each wants its own reproduction, pinning test, and — for the four with no test sibling — a CI step; that is a round, not a rider on § 770.
  - [x] **`check_workflow_binaries.mjs` — a runner whose binary is on the next physical line is dropped silently.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 773](../architecture/decisions.md)). **Held exactly as filed, and latent by measurement rather than by assumption.** `run: |` / `npx \` / `totally-unpinned-cli` parsed to zero invocations and the folded scalar was the same hole; both reproduce. `parseInvocations` now scans logical lines — a `>`-headed block folded the way YAML folds it (paragraph breaks respected, `|` left alone), then a trailing backslash joined the way the shell joins it. The committed workflows carry **9 folded scalars and 124 continuation lines**, and the invocation set is bit-for-bit identical before and after (**16**, same files, same lines), which is now a test rather than a claim. `binaryOf` still returns null silently for genuine prose, which is correct once the two continuation shapes resolve before it is asked.
  - [x] **`check_lambda_alias_sync.mjs` — reads only the first `FUNCTIONS=(…)` in the file.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 773](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The headline held; the secondary was wrong about its direction.** The per-environment array reproduces exactly (`[coach, osrm-proxy]` parsed from a script whose `preview` branch names only `coach`), and both directions now ask the right set — a Terraform alias must be in EVERY assignment to count as synced, any assignment's suffix must be declared in Terraform, and two disagreeing `NAME=` templates are reported. The 400-character window was filed as reading a stray `--name` off an unrelated command, i.e. loud noise; measured, the lazy first match means a decoy INSIDE the alias call's own arguments answers for it (`--function-name "$(aws ssm get-parameter --name live …)" --name stable` reads `live`) — a **false pass**, the sources "agreeing" on an alias the script never repoints. Second undocumented direction: `--name "live"` was captured with its quotes and reported as a rename. Both gone; the name comes off a command lexed by the new `scripts/shell_lex.mjs`. Committed script measured at 1 assignment, 1 template, 1 alias name.
  - [x] **`check_ios_native_declarations.mjs` — no blindness check on `swiftSources`.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 773](../architecture/decisions.md)). **Both halves held.** Passing `swiftSources: []` against the committed tree yields **errors 0 and 41 `[OK]` lines instead of 43**, exit 0, with nothing saying two rules stopped enforcing. The blindness check is now symmetric and its subject is DERIVED from the rule tables (`swiftRuleKeys()`), so it exists only while a rule reads Swift and covers one added tomorrow. The missing reverse direction is closed too: a `*UsageDescription` key no rule claims is an error, exempted only by the `FIXED_PLIST_KEYS` admission that already carried `NSPhotoLibraryAddUsageDescription`. All 11 declared keys are accounted for — 10 by a rule, 1 by that list.
  - [x] **`check_watch_ble_uuids.mjs` — both parsers read UUIDs out of comments.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 773](../architecture/decisions.md)). **Worse than filed: the guard does not merely fail to catch § 410, it certifies it.** "A stale UUID quoted in each file's comments agrees with itself" understates the measured result — give the firmware a live table shifted one position with the old table kept in a *trailing* comment (the characteristic scan is last-writer-wins) and a phone that never moved, and `compareTables` returns **zero errors and three `[OK]` lines**. `gatt_service` was also matched non-globally, so the `//!` module doc above the declaration wins. Both sources are now read through `scripts/comment_strip.mjs`, which tracks each language's strings honestly (Rust lifetimes are not char literals; Dart triple quotes span lines; block comments nest) and throws on what it cannot read; a second `gatt_service` throws rather than picking one. Measured: **0 UUIDs in comments** across both committed files (10 live attributes, 10 live constants), so the misread was latent.
  - [x] **`check_pgtap_refusal_assertions.mjs` — `parseTap` keys verdicts by description string.** `Map<description, ok>` over the whole mutant's TAP output, so two assertions in one file sharing a description collapse last-writer-wins and a genuinely vacuous refusal is scored *killed* on its twin's verdict — § 741's inversion pointed at the instrument. `EXPECTED_SURVIVORS` has the same collision.
