Latent: 0 duplicate descriptions across the committed pgtap tree (scanned with the guard's own `findCalls`/`literalOf`, no database needed). **Closed 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 774](../architecture/decisions.md)). Held, and wrong in BOTH directions rather than one: with the description shared, `ok` then `not ok` scores the SURVIVING (vacuous) assertion as killed — the § 741 inversion pointed at the instrument — and `not ok` then `ok` scores a genuinely killed one as a survivor. Both reproduced verbatim against `parseTap`. Every verdict is kept now and a repeated description is reported as unattributable rather than resolved by guessing, which also closes the `EXPECTED_SURVIVORS` collision by construction: an ambiguous assertion never reaches the survivor list, so no entry can excuse a second one. Latent confirmed and measured wider than filed — 0 duplicates across **2001** assertion descriptions in the **262** committed pgtap files, counting every pgtap assertion kind rather than only the refusal candidates, now pinned by a test.

The summary line was overclaiming in the same register: **"passed (95 scripts)" over 28 claims, where 14 of the 95 are `//`-prefixed documentation headers whose values are prose**; it now reports 29 claims across 81 scripts with what it could not verify printed and counted. Rewritten over `scripts/shell_lex.mjs` with the exports and the test file it never had (20 cases), plus a diagnosed step in `workflow-lint`. `cd` is now resolved against where the previous `cd` left the script, so `dev:db:reset`'s `cd ../..` means the repo root rather than being vacuously true against it, and a `cd` out of the repository fails. `RESERVED_PNPM_VERBS` was deliberately NOT narrowed — `build`/`test`/`start` are pnpm script shortcuts and listing them costs a real check on `pnpm -C apps/web build`, but that is a judgement about pnpm's command surface rather than a misread; what changed is that the skip is reported instead of dropped.

None of the anti-vacuity checks fire; the first line satisfies them. Latent: the committed bullet is one 70,133-character line. **Closed 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 774](../architecture/decisions.md)). Held, and the silent path is not the one filed. A WHOLESALE reflow is loud (the first line then ends before `The pairs are:` and the anchor check fires), and a single wrap mid-list is loud but misdirecting — measured at the `gym_prs` entry it hid **86 of 99** pairs and reported them as pairs the SYNCER table had added, which nobody had. The silent case is a new pair appended on a continuation line: invisible, **zero errors reported**, which is § 604's exact defect. The committed bullet measures **71,208** characters, not 70,133. Markdown's lazy continuation is applied now (`scripts/markdown_lines.mjs`, shared with the iOS-column guard), and the blank line separating the bullet from the watch-port paragraph is asserted rather than assumed.
  - [x] **`check_parity_ios_column.mjs` — rule 2 is per-LINE.** `checkSingleStatement` needs `\biOS\b` and a legend symbol on the same physical line, and markdown soft-wraps paragraphs freely, so a wrapped fourth statement defeats the check described as catching "the failure that happened". Latent: `parity.md` soft-wraps nothing today. Weaker sibling: `splitRow`'s `.split('|').slice(1, -1)` loses the last cell of a GFM row with no trailing pipe, then demands the author add a note that is already there — caught first by `check_parity_matrix.dart`'s 6-cell rejection in the same job. **Closed 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 774](../architecture/decisions.md)). Both halves held, and the sibling is worse than filed. Rule 2 is graded per folded line now; `parity.md` already carries **5 multi-line prose blocks** outside the rule block, so the wrapping habit is in the document rather than hypothetical. And `splitRow` is NOT caught first by `check_parity_matrix.dart`: that guard drops its own end-empties conditionally, so a row with no trailing pipe parses to seven cells and PASSES its 6-cell check, and it runs after this guard in the job besides. Reproduced: the lost cell is the Notes column, so rule 4 read the Apple Watch cell as the notes and demanded an `**iOS ✗:**` marker that was already there.
  - [x] **`check_pnpm_overrides.mjs` — a parent-scoped pin is enforced against every copy in the tree.** `pinTarget` extracts `{target, parent}` correctly and `checkResolutions` then uses `parent` only for the existence check; the version scan is `resolutions.filter(r => r.name === target)`. A scoped `@sveltejs/kit>cookie` therefore accuses a `cookie` copy under a different parent that the override never reached. Cannot fire today only because `package.json` declares a bare `cookie` pin beside the scoped one — narrowing to the scoped pin alone, a reasonable change, turns it on. **Closed 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 774](../architecture/decisions.md)). Held exactly as filed: reproduced against a tree where `express` carries its own nested `cookie@0.6.0`, which `@sveltejs/kit>cookie` accused. A scoped pin's subject now comes from the dependency graph, read per lockfile because the two record it differently — npm's key IS the install path (nested copy names its parent; a parent with no nested copy resolves the hoisted one), while pnpm's `packages:` is flat, so the edge comes from `snapshots:` (**146** parents in the committed lockfile, carrying both `kit>cookie` and `kit>devalue`). A scope over a parent that resolves nothing, and an empty snapshots graph under a parent that does resolve, are now errors rather than a quiet pass.
  - [x] **`apps/web/scripts/check_constraint_unions.mjs` — strips `--` before knowing whether it is inside a literal.** `.replace(/--[^\n]*/g, '')` runs over a migration with no string awareness, so a `--` inside a literal can delete the `check (col in (…))` clause it exists to read. Re-parsed through `apps/backend/scripts/sql_lex.mjs`: **the same 68 `table.column` → value sets**, and its realistic failure is a loud `no CHECK constraint found` rather than a false pass. The fix is three lines — import `splitSqlStatements` and parse `splitSqlStatements(sql).join(';')` — but it sits in another package root with no test sibling and no CI lane that would run one. **Closed 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 774](../architecture/decisions.md)). Held, and there is a SECOND direction the filing missed: the eaten run can also be the `alter table <t>` header that says which table the next line's clause belongs to, which files that clause under the PREVIOUS table — so a registered pair gets certified against another table's constraint, silently, rather than merely failing loud. Both reproduced. Parsed through `sql_lex.mjs` now; the re-measurement confirms **the same 68 `table.column` value sets** across **436** migrations, none of which the lexer refuses. The missing test sibling exists and runs in `parity-types` after the drift check, with its own `::error::` per § 764.

## Surfaced by the #789 partial-box reconciliation (2026-08-26)

- [x] **`parity-matrix` bundles four unrelated guards under one job name and is not held to per-step diagnosis.** Closed 2026-08-27 ([decisions § 764](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The count of four was right; "the other three steps already have one" was wrong, and the two-line fix would have closed one instance of a class with three members.** Two steps carried no `::error::`, not one — the pair registry, and the Dart matrix check, which was unnamed and therefore outside the rule entirely, so deleting a step's name had always been the shortest way to satisfy the guard. Rather than register the job, membership is now DERIVED: a job running two or more of this repo's guards is bundled by that fact. That is five jobs, and the registry named two — the three it never named are `parity-matrix`, **`workflow-lint` (five guards, zero diagnosed)** and **env-isolation's `unit` (two, zero)**, with `pgtap-rls` diagnosing two of three. Seven steps fixed. Kept bundled rather than split into four jobs: each split needs its own line in the `CI gate`'s `needs:` list, and an omitted line turns an unlabelled failure into an invisible one — so that list is now enforced too. [Decisions § 756](../architecture/decisions.md) put the doc-checkbox guard there because it is the one job a docs-only diff does not skip — but the job now runs the TS↔Dart pair registry, the parity.md iOS column, the doc checkboxes and the Dart matrix check, which is exactly the "the job name cannot say which one broke" condition `scripts/check_ci_diagnostics.mjs` exists for. It lists only `parity-types` in `DIAGNOSING_JOBS`. Adding `parity-matrix` there is a two-line change to that guard, but it fails until the job's first step (**CLAUDE.md vs shared-library-syncer pair registry**) gains an `::error::` of its own — the other three steps already have one. Do both together, or the guard's own suite goes red.

## Surfaced by the #789 e2e base-URL sweep (2026-08-26)

- [x] **~30 `tests-e2e` sites hard-code `http://localhost:7777` while `playwright.config.ts` advertises `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL`.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 743](../architecture/decisions.md)). The count was right — **34 occurrences across 20 files** — but "any run on a second port fails" was true of **20 of them**, and the other 14 were a different defect. Genuinely broken: 14 `request.get` / `request.post` calls that had no reason to name an origin at all (Playwright resolves a relative path against the lane `baseURL`), five `context.grantPermissions({ origin })` grants, which take an *exact* origin and on any other port land on a foreign one — `navigator.clipboard.writeText` then rejects and the spec takes the catch branch it exists to prove is not taken — and one bare Node `fetch`. Inert: four `?? 'http://localhost:7777'` fallbacks after a value the config always sets (unreachable), two Edge Function `redirect_uri` payloads whose asserted branch returns before the gate that reads them, and the config's own two. Five more re-read `process.env.PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL`, which is right in the sharded lane and wrong in the other three, since livehub / exporthub / sso set no override. **The override did not work end to end either**: the config pinned `webServer` to `pnpm run dev`, which is `vite dev --port 7777`, so `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL` moved the tests without moving the server they ran against. All of it now resolves through `tests-e2e/fixtures/base-url.ts`, guarded by `fixtures/base-url.test.ts` — 4 of its 6 tests fail against `main` and name all 32 offending sites.
- [x] **`playwright.sso.config.ts` and `playwright.exporthub.config.ts` both bound dev port `:7779`.** Found by the same sweep, closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 743](../architecture/decisions.md)). CI runs them as separate jobs so they never collided there; locally `reuseExistingServer` makes whichever lane starts second silently adopt the first lane's dev server, and an export-hub run that inherits the sso server has no `PUBLIC_EXPORT_HUB_URL` — so the one spec the lane exists for takes the Edge Function fallback it was written to bypass. SSO moved to `:7780`, with `config.toml`'s `additional_redirect_urls` following it. **The local SSO lane needs a `supabase stop && supabase start` to pick that up**; CI starts fresh.
- [x] **The allowlist was unexercised, and the CI value was unread for a stronger reason than filed.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 750](../architecture/decisions.md)). The count was right — **three** spec bodies carry a `redirect_uri` — but "the Edge Function answers before it compares against the allowlist" is true of only **two** of them. `invalid_code` and `invalid_scope` are answered in the top-level shape validation, upstream of `handleConnect` entirely; the third **does** reach the allowlist block and short-circuits *inside* it, on the empty-parse 503, one line above the comparison. And the CI literal was never in the spec's path at all: `STRAVA_ALLOWED_REDIRECTS` is written into the **`edge-functions`** job's `.env.local`, consumed by a separately-booted `supabase functions serve`, while `strava-import-guards.spec.ts` runs in **`e2e-web`** against the auto-started edge runtime, which sets no such var. So the foreign-origin guard passes because the allowlist is EMPTY, not because a foreign origin was rejected — and no `strava-import` test posts to the host that has the value. Measured across all four suites: no Deno test imported `strava-import/index.ts` (its `index.test.ts` is pure-helper, importing only `_shared/strava.ts`), no pgtap or web unit test touches it, and `handler_envelope.test.ts` names `strava-import` only in a comment about the gateway. **Neither the accept nor the not-in-the-list branch had any coverage anywhere.** Both now do, pure, in `_shared/redirect_allowlist.test.ts` (19 tests) — the wire layer cannot host the accept path, because accepting means proceeding to Strava's `/oauth/token`. Four source guards pin that the handler still calls the gate, still before the exchange, still fails closed when unset, and that none of the **four** functions carrying a `*_ALLOWED_REDIRECTS` var re-spells the parse. The workflow no longer names a port: it reads the line out of `.env.development`, which two further guards pin to `base-url.ts`'s `DEFAULT_E2E_PORT` and to the path the web client actually sends. A found-and-fixed bystander: `api_database.md` said an empty allowlist **disabled** the check "(single-tenant / dev)" — the exact fail-open the code refuses.
- [x] **`tests-e2e` is outside `svelte-check`'s `include`, so nothing typechecks the Playwright tree.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 749](../architecture/decisions.md)). The premise held, and survives its obvious rebuttal: `.svelte-kit/tsconfig.json` names `src/`, `test/` and `tests/` only, Playwright's esbuild transpile never checks, **and the one lane that already reads the tree is not a typecheck either** — `parity-types` runs `tsx --test 'tests-e2e/fixtures/**/*.test.ts'`, and `tsx` strips types the same way. So the tree was outside every type check in the repo, not just svelte-check's. **Six was measured before round 16 landed; the real figure is ten.** All six filed are present and real; four more were not in the entry — a stale `@ts-expect-error` in `fixtures/auth.ts`, and three in `scripts/start-mock-oidc.mjs`, which a hand-run over `*.ts` could not have seen. **Two were more than cosmetic.** `extended-nutrients.spec.ts` passed an assertion message as `toHaveCount`'s *options* argument, off which Playwright reads only `timeout` / `signal` — so that explanation has never once printed on a failure; and `account-deletion.spec.ts` kept `plantedRunId` nullable only to serve a trailing `= null` whose comment claims the `afterAll` reads it, which it does not (the assignment was dead, and the nullability it forced is what broke the two call sites). The remaining eight are type-level: the two coaching specs assign a `BrowserContext` into an outer `let` from inside a `test.step` closure, so at the `finally` it is still `null` and `if (ctx)` narrows to `never` — both contexts are now created up front and closed unconditionally, deleting two `!` with them. Closed with `apps/web/tsconfig.tests-e2e.json` (a **second `tsc` root**, not a widened `include`: TypeScript does not merge `include` across `extends`, so widening the app's own would mean committing a copy of SvelteKit's generated list that goes stale silently) run as `check:e2e-types` in `parity-types`, whose `code` gate cannot skip a diff that could break it, and guarded by `tests-e2e/fixtures/tsconfig-coverage.test.ts`. Pre-fix 10 errors, post-fix 0.

## Surfaced by the #789 `apps/web` typecheck-coverage sweep (2026-08-26)

- [x] **`apps/web/scripts/*.mjs` is typechecked only by accident.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 752](../architecture/decisions.md)). The mechanism held exactly as filed — `.svelte-kit/tsconfig.json` names `src/`, `test/`, `tests/` and `vite.config.*`, TypeScript does not merge `include` across `extends`, and `tsc -p tsconfig.json --listFilesOnly` names `scripts/env_isolation.mjs` and no other file under `scripts/`, confirming it rides in on `vite.config.ts`'s import. **The tree was one of three, and not the one that mattered.** Also inside no `tsc` program: `apps/web/lambda/` — sixteen files, of which eight are the production AWS handlers behind `/api/coach`, the four `/share/*` surfaces, the OG images, `generate-route` and `osrm-proxy` (only `share-entity/src/index.ts` was covered, and only because something under `src/` imports it) — plus `svelte.config.js` and `static/sw.js`, the web-push service worker shipped verbatim to every browser that registers it. Closed with **two** new roots (`tsconfig.node.json`, `tsconfig.service-worker.json` — they cannot be one file, because a service worker's globals come from the `WebWorker` lib and that cannot share a program with `DOM`), each declaring its `types` explicitly since the automatic type-root scan does not fire under this config chain. **47 errors in the Node root, 23 in the service worker**, and four were more than type-level: a `prerender` option `adapter-static` has never had (dead since the SvelteKit 1 template), a `paths.base` built from `BASE_PATH` with no check of the leading slash SvelteKit requires (a bad value 404s the whole site with nothing naming the cause), a nullable `Response.body` in the two run round-trip scripts' gzip decode, and an unchecked nullable `public_runs.started_at` fed to `new Date(...)`. The single biggest cause was one annotation: `fail()` is a function *declaration* ending in `process.exit`, which TypeScript infers as `void`, so no env guard in any of the four round-trip scripts narrowed anything — `@returns {never}` closed 24 at once. Guarded by `apps/web/scripts/tsconfig_coverage.test.mjs`, which names no tree: it reads every `tsconfig*.json` at the top of `apps/web`, follows each `extends` chain to whichever config declares `include` (so the generated SvelteKit one is read, not transcribed), and requires every compilable file git reports under `apps/web` to match one.
- [x] **`check_production_env.mjs` carried a full inline copy of § 709's affirmative set, and § 709's own guard could not see it.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 752](../architecture/decisions.md)). Surfaced by the typecheck above (the copy's `flagOn` param was an implicit `any`). Not a divergence today — the four literals matched — but the guard that exists to stop one walked `src/` and `lambda/` for `.ts` and `.svelte`, and this copy was a `.mjs` under `scripts/`, outside both. The release guard now imports the canonical `isTruthyFlagValue` (Node 24 strips types off the `.ts` import, so the plain-`node` release and CI invocations are unchanged) and the guard's walk covers `scripts/` and `.mjs`, verified by restoring the copy and watching it fail by name.
- [x] **The same hole outside `apps/web`.** **CLOSED 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 757](../architecture/decisions.md)). Re-measured before anything was written: **35 tracked `.mjs` plus one `.js`, 386 errors**, not 33 and 369. The filing was right on its own date and the arithmetic reconciles exactly — `check_doc_checkboxes.mjs` and its test (13 errors) landed in the *same commit* § 752 did, from another slice of the same round, and a same-commit edit to `check_pgtap_refusal_assertions.mjs` moved it 35 -> 38; 369 + 13 + 3 + 1 = 386. So it was understated, and only because the tree moved under it. **Two claims were wrong on any date.** "Three package roots" is wrong in both directions: the sentence names *four* trees and only *two* are package roots (the repo root and `apps/backend`; `.github/` and `infra/` carry no `package.json`, which is why nothing had ever pointed a compiler at them). And the set is not `.mjs`: `infra/modules/web-stack/functions/www_redirect.js` is a **CloudFront Function** running at the edge on every viewer request to the site, in no program either — § 752's own shape repeating, where the production artifact is the one the filing did not count. Closed with two roots over a shared strictness base (`tsconfig.scripts.json`, and `tsconfig.cloudfront.json` because `cloudfront-js-2.0` is neither Node nor a browser), plus `scripts/tsconfig_coverage.test.mjs`, which names no tree and derives its one exemption from where `supabase/config.toml` sits. 251 of the 386 were unannotated parameters; the residue was **ten guards that were not guarding**, worst among them `check_migration_online_safety.mjs` passing a bare `ADD CONSTRAINT` riding beside a `NOT VALID` one on `runs` (the blocking-lock downtime it exists to stop), `check_lambda_alias_sync.mjs` letting any one of its three sources fall silently out of the alias union, `check_toolchain_pins.mjs` unable to see a `defmt-print` cache key that carried no version, and three guards that reported a clean tree over an empty input set. 271 unit tests across the 15 suites, every guard exits 0. Original filing below.
  - **The same hole outside `apps/web`: 33 tracked `.mjs` files under the root `scripts/`, `apps/backend/scripts/`, `.github/actions/` and `infra/` are in no `tsc` program, in a repo with no root `tsconfig.json` at all.** Measured 2026-08-26 during § 752: a `tsc` pass at the same strictness reports **369 errors**, concentrated in `check_ios_native_declarations.mjs` (46), `check_pnpm_overrides.mjs` (36), `check_toolchain_pins.mjs` (35) and `apps/backend/scripts/check_pgtap_refusal_assertions.mjs` (35). These are the repo's own CI guards — the files whose verdicts every merge depends on — spread across three package roots, so this needs its own root (or roots) and its own coverage guard rather than a line in `apps/web`'s. Not attempted here deliberately: § 752's guard can state `apps/web`'s coverage completely and would have to lie about the rest.

## Surfaced by the #789 repo-root typecheck-coverage sweep (2026-08-27)

- [x] **Nothing typechecks the Edge Functions.** **CLOSED 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 762](../architecture/decisions.md)). The premise held exactly and the counts reconcile, but **"47 of them production handlers" is the wrong label for that number**: 85 tracked `.ts` splits 47 non-test / 38 test, and the 47 is the count of non-test MODULES — eleven of them shared library files under `_shared/` with no HTTP surface at all, the rest the `lib.ts` / `handler.ts` / `paging.ts` halves the tests exercise. The number of deployed functions is **16**, one `index.ts` per directory (`config.toml` names seven of them, those needing `verify_jwt = false`). Sixteen internet-facing entrypoints is the alarming figure and it is smaller than the one filed; 85 is the figure the lane has to cover. **First-run count: 81** — 39 `TS2339`, 32 `TS2345`, 4 `TS2353`, 2 `TS2365`, 2 `TS2322`, 2 `TS18047` — and the § 757 shape the filing predicted did hold, with one omission producing 71 of them: no `createClient` call carried the `Database` generic, so `.from()` resolved to `never`, and the `_shared` helpers typed their client parameters as `ReturnType<typeof createClient>`, which is not what a real `createClient(url, key)` returns. **81 was a floor, not a total**: a `never` collapse conceals everything downstream, so binding the generic revealed 20 more, six of them in five files that had reported none. **The one file with zero errors was the one typed against `any`** — `delete-account/index.ts`, thirteen client parameters annotated `SupabaseClient` (i.e. `SupabaseClient<any>`), the best-typed-looking and least-checked file in the tier; typing it properly found its audit record was an `interface`, which gets no implicit index signature and so was not assignable to the jsonb column it is written into. Seven defects were more than type-level (the strava-import lookback validating one value and running on another; a nullable view owner used to build a Storage path and then passed to a NOT NULL RPC after the track was already decompressed; a jsonb column spread into an object literal; `gzipBytes` promising a `SharedArrayBuffer`-backed width its own first line would throw on; a stale declared return type; a cast erasing a nullable column; an untyped column-name table). No second generated artifact: `_shared/database.ts` re-exports `apps/web/src/lib/database.types.ts` type-only, so nothing crosses the boundary at runtime. Guarded by `scripts/edge_functions_typecheck_coverage.test.mjs`, which lifts the CI step's own `files=$(…)` expression out of `ci.yml` and runs it rather than restating the file set. **81 before, 0 after; 520 Deno tests pass, 0 fail, 23 ignored.** Original filing below. **Nothing typechecks the Edge Functions: 85 tracked `.ts` files under `apps/backend/supabase/functions/`, 47 of them production handlers, are in no type-checking program of any kind.** Found while scoping [decisions § 757](../architecture/decisions.md)'s coverage guard, which had to decide what to do about that tree and chose to exempt it *and say so* rather than pretend a `tsc` root could read it. CI runs `deno test --no-check` twice and `deno cache --no-check`, and there is no `deno check` anywhere in `.github/workflows/`. This is the same hole § 749 / § 752 / § 757 closed three times over, in the one tier that answers requests from the public internet — Stripe and RevenueCat webhooks, the account-delete and data-export handlers, the auth-email hook. It cannot be a line in a `tsc` root: the modules import over `npm:` / `jsr:` / `https:` specifiers tsc's resolver cannot follow. The fix is a `deno check` step over `supabase/functions/` in the `edge-functions` job, plus whatever it turns up — expect the § 757 shape, most of the count being one diagnostic and the residue being real. Its own slice: a different toolchain, a different failure surface, and a first-run error count nobody has measured.
- [x] **The Stripe SDK is `any` in every Edge Function that uses it, so the new typecheck stops at the payment boundary.** **CLOSED 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 765](../architecture/decisions.md)). The probe reproduced exactly and the diagnosis under it did not: **the declarations were never missing.** esm.sh serves them, Deno follows the `x-typescript-types` header, and `deno info --json` shows all ~450 of stripe's `.d.ts` files resolved with no error on any. What breaks is ambient-declaration MATCHING — esm.sh rewrites `declare module 'stripe'` to name the declaration file's own URL, and TypeScript matches an ambient module declaration against the LITERAL specifier text, so the runtime specifier matches nothing and the file it resolves to has no top-level exports. Importing that URL literally binds the whole SDK. So the fix is a `@ts-types` directive plus a three-line declaration file (`_shared/stripe.ts` + `_shared/stripe_types.d.ts`), **not** a dependency change: none of the three candidates the entry named was needed, and two would have been worse (an `npm:` specifier re-arms [§ 699](../architecture/decisions.md)'s boot-time registry dependency; a hand-written declaration is a maintained approximation of a 450-file SDK). No runtime byte changes and **no live test-mode key was involved** — the change is entirely type-level. Three further corrections. **Stripe was the only one of the tier's seven remote dependencies with this shape** (supabase-js at both targets, zipjs, sentry, cheerio and std/assert all resolve to real types, measured). **"All four payment functions" undercounts the boundary by one** — `stripe-events-webhook`, the inbound half that writes order status and seats attendees, hand-parses Stripe payloads as `Record<string, unknown>` and appears nowhere in the entry. And **binding the declarations does not finish the boundary**: what reaches a Stripe call is a function return, not a fresh literal, so no excess-property check runs and a `defualt_currency` in the params helpers compiled silently — closed with a per-call-site key assertion. **0 errors before, 2 with the declarations bound, 0 after** — both genuine, both the interface-has-no-implicit-index-signature shape § 762 found on `ThirdPartyOutcomes`. 524 Deno tests pass, 0 fail, 23 ignored. Original filing below.
  - **The Stripe SDK is `any` in every Edge Function that uses it, so the new typecheck stops at the payment boundary.** Surfaced by [decisions § 762](../architecture/decisions.md). All four payment functions (`donations-checkout`, `events-cancel`, `events-checkout`, `events-connect-onboard`) import `https://esm.sh/stripe@17.5.0?target=deno`, and the declarations that specifier serves resolve to `any`: measured with `declare const a: Stripe.Account; const bad: number = a.id;`, which type-checks. So `stripe.accounts.create(...)`, `stripe.checkout.sessions.create(...)` and every field read off their results are unchecked in a tier that now checks everything else — and worse than unchecked, since an `any` flowing into a narrowed local silently *widens* it back (`accountId` in `events-connect-onboard` had to be annotated round it). Not fixed here because the fix is to change how the dependency is resolved — a different esm.sh variant, an `npm:` specifier, or a hand-written declaration for the handful of shapes we read — not to annotate a call site, and the payment functions are the ones gated on the Stripe sign-off ([decisions § 139](../architecture/decisions.md)) so the change wants measuring against a live test-mode key.
- [x] **`stripe-events-webhook` parses Stripe's event payloads as `Record<string, unknown>`, and typing it costs a 10x eszip.** **CLOSED 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 785](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The cost was measured for the wrong import, by four orders of magnitude.** [§ 699](../architecture/decisions.md)'s 228 KB -> 2.4 MB is a VALUE import of a runtime module; a reader needs only the declarations, and a type-only import is erased before anything is bundled. `edge-runtime bundle` on this entrypoint, three ways: **761,148 bytes with no Stripe import, 761,378 with `import type` (+230 — the source text of the lines themselves), 3,373,077 with a value import (+2.61 MB, 4.43x)**. The shipped eszip carries zero `esm.sh/stripe` modules and zero of the `deno.land/std@0.177.1` polyfill tree, against the value variant's 6 and 131. § 699's second argument — that a type-only import re-arms the offline worker boot — is true in general (`deno info` finds a bare `node:http` in the SDK's graph) and **void for this function**, which already imports supabase-js at the default target and so already carries `node:buffer` + `node:process`: it is one of the seventeen § 699 left un-converted and could not boot offline before this change either. The shape is § 765's, not a third pattern: a narrow source interface per object, each pinned to `Stripe.Checkout.Session` / `Stripe.Charge` / `Stripe.Account` by an `AssertAssignable` instantiation, plus the six dispatched event types `as const satisfies Record<string, Stripe.Event.Type>` — all four proved live by injection (`TS2344` twice, `TS2820` once). **Four defects fell out.** The headline: **`checkout.session.completed` is not a payment.** For a delayed-notification method the Session completes with `payment_status: 'unpaid'` and the outcome lands days later, and the webhook marked the order paid + seated the attendee anyway while handling neither async event — a payment that never arrived left a paid order holding a seat, permanently, with no corrective event. Reachable by configuration alone: neither checkout params helper declares `payment_method_types`, so the live set is a dashboard checkbox. Both confirm arms now gate on `isPaymentSettled` before any write and both async outcomes are dispatched (**the Stripe endpoint must subscribe to them** — a deploy-checklist item, now in the docs). Then: an EXPANDED `payment_intent` read as null, which answers a refund `200 missing_payment_intent` and leaves the order holding its seat with no retry; an empty-string metadata value reached `.eq('id', '')` and 22P02'd forever; and `handleExpired` dropped its read error where both donation twins had been hardened against exactly that. **596 Deno tests pass, 0 fail, 23 ignored** (581 baseline), `deno check` 0 both sides, and every new assertion was proved to fail against the code it fixes. **Filed rather than closed:** an async refund that FAILS arrives as `charge.refund.updated` with `status: 'failed'`, which this webhook does not handle — the order stays `refunded` with its seat released and possibly re-promoted. That needs a seat-reinstatement decision, not a handler. Original filing below.
  - Original filing: Surfaced by [decisions § 765](../architecture/decisions.md), which bound the SDK's declarations for the four OUTBOUND payment functions. The fifth Stripe-touching function is the inbound one — it verifies the HMAC by hand and reads `checkout.session.completed` / `charge.refunded` bodies field by field with no SDK types, and it is the sole writer of `event_orders.status`, the thing that seats an attendee and the thing that releases capacity. Typing it against `Stripe.Event` means putting `?target=deno` in its graph, which [§ 699](../architecture/decisions.md) measured at 228 KB -> 2.4 MB per eszip; this is the function Stripe itself calls with retries on timeout, so that is a real cold-boot cost for a type-only benefit. Worth revisiting if the polyfill tree ever stops being the price of `?target=deno`, or with a small hand-written declaration for the four event shapes it actually reads — which is a defensible shape HERE, where the surface is four objects, and was not for the whole SDK.
- [x] **A destination-charge refund claws back the application fee without reversing the transfer.** **CLOSED 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 769](../architecture/decisions.md)). The fault held and the charge type held — measured from the code that CREATES the charge, not the refund: `buildCheckoutSessionParams` and `buildDonationSessionParams` both set `transfer_data.destination` + `application_fee_amount`, so a destination charge it is and `reverse_transfer` is the right instrument. **The mechanism was backwards, and the arithmetic forty times bigger than filed.** `refund_application_fee: true` does not claw our fee back: Stripe's words are *"To push the application fee funds back to the connected account, set the `refund_application_fee` parameter to `true`."* Sent alone it paid the fee TO the host on top of the ticket they already kept, so a cancelled $50 class at 250 bps cost the platform **$50.00** and paid the host **$50.00** — not "the class fee", the whole ticket plus our margin. Stripe does not treat the half-and-half state as a configuration: *"If you refund the application fee for a destination charge, you must also reverse the transfer."* Both flags now go, via a `buildRefundParams` helper so the call site can carry the `AssertNoUnknownParamKeys` guard every other Stripe call already had — this was the only money-MOVING call without it, and a typo'd `reverse_transfers` would restore the bug invisibly. **Not a reason to defer the code:** the sign-off gates prod, not code ([decisions § 139](../architecture/decisions.md)), the function still 503s fail-closed with `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` unset, and what the sign-off now gets is a five-item pre-deploy checklist in `club_events.md § Refunds` rather than an unwritten path. Three more money flows fell out of the same read and are filed below.
- [x] **A partially-refunded registration could be neither attended nor cancelled — the whole chain was wired and dead at the last link.** **CLOSED 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 769](../architecture/decisions.md)). Found while auditing `events-cancel` for the entry above. `20270522_001` widened the buyer UPDATE policy to admit `partially_refunded`, `events-cancel` widened its order query to match, and both carry comments saying such a registration must stay cancelable — while `cancelAction` returned `'noop'` for it and a test *pinned* that, listing it beside `refunded`/`canceled`/`failed`. The buyer got `{ok:true, action:'noop'}` and the web toast said **"Reservation released"**: a success message over no refund and no freed seat. It now decides exactly as `paid` does, and the `refund_initiated_at` stamp CASes on the status read instead of a hardcoded `'paid'` (which matched zero rows and reported no error).
- [x] **A partial refund on a donation erased the whole donation from the charity's public thermometer.** **CLOSED 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 769](../architecture/decisions.md)). `fundraiser_totals` sums `amount_cents` filtered on `status = 'paid'` (`20270213_001`) and `donationStatusTransition` flipped `paid -> refunded` on any `charge.refunded` — the event ledger grew a partial branch in `20270521_001`'s companion change and the donation ledger, five lines below in the same file, did not. $5 back on a $500 donation removed **$500**. Partially closed: the status no longer moves on a partial refund (overstating by the $5 that came back beats understating by the $495 that did not, and is the only one of the two also TRUE about the status), the gap is logged for reconciliation, and the completing refund still moves it on. The exact fix needs a schema change — see below.
- [x] **`donations` cannot represent a partial refund at all, so `fundraiser_totals` overstates by whatever came back.** **CLOSED 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 776](../architecture/decisions.md), migration `20270620_001`). The filing held exactly: measured, a `charge.refunded` carrying `refunded: false, amount_refunded: 500` on a $500 donation classifies as `partial`, `donationStatusTransition` returns null, nothing is written, and the thermometer keeps counting $500 with **no later event correcting it** unless the refund completes to full. `donations.refunded_cents` now holds Stripe's **cumulative** `charge.amount_refunded`, so the write is idempotent and order-insensitive, and the CAS is compound (`.eq('status', <read>)` **and** `.lte('refunded_cents', <reported>)`) so two instalments delivered out of order cannot walk the figure back. `fundraiser_totals.raised_cents = sum(amount_cents - refunded_cents)` over `('paid','partially_refunded')` and `fundraiser_feed` shows each donor's net, because a gross figure beside a net total is two public numbers that do not add up. Two CHECKs bound the column and the lock trigger refuses a non-service-role write to it. **Deliberately larger than the filing in one place and smaller in another:** `partially_refunded + partial` is a self-transition for a donation where it stays null for an order (an order has no amount to rewrite), and a fully `refunded` row is EXCLUDED from the sum rather than netted to zero, because a pre-migration row carries `refunded_cents = 0` and netting would add its whole amount back. **Not backfilled** — see the entry below. 12 pgtap assertions added (20/20 pass).
- [ ] **The host is documented as merchant of record in four places and is not one in any of them.** Surfaced by the § 769 sweep. Naming the settlement merchant is `on_behalf_of`, which no call site sends; Stripe: *"If `on_behalf_of` is omitted, the platform is the business of record for the payment."* The `club_events.md` parenthetical "they own refunds, chargebacks, and the tax relationship" is wrong on each — the platform issues the refunds with its own key, and *"For destination charges, with or without `on_behalf_of`, Stripe debits dispute amounts and fees from your platform account."* The two code comments and the engineering doc now state what the code does. **The decision itself is deliberately left open:** sending `on_behalf_of` moves settlement currency, fee structure and the identity on the buyer's card statement, and either vindicates or contradicts `/terms` §6, which tells the buyer the host is merchant of record — an owner + counsel call, now a ticked-or-not item on the pre-deploy checklist in `club_events.md § Refunds`. Note it is **required, not optional**, for a host outside the platform's region, and `buildAccountCreateParams` already accepts a `country`, so such a host can be onboarded today and every checkout for them would 502.
- [x] **`donations-checkout`'s idempotency key is unique per invocation, so it dedupes nothing across retries.** **CLOSED 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 776](../architecture/decisions.md), migration `20270620000002`). The mechanism was exactly as filed — the mint site is unconditional and no read of any existing row precedes it — but the stated exposure was **wrong in the direction that matters**: a double-click is *not* reachable from the web caller, whose `submitDonation` sets an in-flight flag before the await and returns early while it is set. What is reachable is the retry after a failed or lost response, because the `catch` clears that flag deliberately. So a **client-side boolean was the entire defence against a double charge**, per page instance and reset on exactly the path where a retry is most likely; two tabs and any non-web caller had nothing. The filing also called dedupe a product call; measured, it is a *fact about the feature* that the key cannot come from the server — `events-checkout` reconstructs its own from `(buyer, event, instance)` **because the buyer is authenticated**, and a donor may be anonymous, so there is no identity to key on and repeat giving rules out the amount. `idempotency_key` is now a required UUID body field persisted as `donations.client_request_id` under a partial unique index; `resolveDonationIntent` resolves it before the Stripe call so the same donation id rebuilds byte-identical params and Stripe replays the session already open. A key against a different request is a 409 `params_changed`, a spent one a 409 `already_used` (reopening it charges a donor who already paid), a losing concurrent insert a 409 `idempotency_in_progress`. The row is written **before** the Stripe call, mirroring `events-checkout`: writing it afterwards left the only record of the attempt at Stripe, where the next call could not find it.
- [ ] **A refund issued from the Stripe *dashboard* reverses nothing unless the operator ticks it.** Surfaced by the § 769 sweep. No code can defend against this: the webhook processes such a `charge.refunded` identically, and host-cancel refunds are still manual pending `refund_orders_for_instance` (P2). It is a **runbook item for whoever holds the Stripe dashboard**, recorded on the pre-deploy checklist in `club_events.md § Refunds`. When `refund_orders_for_instance` is built it must construct its refunds through the same `buildRefundParams` shape.
- [ ] **A transfer reversal against a host who has already been paid out is unmeasured, and decides whether the § 769 fix can strand a buyer.** Surfaced by the § 769 sweep. Adding `reverse_transfer: true` means the refund now depends on recovering funds from the connected account; Stripe documents connected accounts going negative and being covered per `controller.losses.payments` / `debit_negative_balances`, but whether the reversal is permitted to go negative or the refund is refused outright was **not** verified — it needs operator `sk_test_` keys, not more reading. If it refuses, a buyer owed a refund gets none where previously the platform absorbed it. First item on the `club_events.md § Refunds` pre-deploy checklist.
- [x] **A column-level `revoke select (…)` is a no-op while the role holds table-level SELECT, and two migrations ship one anyway.** **CLOSED 2026-08-28** ([decisions § 781](../architecture/decisions.md)) — every claim in the filing reproduced against the live catalog, including the own-row self-read (a host reads `acct_…` today; after `20270621_001` the same query is `42501`) and the nil exposure on `donations` (0 rows to the donor of their own donation, both before and after). The sweep the filing suggested is **complete at two**, but its `grep` finds three sites and one of them is `20260707_001`'s prose warning; a lexer-based pass over all 439 migrations finds exactly the two real ones and no `revoke update (…)` / `revoke insert (…)` sibling. Two corrections to the filing's mechanism: the withheld set had to be **wider** than the five it names — `refunded_cents` and `client_request_id` arrived after the lockdown was meant to exist and a re-grant is cumulative, and `display_name` is what `fundraiser_feed` nulls on an anonymous row — and `20270408_001` did not *introduce* the table grant, it version-controlled one Supabase's default privileges had already made (measured: a fresh `public` table lands with `anon=arwdDxtm`). Fixed by `20270621_001` (two `revoke` + two `grant … (cols)`, no relation lock), registered column-by-column with its reason in `column_grant_lockdown_registry_test.sql` (34 rows, both new tables pinned by `role_grant_matrix_test.sql` too), and closed against a repeat by a static guard with **no allowlist**: `check_migration_column_revoke_noop.mjs` replays the set and reports a column revoke only while the table-level grant survives to the end, so a repair is a migration rather than an edit to the guard. Original filing below. Surfaced and **measured** by the § 776 sweep, against the live local catalog: on `donations` (`20270213_001`, five columns) and `instructor_payout_accounts` (`20261229_001`, `stripe_connect_account_id`), `has_column_privilege` is **true** for both `anon` and `authenticated` on every supposedly-revoked column, table-level SELECT is granted, and `pg_attribute.attacl` is null for every column of both tables — no column ACL exists at all. Postgres: *"if a role has been granted privileges on a table, then revoking the same privileges from individual columns will have no effect"*, and the grant matrix (`20270408_001`) grants it. `20260707_001` **documents this exact trap in its own header** and prescribes the fix — revoke table-level, then re-grant SELECT per public-safe column — and both later migrations reached for the broken shape anyway. Exposure differs sharply between the two: `donations` has no permissive client SELECT policy, so a client read returns zero rows and the loss is only the stated defence in depth; `instructor_payout_accounts` has one (`user_id = auth.uid()`), so a host's raw `acct_…` id is readable by its owner **today**, against that migration's explicit *"even the own-row SELECT policy should not hand the raw id to the client"*. Not fixed in the § 776 branch because the correct shape lands in two shared registry test files — `role_grant_matrix_test.sql` requires every table stay readable by `authenticated` (satisfied by the re-grant) and `column_grant_lockdown_registry_test.sql` requires a per-column carve-out to declare itself with a reason — both held by other agents that round. Sized at one migration (two `revoke` + two `grant … (cols)`) plus a registry entry per withheld column. **Sweep for the shape when fixing**: `grep -rn "revoke select (" apps/backend/supabase/migrations/` finds all three sites.
- [x] **The `edge-functions` warm-cache step does not walk the declaration graph, so the typecheck fetches ~450 `.d.ts` outside the retry wrapper.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 775](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The filing is wrong: the fetches are already inside the retry.** `--no-check` skips TYPECHECKING, not type RESOLUTION. Measured on a cold `DENO_DIR`: the warm step downloads **776 URLs, 458 of them `.d.ts`** — `stripe@17.5.0/types/index.d.ts` and 285 further stripe URLs among them — and `deno check` immediately afterwards downloads **nothing**; run with no warm step at all, `deno check` downloads **403 URLs, 362 `.d.ts`**, every one a subset of the warm step's 776. So the ~450 figure is about right as a declaration count and wrong about its side of the wrapper, and a retry around the check would guard nothing. (`deno check --deny-import`, the instrument the filing used, is not one: on deno 2.9.3 it exits 0 while downloading. The cache file counts can tell warm from cold; it cannot.) What was asserted nowhere is the property that makes this true — that the warm step and the typecheck derive the **same** file set. Narrow the warm step to `*.test.ts` (which the sibling test step once was) and ~450 declaration fetches move outside the retry, making the filed hazard real. `edge_functions_typecheck_coverage.test.mjs` now lifts and runs **both** `files=$(...)` expressions rather than restating either, and fails when the warm set stops covering the checked one — verified failing against exactly that mutation. `deno install` is the one command left in that job with no retry; covered by the restored cache in the steady state, noted rather than pre-emptively wrapped. Original filing below. **The `edge-functions` warm-cache step does not walk the declaration graph, so the typecheck fetches ~450 `.d.ts` outside the retry wrapper.** Pre-existing since [decisions § 762](../architecture/decisions.md), restated in [§ 765](../architecture/decisions.md) rather than introduced by it. `deno cache --no-check` exists in that job specifically to absorb an esm.sh 5xx behind three retries, but `--no-check` skips type resolution, so stripe's declarations are pulled by `deno check` itself. On a warm cache (the steady state — the cache key is `hashFiles('deno.lock')` and the previous run's `deno check` populated it) nothing is fetched, verified with `deno check --deny-import`. On a cold one a CDN blip fails the lane with a bare fetch error. Dropping `--no-check` from the warm step would run the whole typecheck twice; the honest fix is probably a retry around the check itself.
- [x] **`strava-import` reports `rate_limited` and no client declares it.** **CLOSED 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 766](../architecture/decisions.md)). The premise held; the scope did not. `rate_limited` names **one of four** ways the page walk stops short of the end of the lookback window — the other three (a non-2xx upstream, a body that is not an array, the 20-page / 1000-activity cap) had **no field at all**, so declaring the flag and adding a line of copy would have left three of the four still reporting a truncated import as a finished one. `backfill` now returns `complete`, raised only on the two end-of-window exits. **The activities are not lost by the truncation — they are lost by the report**: there is no advanced cursor anywhere (`after=` is derived from `Date.now()` on every call, `sync_cursor` is written by nothing, `last_sync_at` is read by nothing but the two tiles), so a re-sync re-walks the same window — but neither client offers a way to raise `lookbackDays` above 90, so a runner told "sync complete" does not sync again and ninety days later the rest is unreachable. A found-and-fixed second sentence: `last_sync_at` was stamped after **every** walk, so a throttled sync also left the tile reading "Last synced just now"; it is now inside the `if (complete)`. Both clients grade the response through the new `strava_sync_result` parity pair instead of casting it — only an explicit `complete: true` earns the success copy, the opposite fail-closed direction to `cloudExportShortfall`'s and for a stated reason. A third defect nobody had filed: the phone dropped `failed` entirely, where web has reported it since that toast was written. The result does **not** join `import_failures`' vocabulary — that records per-activity refusals, and a truncated walk never saw the activities it skipped. `strava-import` is the only importer with this shape (parkrun fetches one page and fails loudly; the two ZIP importers walk a local archive and report their caps). Original filing below. Surfaced by [decisions § 762](../architecture/decisions.md), where widening `backfill`'s stale return type showed the flag has always been in the response body. Its own comment says it is there "so the client can show 'Strava is rate-limiting us, try again in 15 minutes' rather than treating partial as success" — but `StravaSyncResult` in `apps/web/src/lib/integrations/strava.ts` declares `imported` / `skipped` / `failed` / `athlete_id` only, `ApiClient.syncStrava` returns an untyped map, and no surface on either platform reads the key. So a rate-limited partial import still presents as a completed sync on both clients. Web-first per [§ 24](../architecture/decisions.md), one field on the result type plus a line of copy in six catalogues; left out of the typecheck slice because it is a client change, not a backend one.
- [x] **Neither client can ask `strava-import` to look back further than 90 days, so a truncation left too long has no in-app recovery.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 768](../architecture/decisions.md)). Measured exactly as filed: two call sites, `syncStrava()` on `/settings/integrations` and `api.syncStrava()` on the Strava tile, **neither passing an argument**. Both now offer the window — a select beside "Sync now" on web, a "Sync older history…" picker in the tile menu on mobile — with the plain sync left at 90 for the reason the filing gives, and the three windows plus the maximum living in the `strava_sync_result` pair beside the fields that grade the answer, since what a client may ask for and what it may believe about the reply are one contract. A web guard pins the maximum to the function's own `400 invalid_lookback_days` bound. The pointer to the ZIP importer was already permanent under the card; what was wrong was its wording, which said the sync pulls 90 days. Original filing below. Surfaced while closing the entry above ([decisions § 766](../architecture/decisions.md)). The Edge Function accepts `lookbackDays` up to 365 and validates it properly; web's `syncStrava(lookbackDays = 90)` has no caller that passes anything, and `ApiClient.syncStrava` the same. That is what puts the 90-day fuse under a false "complete": a runner who now sees the partial banner can recover by syncing again, but one who dismissed the old "sync complete" and comes back in four months cannot reach the missed activities from the app at all. The Strava ZIP importer is the real answer for that case and is not offered anywhere near the sync affordance. The durable fix is a lookback control on the partial banner's retry path (surface the field the function already accepts) plus a pointer to the ZIP importer when the gap is older than the maximum — not raising the default, which would make every routine sync twenty times heavier against Strava's per-user budget.
- [x] **The 20-page cap tells the runner to sync again, and a re-sync cannot get past it.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 768](../architecture/decisions.md)). Every claim held — `sync_cursor` appears in the DDL, two export column lists, the two generated type files and four doc lines, and is written by nothing. A truncated walk now records the window still to walk (`{v, from, after, before}`, epoch seconds) and the next sync resumes it; measured against a simulated 1,250-activity window, the first sync spends 20 fetches and records a cursor, the second starts past the 1,000th activity, finishes in 6 and clears it. **The rule the obvious design gets wrong is the containment test**: `after=` is recomputed from the clock, so a truncated walk's frontier is always newer than the floor the next same-lookback walk asks for, and comparing against it refuses every re-sync there is — hence `from`, the oldest instant the *job* covers, stored separately. The walk direction is measured off the page rather than assumed, because Strava orders the list oldest-first with `after` set and newest-first without, and that decides which end of the walked span is the frontier. No migration: the column has existed since `20260405_001` and is already in both generators and the Art 20 export. **Found while measuring: the walk has seven exits, not five** — `fetch` and `resp.json()` each reject, escaping `backfill` into a 500 `internal_error` that discarded every count the walk had earned; both now truncate like the rest. Original filing below. Also from § 766. The walk always restarts at page 1 with `after=` recomputed, so a runner whose window holds more than 1000 activities re-skips the same 1000 and hits the cap again — the new "sync again to finish" copy is, for exactly that runner, an instruction that can never succeed. It is unreachable in practice (1000 activities inside 90 days is 11 a day) and was left alone rather than papered over: the honest fix is for the cap exit to page from where it stopped, which means a real cursor on `integrations.sync_cursor` — a column that exists, is exported, and is written by nothing. That is a backend design change with its own resume semantics, not a client one, and it should not be smuggled in behind a copy string.
- [x] **`handleConnect`'s `if (!code)` branch is unreachable, and the mobile callback is absent from the committed dev allowlist.** From issue #789 section S; closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 768](../architecture/decisions.md)). Both held. The branch is gone, and so is a **second** dead one the filing did not name — the trailing `unknown_action` after the three action `if`s, dead for the same reason one level up; the dispatch is now a `switch` the compiler proves exhaustive. The one adjacent branch that **is** reachable, `handleConnect`'s `'<missing>'` log arm on an empty `redirect_uri` (the top level rejects an empty `code` but accepts an empty `redirect_uri`), is correct and left as found — `parseRedirectAllowlist` drops blanks, so an empty claim can never match. The allowlist entry was **not** only a dev-file matter: `threkir://strava-callback` was missing from `.env.example` too, which is the file an operator copies to configure production, where a whole-string comparison against a web-only list answers every in-app connect with 400 `invalid_redirect_uri`. Both files now carry it, pinned by a guard reading `kStravaCallbackUri` out of the Dart client.
- [x] **`check_migration_online_safety.mjs` splits SQL on `;` without tracking `$$`-quoted bodies.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 770](../architecture/decisions.md)). **The fragmentation held and was far larger than "a function body"; "it fails in the safe direction" did not hold at all.** Over the 435 committed migrations the split turns **3441 statements into 7366 fragments** — 504 dollar-quoted bodies carry 3540 semicolons between them, so more than half of what the guard graded was never a statement. The unsafe direction is not the split but the comment strip that ran before it: `--` was eaten to end of line before anything knew whether it opened a comment, so a statement whose *literal* contains one loses its own terminator, is glued to the next line, and is vouched for by that line's `not valid` — a bare `ADD CONSTRAINT … CHECK` on `runs` reading as clean, which is the blocking-lock downtime the guard exists to stop and the § 757 defect one layer up. The shape is committed: `20270518_001` line 33 carries `-- the ` inside a `comment on index … is '…'` literal. A third variant needs no comment — `check (state <> 'not valid')` hands over the escape hatch. Closed with `apps/backend/scripts/sql_lex.mjs` (line + **nested** block comments, `''` and E-string escapes, quoted identifiers, `$tag$` bodies, throws on anything unterminated rather than consuming to EOF) rather than a wider regex; literals are blanked for the scan so their contents cannot vote. Measured with the cutoff ignored: **no committed migration changes verdict**, 27 change only the quoted text. The `check_compliance_drift.mjs` sibling closed with it — see the next entry.
- [x] **`check_compliance_drift.mjs`'s new-Edge-Function detector reads a hunk header as a claim about a file.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 770](../architecture/decisions.md)), filed as a sibling of the entry above and understated by being called merely "the same character". Measured over the last 2000 commits: **109 touches of a `functions/*/index.ts`, 6 genuinely new, and the detector fired 28 times — 22 false positives, 0 false negatives**, the most recent being `d67ab6fdf`, the commit before the branch's base. git answers this exactly (`--diff-filter=A`) and is now asked; no heuristic was ever needed. Two § 757 shapes went with it: `git diff … -- ${path}` interpolated into a shell string splits a filename with a space into two pathspecs and reads the diff of neither (now `execFileSync` with an argv array), and two per-file `catch { return '' }` swallows turned a git failure into an empty diff that disabled that file's rules while reporting a pass. **Nothing could see any of it because the file had no exports and no tests** — the decisions are now pure functions over `{changed, added, diffFor}`, `main()` is the only part that talks to git, and the new suite pins the old detector's premise beside the new verdict.

- [x] **`check_toolchain_pins.mjs` matches a `defmt-print` install to a cache key per FILE, not per job.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 770](../architecture/decisions.md)). **Both halves of the filing were wrong.** "The guard still fails, just with an extra misleading line" describes one of the two arrangements; mirror it — job B's key naming job A's version, so it restores A's binary and B's `command -v defmt-print ||` wins — and the guard prints **`[OK] cache key matches defmt-print 1.1.0`** over run 31623789083 repeating, the incident the file's own header records. And it is not "a shape the repo does not have today": `ci.yml` has held two sim jobs (`sim-firmware`, `sim-scenarios`) since the day it was written, agreeing only by hand. "Per-job matching needs step-boundary tracking" is true and already built: `parseSteps` in the sibling `scripts/check_ci_diagnostics.mjs`, tested. The fix is an import plus a line offset. A job that caches the binary and never installs it is now named too; an install present but not a valid pin stays silent so the range error is not buried.

- [x] **An unnamed step running a non-guard check in a bundled job is still exempt from per-step diagnosis.** Closed 2026-08-28 ([decisions § 770](../architecture/decisions.md)) **by measuring it and declining to widen.** Across the **6** bundled jobs there are **33** `run:` steps, **31** already in the rule's subject, and the exempt set is **exactly 2**: `npm ci` in `parity-types` and the `go install` that fetches actionlint in `workflow-lint`. Both are setup, and § 764's motivating case — actionlint's *invocation* — is already a named, diagnosed step, so the filing's premise has no instance left in the repo. **The cheap partial close would newly catch 0 of them.** The other implementation of the same sentence is not equivalent and is the one a reader reaches for, because `isGuardStep` also decides bundling: widening the predicate takes the bundled set from **6 jobs to 15** and the subject from **31 steps to 127**, of which **89** print no `::error::` today (`cp .env.example`, `npm run check --workspace=apps/web`, every "Parse version from tag" in every release workflow). One filing, two readings, 0 against 89. What was actually missing is that the blind spot was invisible — the guard reported 31 steps diagnosing themselves and nothing about the ones it never asked, which is why this entry had to ask someone to re-derive it. It now lists them as `[SKIP]` with job and command and counts them in the summary, and the real-tree test asserts no exempt step invokes anything from this repo — the property the widening was reaching for — without pinning a count.

## Surfaced by the #789 mobile challenge-create build (2026-08-26)

- [x] **`SocialService.createChallenge` is dead code in both twins — defined, never called.** Closed 2026-08-26 ([decisions § 754](../architecture/decisions.md)). The filing was accurate, which made it the rarity of the round: of `SocialService`'s 58 public methods it is the only one with zero references anywhere under `apps/mobile_*/lib`, `apps/mobile_*/test` or `packages/` — `myActiveChallenges`, `recomputeChallengeCompletion` and `fetchCancelledInstancesForEvents` all turn up in a naive sweep and are all called from inside `social_service.dart` itself. Closed by **building** the surface rather than deleting the method: § 24 makes mobile additive, `SocialScreen`'s FAB seam is a general contract that Challenges simply had no create surface to feed it, and § 747 had already shipped `rateLimitCreateChallenge` into all seven ARBs — a sentence no mobile code path could reach, which deleting the method would have stranded. `widgets/challenge_form_sheet.dart` + the hoisted FAB + 20 widget tests; `parity.md`'s row is now ✓ / Partial / ✓.
- [x] **Web's challenge goal field asks for the stored number with no unit named, so a typed `100` on a distance challenge is a 100 metre goal.** Closed 2026-08-27 ([decisions § 758](../architecture/decisions.md)). Fixed the durable way rather than by copying the mobile suffix onto web: mobile's inline `challengeGoalToStored` became the registered `challenge_goal` parity pair (web `social/challenge_goal.ts` ↔ `challenge_goal.dart`, 16 web / 14 Dart tests), and both editors now convert through it, echo the converted figure back in the leaderboard's own formatter where a conversion happened, and clear a typed number when the metric changes. One correction to the entry below: the catalogue count is **seven**, not six — `pt-PT.ts` landed in § 755 the day before. Original entry: Surfaced building the mobile form (2026-08-26, [decisions § 754](../architecture/decisions.md)). `challenges.goal_value` is metres for `distance` and `vert`, seconds for `duration`, a bare count for `activity_count` / `streak_days`; `ChallengeEditor.svelte` renders one `<input type="number">` labelled `challenges.goalOptional` ("Goal (optional)") with no suffix and no hint. The value is valid, the insert succeeds, and the progress bar fills on the entrant's first run — there is no error state to notice. The durable fix is web's half of what mobile now does: a unit suffix derived from the metric and the reader's `preferred_unit`, converted at the boundary, reusing `units.svelte.ts`. Costs three new keys (hours / activities / days suffixes) across the six catalogues plus a Playwright update, which is why it was not bundled into a mobile slice with four other agents in the same round. Mobile is deliberately stricter until then — the two clients agree on what is *stored*, not on what a bare `100` means.
- [x] **Nothing checks that a challenge's stored goal is achievable inside its own window.** Closed 2026-08-27 ([decisions § 758](../architecture/decisions.md)) by `challenges_goal_ck` (migration `20270615_001`, `NOT VALID` + `VALIDATE`), mirrored client-side by `checkChallengeGoal` so the refusal is named inline rather than arriving as a 23514. **Two corrections to the entry below.** `duration` is NOT window-bounded: the aggregate sums `duration_s` over runs whose START falls inside the window, so a run begun a minute before it closes carries its whole duration and a 112-hour Moab finish satisfies a goal longer than the window itself — a constraint refusing it would refuse a legitimate ultra challenge, and both suites plus the pgtap file pin that it is allowed. And the *bigger* hole was the one the entry did not name: `goal_value` accepted **zero**, which `recompute_challenge_completion` (returning early only on NULL, then comparing `value >= goal`) turns into a completion badge, a `completed_at` stamp and a `challenge_complete` notification for every participant on the nightly sweep, while both clients render the board as goal-less — and web's field carried `min="0"`, so it was reachable. Original entry: Same origin. Even with a unit-labelled field on both clients, `goal_value` accepts any positive number: a `streak_days` goal can exceed the days between `starts_at` and `ends_at`, and a `duration` goal the seconds between them. Both are row-local arithmetic over columns the row already carries, so this is a genuine `challenges_goal_ck` rather than client validation — and it belongs in the same change as the web field above, since both clients then owe the matching inline message. `20270302_001` is the precedent for widening a `challenges` CHECK.

## Surfaced by the #789 column-grant sweep (2026-08-27)

- [x] **`public.events` grants SELECT per-column rather than table-wide.** Closed 2026-08-27 ([decisions § 759](../architecture/decisions.md)). **Both observations reproduce and both conclusions drawn from them are wrong.** `has_table_privilege('anon','public.events','SELECT')` really is `false` while `set role anon; select count(*) from public.events` returns 12 — a `count(*)` names no column, and Postgres checks column privileges for the columns a query names — and a direct `select xmin`/`ctid`/`tableoid` as `anon` or `authenticated` really does raise `42501`. **Four tables share the shape and every one of them is a lockdown, not an accident**: `user_profiles` withholds 16 columns (`20260707_001` + `20260810_001`), `events` 3 (`meet_lat`/`meet_lng` + the Stripe Connect `host_user_id`), `clubs` 1 (`invite_token`), `checkpoint_crossings` 5 (the Art 9 weigh-in fields + `recorded_by`) — 25 in all, each with a named `SECURITY DEFINER` replacement read path, and `20270408_001`'s header states the invariant outright. **So there is no migration in this change**: the filed table-wide `grant select` would hand every signed-in caller an organiser's home coordinates, every public club's join token, every runner's date of birth and every racer's medical hold, and would fail `role_grant_matrix_test` and `rls_user_profiles_column_lockdown_test` on the way. **The consequence the filing was built on does not occur**: [§ 753](../architecture/decisions.md)'s operator 1 is a permissive RLS policy whose `USING` references `xmin`, and a policy expression is not column-privilege-checked — measured directly, `anon` reads `events` through it normally. § 753's aside is true only of operator 2's `SECURITY INVOKER` replacement relation, which is precisely why operator 2 keeps its owner bypass. Guard population unchanged at **168 assertions / 5 expected survivors** across 259 files. **The real defect is the drift nobody looked for**, and it has fired in prod: a re-grant is cumulative, so a column added after a lockdown is deny-by-default until an explicit grant lands — `clubs.is_verified` (`20260909_001`) shipped that way and took down every non-service-role read of `clubs` with `42501`, caught a day later by eleven `api_client` failures and repaired by `20260913_001`. The `select('*')` source scans police the call-site half of that class; nothing policed the grant half. `column_grant_lockdown_registry_test.sql` now names all 25 withheld columns with a reason each and pins the withheld set — the direction that detects drift, since pinning the *granted* set does not move when a column arrives ungranted. Four probes, each rolled back, trip the intended assertion by name.
- [x] **The write side carries the same shape with the mirror-image drift, and no registry.** Measured 2026-08-27 alongside § 759, deliberately not bundled. Four tables hold column-level INSERT/UPDATE grants for `authenticated`: `achievements` (UPDATE `is_public` only; the other 8 columns are server-awarded), `challenge_participants` (`team_club_id` only — `completed_at` is server-recomputed), `coach_messages` (`archived_at` + `reaction`, [decisions § 29](../architecture/decisions.md)), `event_attendees` (`attendance` + `order_id` blocked on **both** verbs since `20270520_001`). Its drift runs the other way — a column added to one of these is silently **unwritable**, `42501` on a PostgREST PATCH, with no integration suite to catch it the way `clubs.is_verified` was caught — and **three of the four still hold a table-level INSERT beside a column-scoped UPDATE**, which is the exact asymmetry [§ 584](../architecture/decisions.md) records for `event_attendees` before `20270520_001` closed it (a table grant implies every column, so the UPDATE lockdown was reachable through INSERT). Each of the four has an individual pgtap pin; none has the class-level one. Extending `column_grant_lockdown_registry_test.sql` to writes is the durable fix, but it needs the same per-column provenance pass the SELECT side got — per role and per verb, where the write side has more legitimate variety — so it is a slice, not a line. **CLOSED 2026-08-27** ([decisions § 763](../architecture/decisions.md)) — both counts hold and the headline defect has never fired, while the aside at the end of the sentence was live on `main`. Only **one** of the four column-scopes INSERT (`event_attendees`, `20270520_001`), so "silently unwritable" is unconditionally true only there; on the other three a new column would have been insertable-and-not-updatable, write-once by accident — and no column has been added to any of the four since its lockdown landed, measured across all 434 migrations. `anon` holds **no** column-level write grant at all, so the write side was never the read side's both-roles-identical shape. The § 584 asymmetry is real on three tables and **bites on one**: `achievements` has no permissive INSERT or DELETE policy so both grants are dead (measured — the INSERT raises `42501 new row violates row-level security policy`, the DELETE affects zero rows), `coach_messages`' `WITH CHECK` already confined a client to `role = 'user'` so only a client-chosen `id` and a backdated `created_at` were reachable, but `challenge_participants` was a live forgery path: join and leave are both own-row verbs, so `delete` + re-`insert` with `completed_at = now()` wrote the column the lockdown reserves for `recompute_challenge_completion`, verified under the participant's own JWT. `joined_at` was backdatable the same way. **A fifth defect nobody filed:** swept across every column, table and DML verb, `coach_messages` was the *only* place in the schema where `anon` held a privilege `authenticated` lacks — a table-level UPDATE against `authenticated`'s column-scoped `(archived_at, reaction)`, left by `20260518_001` revoking from `authenticated` only and then version-controlled by `20270408_001` from the drifted prod schema. Inert (the owner policy reads `auth.uid() = user_id`) but that is RLS covering for a grant, which is what § 759 refused on the read side. `20270616_001` column-scopes the two INSERTs to exactly what the clients send, revokes `anon`'s UPDATE, and revokes `achievements`' dead INSERT + DELETE so the class invariant needs no exemption. GRANT/REVOKE take **no lock on the target relation** (measured), so no online-DDL machinery applies. `column_grant_write_lockdown_registry_test.sql` is the class guard — six assertions, seven probes tripping all six by name; it caught two unregistered `coach_messages` INSERT withholdings on its first run, and the first draft of the new `challenge_participants` pin was § 741-vacuous (a non-existent challenge id raised the same `42501` from the `WITH CHECK`) until it was rewritten to perform the exploit's own DELETE first.

## Surfaced by the #789 round 24 Stripe webhook typing (2026-08-28)

- [x] **An asynchronous refund that FAILS is not handled, and the seat is already gone.** **CLOSED 2026-08-29** ([decisions § 789](../architecture/decisions.md)). The mechanism was real and slightly wider than filed — `charge.refunded` fires when a refund is *created*, including one Stripe holds `pending` because our balance does not cover it, so the seat-released-before-the-money-settles window is not confined to delayed-notification methods. `promote_event_waitlist` was read and confirms the seat is genuinely unrecoverable: nothing else re-seats. Two claims did not hold. **The event was wrong**: `charge.refund.updated` is *deprecated* in Stripe's own refund-events table ("Listen to `refund.updated` for updates on all refunds instead"), and the failed-refund section names `refund.failed` outright — the compiler suggested the deprecated one because it is the nearest string to a typo, not because it is the event to handle. All three refund-lifecycle events are now handled (`refund.failed` / `refund.updated` / the deprecated `charge.refund.updated`, for an endpoint still pinned below API version `2024-10-28.acacia`), gated on the *refund's own status* rather than the event type — `refund.updated` also fires when Stripe attaches an acquirer reference number, and acting on the type alone would walk a good refund back. **And `charge.amount_refunded` walking down is a red herring**: a Refund object carries no cumulative total, so nothing on this path reads it. **The proposed repair was also wrong**, measurably: CAS-ing back to `paid` would render "Refund in progress" forever (`refundPending = status === 'paid' && refund_initiated_at != null`, and the stamp is never cleared), present a seatless cancelled registration as a live one to `enforce_paid_order_for_priced_event`, and offer the buyer a button firing another automated refund at the rail Stripe just said cannot deliver. Built instead: a terminal `refund_failed` on **both** ledgers (migration `20270624000001`) — excluded from every seat predicate and from `fundraiser_totals` by construction, reachable only from `refunded`, seats deliberately untouched, and reversible only by the refund actually landing. The donation half of the filing was right; § 785's note that the twin "had already been hardened" was about the *read* hardening of § 769/§ 776, not about refund failure, which neither ledger handled.

- [x] **A `refund_failed` order is invisible to the person it happened to.** **CLOSED 2026-08-31** ([decisions § 825](../architecture/decisions.md)). Three of the filing's four clauses did not survive measurement. **The event page's problem was worse than a missing badge**: every order state renders inside `{:else if regState === 'already_registered'}`, and `regState` is derived from the *attendee* row, which § 789 deliberately does not restore — so the block could not draw at all and the page was offering the buyer a fresh **Register · $X** button while holding money it owed them. The new banner sits outside that block and outside the `!isPast && pricing` gate, because money we still owe does not stop being owed once the class has happened. **The donation ledger is not equally silent — it has no donor-facing surface to be silent on**: `donations` carries no client SELECT policy, so a donor cannot read their own donation row anywhere, in any state, and there is no `my_donations` RPC, route or screen. **And there is no mobile twin to write**: mobile has no paid-registration surface at all (`event_detail_screen.dart` contains neither `price` nor `stripe`; P3 in [club_events.md](../features/club_events.md)), so `parity.md`'s claimed "read-only price handoff" is not in the source. **The email decision is yes**, and its shape answers the mobile question too: a `refund_failed` **notification kind** (migration `20270701000001`) rather than a `jobs.kind`, because the notifications fan-out already turns one row into the inbox entry, the email and both pushes in all seven locales — and the inbox is the only one of those four mobile renders, and the only surface a donor can be reached on at all. No `kindMutePrefKey` entry: a per-kind opt-out of being told we still hold your money is not a control anyone benefits from having. An anonymous donation (`donor_user_id` null) is a recorded limit — the trigger guards on it so a 23502 cannot abort the webhook's own ledger UPDATE, and that donor stays a § 789 worklist item. Pinned by `refund_failed_notification_test.sql` (11/11 against the live local database), two Flutter widget tests that render the row, a Playwright case, and `order_status_copy_guard.test.ts`, which reads both ledgers' CHECK out of the newest migration to restate it and fails if the copy names a status the ledger cannot hold.

- [x] **A failed PARTIAL refund has no ledger representation, only a log line.** **CLOSED 2026-08-31** ([decisions § 823](../architecture/decisions.md)).
